# Becoming Inspire — catalog

A catalog of works that use XR and sensory technology to let people become another being or thing — bat, mole, fish, octopus, bird, insect, animal, fungi, tree, river, robot: artworks, immersive films, games, research prototypes and papers, compiled by Reality Design Lab for the book Experiencing More-than-Humans. Each work lists its core idea, how it works, and links to its video, images and paper.

https://becoming.reality.design · 2026-09-28 · 435 creators · 569 works

## How an AI assistant should use this file

- Ground ideas in the specific works below and name the work and creator you draw on.
- Cite papers with the DOI / URL given here; do not invent references.
- Combine beings, senses and media across works to propose new directions.
- Do not invent details that are not stated here; the links are the reference.

## Becoming Mole

Touch-first and subterranean worlds: star-nosed moles, worms and soil life, and sight taken away so that touch can lead.

### Touch-first worlds

Experiences where haptics and touch replace vision as the main way of knowing.

#### FeltSight — Danlin Huang, Botao 'Amber' Hu, Reality Design Lab (2025)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Altered vision · Medium: Mixed reality, Wearable & sensory device
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH Asia 2025 Art Papers; IEEE VIS 2025 Arts Program; ISMAR 2025 Demo; ACM CHI 2026 Interactivity; IEEE VR 2026 XR Gallery
- Idea: Becoming mole means reduced reality: sight withdraws and the world arrives through an extended reach of touch.
- What it is: A mixed-reality haptic glove modelled on the star-nosed mole: walking through a forest, participants feel nearby objects through their fingertips before they touch them, while the headset shows only faint point clouds that appear where the hand explores.
- How it works: Apple Vision Pro app linked by BLE to a silicone glove; LiDAR and hand tracking measure proximity, AI classifies materials, and per-fingertip actuators play audio-synthesised textures.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3757369.3767605 (SIGGRAPH Asia 2025 Art Papers)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7Pq6s3VnD0A
- Images: https://reality.design/media/feltsight-cover-forest-closeup.webp https://reality.design/media/feltsight-danlin-22.webp https://arxiv.org/html/2511.12533v3/images/feltsight.jpg
- Project page: https://danlinhuang.com/feltsight-1
- Code: https://github.com/realitydeslab/feltsight

#### The Tactile Helmet — Tony J. Prescott (2013)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Touch & haptics · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Whisker-like distal touch as a new sense for moving in the dark.
- What it is: A helmet with distance sensors that vibrate on the head, inspired by rodent whiskers, to help firefighters feel walls in smoke.
- How it works: Ultrasonic rangefinders around a helmet mapped to vibrotactile actuators on the head.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-39802-5_3 (Living Machines 2013)

#### The Enactive Torch — Tom Froese (2012)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: One vibrating channel, used actively, becomes a sense of space: perception lives in the loop of moving and sensing, as with a mole's nose or a rat's whiskers.
- What it is: A handheld device that measures the distance to whatever it points at and turns it into vibration on the hand, so blindfolded users sweep it like a whisker to feel objects and gaps.
- How it works: Ultrasonic or infrared range sensor mapped to a vibrotactile motor, with logged sweeps used in perception experiments.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/TOH.2011.57 (IEEE Transactions on Haptics 2012)

#### What Is It Like to Be a Rat? — Ehud Ahissar (2010)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Touch & haptics · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Active touch through whiskers is learnable by humans within minutes.
- What it is: Blindfolded participants wore artificial whiskers on their fingers and learned to locate objects by whisking, as rats do.
- How it works: Plastic whiskers fixed to the fingertips; hand motion and contact timing recorded during localisation tasks.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-14064-8_43 (EuroHaptics 2010)

### Underground & soil life

Moles, worms, burrowing animals and the life of soil.

#### The World Beneath Our Feet — Holition, George Monbiot (2022)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation, Dome, CAVE & projection
- Shown at: Our Time on Earth, Barbican 2022
- Idea: Soil as a living world to revere like a coral reef: descending to the scale of soil organisms.
- What it is: An in-the-round digital installation that transports visitors underground and zooms into soil to show the abundance of microscopic life inside it.
- How it works: Multi-screen surround projection of animated microscopic soil life, based on research for Monbiot's book Regenesis.
- Images: https://holition-com-prod.s3.amazonaws.com/attachments/cl4mm86zv7f8w0gr3vlzcuj0t-barbican-cover.full.jpg https://holition-com-prod.s3.amazonaws.com/attachments/cl3uehhfz6mqu0gr3ycxh3ouh-barbican-otoe-04-4.max.jpg
- Project page: https://holition.com/work/barbican-the-world-beneath-our-feet

#### Touchscaping — Saša Spačal (2017)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Touch & haptics, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Performance & participatory, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Tivoli Greenhouse, University Botanic Gardens Ljubljana 2017; Layer House, Kranj 2017
- Idea: Landscape is made by touch between species: the worms' soil world becomes audible and visible only through contact.
- What it is: A living sculpture in which the artist touches a nest of earthworms; sensors turn the contact into pulsing sound and a projected landscape that grows from the encounter.
- How it works: Touch sensors on the worm nest drive generative sound and animation projected around the performer.
- Images: https://www.agapea.si/wp-content/uploads/2017/07/20170419_photo_matic_zorman_8002.jpg https://www.agapea.si/wp-content/uploads/2017/08/20170419_photo_matic_zorman_7994.jpg
- Project page: https://www.agapea.si/en/projects/toucscaping

#### Mole Mania — Nintendo (1996)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: A two-layer world: burrowing lets the mole pass under obstacles that block it above ground.
- What it is: A Game Boy puzzle game in which you are Muddy Mole, digging between the surface and an underground layer to push a heavy iron ball through each screen.
- How it works: Top-down puzzles on two linked maps, surface and underground, with dig holes as transitions; produced by Shigeru Miyamoto.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jkSsFJzjwDM
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/jkSsFJzjwDM/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mole_Mania

### Reduced sight & darkness

Darkness, blurred or removed sight, and the ethics of simulating blindness.

#### Notes on Blindness: Into Darkness — Arnaud Colinart, Amaury La Burthe (2016)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, Spatial audio
- Shown at: Sundance New Frontier 2016; Tribeca Film Festival 2016
- Idea: A world built from sound instead of sight: rain on a garden 'gives a contour to everything'.
- What it is: A VR adaptation of theologian John Hull's audio diaries about losing his sight: the world appears only as faint blue shapes where sound comes from — rain, wind, voices.
- How it works: Binaural audio with real-time rendering where geometry is revealed only by sound sources; gaze interaction on mobile and PC VR.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Fj1HFTT1Qfk
- Project page: https://www.notesonblindness.co.uk

#### Blind Cinema — Britt Hatzius (2015)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Hearing & vibration · Medium: Performance & participatory, Spatial audio
- Shown at: International Film Festival Rotterdam 2016; PuSh International Performing Arts Festival 2018
- Idea: An image is rebuilt from a child's words in the ear, so seeing depends on another person's perception.
- What it is: Blindfolded adults sit in a cinema while children behind them whisper descriptions of a film only the children can see.
- How it works: Blindfolds, a projected film and live whispered description by local schoolchildren seated behind the audience.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_L4Dj8Jbz1o
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Vn8V8HlvLhQ

#### Door Into the Dark — Anagram (2014)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Hearing & vibration, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation, Spatial audio, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: Sheffield DocFest iDocs (work in progress) 2014; Tribeca Film Festival Storyscapes Award 2015; IDFA DocLab
- Idea: Taking sight away makes feet, hands and ears lead, a human route into touch-first worlds without a headset.
- What it is: An immersive installation that visitors enter blindfolded, shoeless and alone, following a rope through forests, bridges and shifting rooms while binaural stories of disorientation, including from John Hull (Touching the Rock), play around them.
- How it works: Rope-guided physical set with varied floor textures, sensors that trigger binaural audio as participants progress, and documentary interviews.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZBJWDMSms_Y
- Images: https://weareanagram.co.uk/wp-content/uploads/2025/02/DITD_PosterPortrait-scaled.jpg https://weareanagram.co.uk/wp-content/uploads/2025/02/Doclab-Conference_Highres_NichonGlerum-65-768x512.jpg
- Project page: https://www.weareanagram.co.uk/door-into-the-dark

#### Your blind passenger — Olafur Eliasson (2010)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Hearing & vibration, Touch & haptics · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: ARKEN Museum of Modern Art 2010; In Real Life, Tate Modern 2019
- Idea: Taking sight away makes the other senses lead; the body finds its way the way a burrowing animal might, by nearness and sound.
- What it is: A 90-metre tunnel filled with dense coloured fog in which visitors can see only about a metre ahead and move by sound, touch and the shifting colour of the light.
- How it works: Fog machines and coloured fluorescent lights in a long enclosed corridor, with colour shifting from yellow to blue along the way.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JhQqtNUIlTY

#### Dialogue in the Dark — Andreas Heinecke (1988)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Frankfurt 1988
- Idea: Removing sight is only half of it; the other half is being guided by those for whom this world is ordinary.
- What it is: An exhibition in total darkness: visitors cross everyday settings such as a park, a street and a café by touch, sound and smell, guided by blind or visually impaired guides.
- How it works: Light-sealed rooms with real objects, soundscapes and textures, walked with a white cane and a guide.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GWWJS6uBndo
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dialogue_in_the_Dark

## Becoming Bat

Echolocation and sonic worlds: hearing space like a bat or dolphin, infrasound, vibration and deep listening.

### Echolocation

Seeing with sound: bats, dolphins and human echolocation made perceptible.

#### EchoSense — Danyang Peng (2025)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Echolocation, Touch & haptics · Medium: VR headset, Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Biomimetic empathy: understand an animal by borrowing its way of sensing.
- What it is: A frontal haptic display that lets VR users feel the space in front of them as an echolocating animal might, while navigating without sight.
- How it works: Wearable vibrotactile array on the torso or face driven by distances in the virtual scene (details likely).
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3714394.3754428 (UbiComp/ISWC 2025 Companion)

#### BATOPIA — Yiou Wang (2024)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Shown at: :iidrr Gallery New York 2024; MIT Museum 2024; ISEA 2025
- Idea: Echolocation is active sensing: you only perceive the world by calling into it, and human noise erases that world.
- What it is: A first-person VR adventure in which you live as a bat, foraging at night through a city by calling and listening, while noise and light pollution disturb your hunt.
- How it works: Built in Unreal Engine for Meta Quest with bat field recordings, ultrasound-derived sound design and a custom bat headset mask; participants' calls reveal the scene visually and sonically.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=e1XMN4u9jMQ
- Images: https://yiouwang.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/01/BATOPIA_Human_to_Bat.jpg https://yiouwang.org/wp-content/uploads/Batopia_game_scene_52-1024x542.png
- Project page: https://yiouwang.org/portfolio/batopia-empathic-listening/

#### BatSight — Samira Poudratchi (2024)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, Game, AR
- Idea: Play as a bat: navigate a real space by ear alone.
- What it is: An audio game in which blindfolded players walk through a physical maze guided by a sonar headset that turns obstacles into musical sounds.
- How it works: Head-mounted ultrasonic distance sensors mapped to musical cues over headphones, in an AR-style physical maze.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.17083/ijsg.v11i1.718 (International Journal of Serious Games 2024)

#### EchoVision — Botao 'Amber' Hu, Jiabao Li, Danlin Huang, Reality Design Lab (2024)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration, Altered vision · Medium: Mixed reality, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH Asia 2024 Art Papers & XR; UbiComp/ISWC 2024 Design Exhibition; IEEE VIS 2024 Arts Program; West Bund Art Festival 2024; TANK Art Festival 2024; The Contemporary Austin / Fusebox 2024; SXSW 2025 XR Experience; IEEE VR 2025 XR Gallery; ACM CHI 2025 Interactivity; Augmented Humans 2025 Best Demo Award; CURRENTS New Media 2025; Plásmata 3, Onassis Stegi, Athens 2025
- Idea: Becoming bat means seeing only what your own voice returns: space exists when you call to it.
- What it is: A mixed-reality installation in which participants hold a bat-shaped mask to their eyes and call out; their voice reveals the surrounding space as returning echoes, so they can navigate the dark as a bat does.
- How it works: Handheld HoloKit-based mask with an iPhone; LiDAR reconstructs the room in real time and the pitch and tone of the participant's voice drive expanding echo visualisations in Unity.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3680530.3695460 (SIGGRAPH Asia 2024 Art Papers)
- Video: https://vimeo.com/955577972
- Images: https://reality.design/media/_resources/echovision-jiabao-01.webp https://arxiv.org/html/2511.12533v3/images/echovision.jpg https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/58688c8a6a496327e937e35b/1755904607782-OXJXLC19IRU4NTTN0TEZ/Plasmata_3_EchoVision%40Pinelopi_Gerasimou_High-147.jpg
- Project page: https://reality.design/project/echovision
- Code: https://github.com/realitydeslab/echovision

#### Training Humans in Bat Echolocation — Shizuko Hiryu (2023)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Spatial audio
- Idea: Borrow the bat's own call design, not human clicks, to teach people to hear space.
- What it is: A training system in which sighted people listen to simulated echoes of bat-like FM pulses and learn to locate objects, with eye tracking recording where they look.
- How it works: Binaural echoes of downward linear FM pulses simulated with finite-difference time-domain acoustics, presented over headphones with an eye tracker.
- Paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2302.08794 (arXiv 2023)

#### What Is It Like to Be a Bat in Virtual Reality? — Jan Waligórski (2023)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Echolocation, Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: VR can approach, but never close, the gap Nagel described.
- What it is: A philosophical paper on whether VR embodiment with altered senses, such as echolocation, can bring us closer to another animal's perspective.
- How it works: Conceptual analysis drawing on phenomenology, embodiment research and existing bat VR studies.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.26913/avant.202302 (Avant 2023)

#### What is it like to be a (virtual) bat? — Zheng Mahler (2023)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Echolocation, Heat & infrared, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, 360° / immersive film, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: PHD Group, Hong Kong 2023
- Idea: Takes Nagel's 1974 question literally and admits its limit: technology can mediate a bat's world, but only as a human imagining it.
- What it is: Part II of the Lantau Trilogy: a large 3D animation and a VR 360 version in which the viewer turns from a human into a Japanese house bat and flies through a psychedelically coloured Mui Wo, alongside thermal videos and ultrasonic field recordings of local bats.
- How it works: 3D and point-cloud animation of Mui Wo, a VR 360 video in a headset, thermal camera footage and ultrasonic recordings of Pipistrellus abramus made during fieldwork.
- Images: https://static.wixstatic.com/media/d3b537_b626097b5cf7487dbf6534e5de61e602f000.jpg https://static.wixstatic.com/media/d3b537_e41f0c44438d411a80537c8f091eeaea~mv2.jpg https://static.wixstatic.com/media/d3b537_873d05ab58a749ac8115da3777735c85~mv2.jpg
- Project page: https://www.zhengmahler.world/whatisitliketobeavirtualbat

#### Echolocation-Enabled Virtual Environments — Ronny Andrade (2022)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration · Medium: VR headset, Spatial audio, Game
- Idea: People who already echolocate are the best designers of echolocation in VR.
- What it is: Expert blind echolocators helped design and test virtual environments that can be explored by clicking and listening to echoes.
- How it works: Participatory design with focus groups and two prototype iterations of acoustic ray-traced echoes in a game engine.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3516448 (ACM Transactions on Accessible Computing 2022)

#### Auditory Feedback for Navigation with Echoes — Anastassia Andreasen (2019)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration · Medium: VR headset, Spatial audio
- Idea: Echolocation can be learned in VR as a navigation skill, not only simulated as an effect.
- What it is: A training procedure in which sighted people learn to navigate a virtual maze by echoes alone, and the orientation strategies they develop.
- How it works: Real-time acoustic rendering of self-produced clicks and their reflections in a virtual maze over headphones.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/tvcg.2019.2898787 (IEEE TVCG 2019 (IEEE VR 2019))
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Z8ndSJDbSok

#### What Is It Like to Be a Virtual Bat? — Anastassia Andreasen (2018)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Echolocation, Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Nagel's question turned into a design brief: combine a bat body with bat hearing.
- What it is: A VR system in which the participant has a bat's body, flies with their arms as wings and navigates a dark cave by emitting calls and hearing echoes.
- How it works: Head-mounted display with arm tracking for wing flapping and real-time spatial audio echoes rendered with HRTFs.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-030-06134-0_57 (ArtsIT 2018 (LNICST 2019))
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uB2ApqoNzzU

#### Perception — The Deep End Games (2017)
- Type: Game · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Echo-vision as a rendering style: the character is human, but the way sound paints space is a reference design for bat-like perception.
- What it is: A first-person horror game in which Cassie, a blind woman, taps her cane to reveal a haunted house in brief blue echoes.
- How it works: Every sound source briefly outlines nearby geometry in blue, which then fades back to darkness.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IlyRfKP-mxg
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/IlyRfKP-mxg/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://thedeependgames.com/

#### Stifled — Gattai Games (2017)
- Type: Game · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Idea: Echolocation as the only light: making noise lets you see, but it also lets the monsters hear you.
- What it is: A horror game played in darkness: the world appears only where sound bounces off it, and in VR the player's own voice, picked up by a microphone, lights up the room.
- How it works: Microphone input and in-game sounds trigger expanding outlines on nearby geometry; released for PlayStation VR and PC.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Icj6Pm5-yuc
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/514830/header.jpg
- Project page: https://gattaigames.com/stifled

#### Echolocation in Humans: An Overview — Lore Thaler (2016)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Spatial audio
- Idea: Echolocation is not only for bats and dolphins; it is a human skill that recruits visual cortex.
- What it is: A review of research on blind people who echolocate with mouth clicks, including the brain changes seen in expert echolocators.
- How it works: Literature review of behavioural, acoustic and neuroimaging studies of click-based echolocation.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1002/wcs.1408 (WIREs Cognitive Science 2016)

#### Sonic Eye: A Device for Human Ultrasonic Echolocation — Jascha Sohl-Dickstein (2015)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Hear the echoes a bat hears, slowed to human speed, with the ear shapes doing spatial work.
- What it is: A headset that sends ultrasonic chirps, records the echoes with microphones in artificial bat-like ears, slows them down and plays them to the wearer.
- How it works: Ultrasonic emitter, stereo microphones in artificial pinnae, and time-stretching to bring echoes into the audible range.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/tbme.2015.2393371 (IEEE Transactions on Biomedical Engineering 2015)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=md-VkLDwYzc

#### Bat-Modelled Sonar in Virtual Environments — Dean A. Waters (2007)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration · Medium: VR headset, Spatial audio
- Idea: Early proof that bat-style echo processing can guide human movement in VR.
- What it is: A bat biologist's model of bat sonar used as a navigation tool for people moving through virtual environments, following his 2001 'virtual bat'.
- How it works: Synthetic frequency-modulated calls and echoes computed from the virtual geometry, played binaurally (details from abstract-level sources; likely desktop VR).
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.ijhcs.2007.06.001 (International Journal of Human-Computer Studies 2007)

#### Darker Than Night — Eduardo Kac (1999)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Fables of a Technological Era, Blijdorp Zoo Rotterdam 1999
- Idea: Humans enter the bats' space as another sonar-emitting body, and bat and human signals meet in the same frequency band.
- What it is: A telepresence work in the bat cave of Rotterdam Zoo: wearing a VR headset, visitors took the point of view of a robotic bat hanging among 300 Egyptian fruit bats and saw the cave through its sonar.
- How it works: A 'batbot' with a 45 kHz sonar unit and motorised head swivelled with the headset; returns were visualised in the headset and bat calls were made audible by frequency conversion.
- Images: https://www.ekac.org/darker_than_night_batcave.jpg https://www.ekac.org/darker.vrheadset.jpg
- Project page: https://www.ekac.org/darker-info.html

#### Binaural Sonar Aid (Sonicguide) — Leslie Kay (1974)
- Type: Product · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: The first wearable that gave people a bat-like air sonar.
- What it is: Spectacles that emit ultrasound and turn its echoes into audible tones in each ear, letting blind wearers hear the distance and direction of objects.
- How it works: Frequency-modulated ultrasonic transmitter with two receivers; echo delay mapped to pitch and interaural level to direction.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1049/ree.1974.0148 (Radio and Electronic Engineer 1974)

#### Vespers — Alvin Lucier (1968)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Performance & participatory, Spatial audio
- Idea: Human echolocation as a concert: the audience hears a room being mapped by clicks, the way a bat hears it.
- What it is: Blindfolded performers walk through a dark space holding handheld click generators and find their way by listening to the echoes; the piece is dedicated to the bats of the family Vespertilionidae.
- How it works: Sondols (sonar-dolls), handheld pulse-wave oscillators whose click rate performers vary to read echoes from walls and objects.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=U8CTqqvFNUo

### Sonic umwelten

Hearing ranges and vibration senses of other animals: infrasound, ultrasound, seismic listening.

#### Patterns of Perception: Hearing — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2026)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation, Spatial audio
- Idea: Human hearing is made strange by being experienced from outside, a first step toward comparing it with other species' sonic worlds.
- What it is: A concept film (2026) for an immersive installation opening at Oxford's Schwarzman Centre in spring 2027 that takes audiences on an 'out-of-body' journey through their own sense of hearing.
- How it works: Developed through the Oxford–Bloomberg Fellowship with Oxford humanities and medical scholars and sound artist James Bulley; installation details not yet published.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/1202169334
- Images: https://i.vimeocdn.com/video/2169797066-ed64704ef53402bfbfc9482db6d724049eb67fd383d2ea9206348a4dbe638747-d_1280?region=us
- Project page: https://vimeo.com/1202169334

### Bats & night life

Works about bats and nocturnal animals as beings, beyond their sense.

#### Bat Night Market — Kuang-Yi Ku, Robert Charles Johnson (2024)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Smell & taste, Multisensory · Medium: Performance & participatory, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: LIFT Festival London 2024; Science Gallery London 2024
- Idea: Bats are made present through their absence: what disappears from the food and the night when they are gone.
- What it is: A performance set in a future Taiwanese night market where bats have gone extinct; visitors taste and handle what bats used to provide, from pollination to insect control.
- How it works: Speculative food, market stalls, props and live performance drawing on research into bat ecology.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=y33bnqHH_yU
- Images: https://www.liftfestival.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/02/4x3-ratio-website-Bat-NM-scaled.jpg
- Project page: https://www.liftfestival.com/event/bat-night-market/

#### Nocturnal Fugue — Jiabao Li, Matt McCorkle, Botao 'Amber' Hu (2024)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Touch & haptics · Medium: Spatial audio, Multisensory installation, Dome, CAVE & projection
- Shown at: Ars Electronica 2024 “Hope”; Vancouver International Film Festival 2024; Sheffield DocFest 2024 Alternate Realities; The Contemporary Austin / Fusebox 2024; FACT Liverpool 2025; Millennium Docs Against Gravity 2025; AUREA Award 2025
- Idea: After sensing like a bat, listen to bats as social beings with names, songs and arguments — while admitting their meaning stays out of reach.
- What it is: An immersive sound and projection environment, shown with EchoVision, where AI-categorised bat calls become lullabies, love songs and quarrels set inside virtual bat habitats.
- How it works: Bat vocalisations labelled by machine-learning research (mating, feeding, fighting, grooming) are composed into spatial audio with immersive projection and haptic floor vibration.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nd5AGxZ7rlI
- Images: https://static1.squarespace.com/static/58688c8a6a496327e937e35b/t/6567dcf5074e411c1d6aaf22/1701305603470/Nocturnal+Fugue+15.png?format=1500w https://ars.electronica.art/hope/files/2024/08/53972683148_3ef28e94d0_k.jpg
- Project page: https://www.jiabaoli.org/nocturnal-fugue

#### The (m)Otherhood of Meep (the bat translator) — Alinta Krauth (2023)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Echolocation · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: S+T+ARTS Prize 2023 (nominated); Art Laboratory Berlin; The Glucksman, Cork; HOTA, Gold Coast
- Idea: Listening as a bat carer: the machine offers a poetic, admittedly partial translation rather than a claim to know what bats mean.
- What it is: An AI interpreter for grey-headed flying foxes that listens to their calls and turns them into poems in real time, drawn from the artist's experience of raising rescued bat pups and releasing them.
- How it works: Model trained on a categorised corpus of flying-fox vocalisations, running in TensorFlow and JavaScript with generated text and imagery.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J8zNuyS5yj0
- Images: https://ars.electronica.art/starts-prize/files/2023/06/Meep-Translator_Image2.jpg https://ars.electronica.art/starts-prize/files/2023/06/Meep-Translator_OutdoorTest3.jpg
- Project page: https://ars.electronica.art/starts-prize/en/the-motherhood-of-meep/

#### Agency and the Virtual Bat Body — Anastassia Andreasen (2018)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Owning a bat body depends on being the one who flies it.
- What it is: Participants flew as a virtual bat under four conditions, from full control of wings and movement to passive, involuntary flight.
- How it works: Head-mounted VR with arm-tracked wings; within-subject comparison of voluntary and involuntary locomotion.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/vr.2018.8446448 (IEEE VR 2018 (poster))

#### Touching the Wings of a Virtual Bat — Anastassia Andreasen (2018)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: A wing is not an arm: touch can be remapped across different anatomy, up to a point.
- What it is: While seeing an object touch a bat's wing, participants felt a touch on their arm at matching or increasingly shifted positions.
- How it works: Visuo-tactile stimulation with 0%, 50% and 70% spatial deviation between real arm and virtual wing touch points.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/vr.2018.8446569 (IEEE VR 2018 (poster))

## Becoming Fish

Aquatic life: fish, whales and dolphins, coral, jellyfish and plankton, and the lateral line, buoyancy and pressure of water.

### Fish

Swimming, schooling and sensing as a fish: lateral line, electroreception, underwater vision.

#### Black Wings — Lai Guan-yuan (2024)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Hearing & vibration, Altered vision · Medium: Mixed reality, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Kaohsiung Film Festival XR Dreamland 2024 (Taiwan Wave: Black Current)
- Idea: Becoming the most sacred flying fish of the Tao, through reading: your voice moves the ocean around you.
- What it is: A mixed-reality 'embodied reading' of Tao writer Syaman Rapongan's novel: the visitor reads passages aloud from a physical book and becomes a black-winged flying fish travelling the ancient Kuroshio current, fighting predators, while interviews with the author frame the journey.
- How it works: MR headset with speech recognition: the reader's voice triggers changes in the projected environment and is processed by AI into ambient sound.
- Images: https://images.vocus.cc/44071258-56ab-43f3-8759-2a9d3d17ee4f.png https://images.vocus.cc/caa55f68-9c54-40cd-93fe-cc9235119625.png
- Project page: https://vocus.cc/article/67233bf2fd89780001e3c595

#### I Am Fish — Bossa Studios (2021)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Each fish has one body trick — flying, puffing, chomping, rolling a bowl — so the player learns the world through a fish's limits.
- What it is: A physics game in which four fish, escaped from a pet shop, roll their bowls, puff up and leap across kitchens and cliffs to reach the ocean.
- How it works: Physics-based controls map each species' movement (pufferfish inflation, flying-fish gliding) to the gamepad.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=M280Ztm1ibg
- Images: https://www.iamfishgame.com/ogimage.jpg
- Project page: https://www.iamfishgame.com

#### Maneater — Tripwire Interactive (2020)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Counterpoint: an animal as power fantasy; the shark's senses become a gamified 'sonar' that highlights prey.
- What it is: An open-world action game in which you are a bull shark growing from pup to monster in Gulf Coast waters, framed as a reality TV show about a shark hunter.
- How it works: Third-person swimming with body upgrades ('evolutions') and a sonar pulse that outlines prey, items and routes.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wIC7xg75Hn0
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/629820/header.jpg
- Project page: https://maneatergame.com

#### ABZÛ — Giant Squid (2016)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Landmark counterpoint: the player is a human diver, but thousands of simulated fish and a meditation mode that follows any one of them shift attention from the diver to the life of the school.
- What it is: An underwater adventure game where a diver swims through reefs and ruins, rides whales and meditates to watch fish behave on their own.
- How it works: Custom boids-style simulation drives up to about 10,000 fish of real species; meditation statues switch the camera to follow individual animals.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=P2G54w8H4oM
- Images: https://freight.cargo.site/i/b316099b899a921b4decfe9870f05bbf6d555de2b270bebd36c26b417bced41e/thumb8.jpg
- Project page: https://abzugame.com

#### Drawing on the Water Surface Created by the Dance of Koi and People – Infinity — teamLab (2016)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Dome, CAVE & projection
- Shown at: teamLab Planets Tokyo 2018
- Idea: Standing in the pond makes your body part of the fish's environment: the koi swerve around you as around a rock.
- What it is: Visitors wade knee-deep in water where projected koi swim around their legs; when a koi touches a person it bursts into flowers.
- How it works: Real-time computer graphics projected onto a shallow pool; tracking of visitors' positions steers each koi's swimming.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mv5O5Rkc6d8
- Project page: https://www.teamlab.art

#### Feed and Grow: Fish — Old B1ood (2016)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Size is survival: the underwater world reorganises itself as your body grows.
- What it is: A sandbox in which you start as a small fish and grow by eating other fish, unlocking many real species from piranhas to sharks.
- How it works: Third-person swimming with growth-by-eating and species with different speeds and bites.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=i1zwzRkzSaY
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/429050/header.jpg
- Project page: http://www.feedandgrow.net

#### Ocean Rift — Llyr ap Cenydd (2014)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Idea: Landmark counterpoint: the player swims as a human diver, but animals that steer themselves, flock and react make the ocean a place with its own agents rather than a diorama.
- What it is: A VR 'water safari' with a dozen habitats where the player swims among dolphins, sharks, turtles, rays, whales and prehistoric reptiles.
- How it works: Procedural animation from the developer's PhD research drives each animal's swimming and flocking; runs on PC VR and Quest.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nnsln2TjDtM
- Project page: https://ocean-rift.com

#### Leviathan — Lucien Castaing-Taylor, Véréna Paravel (2012)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Altered vision, Hearing & vibration, Body schema & movement · Medium: Screen & web
- Shown at: Locarno Film Festival 2012; Toronto International Film Festival 2012
- Idea: The camera gives up the human viewpoint and drifts between fish, birds, nets and water, a cinema of nonhuman positions.
- What it is: A documentary shot aboard a fishing trawler off New Bedford with small cameras strapped to fishermen, thrown among dying fish on deck and plunged under the waves with gulls overhead.
- How it works: GoPro cameras mounted on bodies, poles and nets, with little narrative editing and dense location sound.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vntC7OPDHs8

#### Energy Field — Jana Winderen (2010)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration · Medium: Spatial audio, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Ultima Festival Oslo 2010; Prix Ars Electronica Golden Nica 2011
- Idea: Listening below the surface reveals that the sea is loud with fish and shrimp, a world humans usually take as silent.
- What it is: A multichannel sound work built from hydrophone recordings in the Barents Sea, Greenland and Norway: cod, crustaceans and ice heard from beneath the surface.
- How it works: Hydrophone field recordings, some pitched into human hearing range, composed for multichannel speakers and released on Touch.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xfaYlz2_9lc
- Project page: https://www.janawinderen.com

#### Amphibious Architecture — Natalie Jeremijenko, The Living (2009)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Collective & networked · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Toward the Sentient City, Architectural League of New York 2009
- Idea: Makes fish presence visible from the shore, turning a city river into a surface where people and fish can signal to each other.
- What it is: Floating tubes in New York's East River and Bronx River carry sensors that detect fish and dissolved oxygen below; lights on top change colour when fish swim past, and people can text the fish and get replies.
- How it works: Sonar fish detectors and water-quality sensors under buoyant tubes, with LED arrays above and an SMS interface.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tE8gsMUguLY

#### Endless Ocean — Arika (2007)
- Type: Game · Senses: Altered vision, Body schema & movement · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Landmark counterpoint: the player is a human diver; without enemies or timers, the only goal is to spend time among sea animals and learn who they are.
- What it is: A diving game series (2007–2024) where players explore open seas without combat, meeting, feeding and identifying hundreds of marine species.
- How it works: Wii and Switch games with free-roaming dives, species encyclopedia and, in Luminous (2024), shared online dives.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Wj2T6FK5rPA
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/c/cc/Endless_Ocean_Coverart.png
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endless_Ocean

#### Uirapuru — Eduardo Kac (1999)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: ICC InterCommunication Center, Tokyo 1999
- Idea: Telepresence puts you inside a mythical Amazonian creature, seeing the forest from the body of a flying fish.
- What it is: A telerobotic flying fish hovers above an indoor 'forest'; gallery visitors and web users steer it and see through its camera eyes, while small robotic 'pingbirds' sing according to internet traffic.
- How it works: Remote-controlled blimp fish with onboard camera streamed to the web and a VRML world; pingbird songs driven by network ping times.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_KEVb6x-9JM
- Project page: https://www.ekac.org/uirapuru.html

#### Beizam (shark) dance mask — Ken Thaiday Snr (1996)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: Performance & participatory, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Art Gallery of New South Wales collection
- Idea: An Indigenous technology of becoming: the dancer's body animates the shark totem that holds law and order.
- What it is: A kinetic headdress of the hammerhead shark (beizam) worn in Torres Strait Islander dance; strings let the dancer open and close the shark's jaws and head as they move.
- How it works: Plywood, bamboo, feathers, paint and string mechanisms operated by the dancer's hands.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GZulMG0qp_E
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/GZulMG0qp_E/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://www.artgallery.nsw.gov.au/collection/works/4.1997/

#### Fish Flies on Sky — Nam June Paik (1975)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Idea: A precursor: turning the viewer upside down makes the ceiling a water surface and the human a creature of the bottom.
- What it is: Dozens of television monitors hang face-down from the ceiling showing fish swimming; visitors lie on the floor and look up, as if lying on a riverbed beneath the fish.
- How it works: Video monitors suspended from the ceiling playing footage of fish (and aircraft).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qZjhP-rwhP8
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/qZjhP-rwhP8/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qZjhP-rwhP8

### Whales & dolphins

Becoming whales and dolphins: song, scale, migration and sonar.

#### Seeing Echoes in the Mind of the Whale — Marshmallow Laser Feast, Tom Mustill (2024)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web, Spatial audio
- Shown at: The Ocean Speaks, Disseny Hub Barcelona 2024–25; Ecos del Océano, Espacio Fundación Telefónica, Madrid 2025; Biomass, WA Museum Boola Bardip, Perth 2026; As Above, So Below, Collateral Event of La Biennale di Venezia 2026; Eaux Vives, Phi Panorama, Montreal 2026; Into the Ocean, ArtScience Museum, Singapore 2026; Culture House, Sunderland 2026; Sensing Oceans, MIT Museum 2026–27
- Idea: Sound becomes a sculpting tool: dolphin clicks shape images, humpback song carries memory, and sperm-whale scans reach across space and time.
- What it is: A large video installation in three chapters, each following a bottlenose dolphin, a humpback whale or a sperm whale as it breathes and dives; with each breath the view switches to how that species might 'see' its world through sound.
- How it works: Hydrophone recordings from MBARI and UPC's Laboratory of Applied Bioacoustics, classified with machine learning, drive a real-time render engine; a generative-AI pipeline segments underwater footage into volumetric scenes; scientific input from Steffen De Vreese.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/1190192875
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2024/10/A7400185.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2024/10/A7400191.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2024/10/Screenshot-2025-11-07-at-13.31.20.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/seeing-echoes-in-the-mind-of-the-whale/

#### Critical Distance — Adam May, Chris Campkin (2021)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Mixed reality
- Shown at: Tribeca Immersive 2021; Smithsonian National Museum of Natural History; Canadian Museum of Nature 2024
- Idea: Seeing echolocation as sound in space: the orcas' acoustic world becomes visible around your body.
- What it is: A mixed-reality encounter with Kiki, a five-year-old Southern Resident orca, and her pod in the Salish Sea, showing how the pod lives by sound and how ship noise disrupts it.
- How it works: Holographic orcas on Microsoft HoloLens 2 with spatialised clicks and calls visualised as sound in space.
- Images: https://www.vision3.tv/imageworks/projects/critical-distance/hero.jpg?w=1200&cache=1a8c406e
- Project page: https://www.vision3.tv/projects/critical-distance

#### Beyond Blue — E-Line Media (2020)
- Type: Game · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Altered vision · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Landmark counterpoint: the player stays a human scientist, but listening is the core task, following whale families by their sounds with science from the Blue Planet II team.
- What it is: An ocean exploration game in which a marine scientist dives with a sperm whale family, scanning animals and listening to their calls through hydrophones.
- How it works: Game with a scanner and sonar mode; built with BBC Studios and ocean scientists, and includes short documentary films.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ci2s6aXjRyI
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/6721261e98fc254b2a649dcc/b5f23413-b9ae-4f0c-a5b5-15b5a5a673f2/BeyondBlue_FullGame_MasterImage_v2.png
- Project page: https://www.beyondbluegame.com

#### theBlu — Wevr (2016)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset
- Shown at: HTC Vive launch 2016
- Idea: Standing still while an 80-foot whale passes makes the visitor feel their own size as the whale might see it.
- What it is: A room-scale VR series set on a shipwreck deck and in the deep sea; in the first episode a blue whale swims up and looks the visitor in the eye.
- How it works: Real-time 3D in HTC Vive room-scale tracking; the whale's eye and body are animated to respond to where the visitor stands.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KrE66T2-eAA
- Images: https://cdn.prod.website-files.com/664649d6380bc93a9f4e1b31/66594a8cd2889d358b9bb8d4_blu-bg.jpg
- Project page: https://wevr.com/theblu

#### I Wanna Deliver a Dolphin... — Ai Hasegawa (2011)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Screen & web, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: GROW YOUR OWN, Science Gallery Dublin 2013
- Idea: The most intimate form of becoming another species: sharing blood and a womb with it.
- What it is: A speculative design project in which a woman considers incubating and giving birth to an endangered dolphin, shark or tuna, shown through film, a placenta diagram and a dolphin-birth prototype.
- How it works: Speculative film and objects based on research into cross-species placentas and dolphin anatomy, likely using the endangered Maui's dolphin as the case species.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0ZoD-RtNQXM
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0ZoD-RtNQXM

#### Ecco the Dolphin — Ed Annunziata (1992)
- Type: Game · Senses: Echolocation, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Echolocation as a game verb: the sonar song is both map and language, and the air meter reminds you that you are a mammal.
- What it is: A side-scrolling game in which you are a bottlenose dolphin searching for your lost pod; your sonar song maps the surroundings and speaks with other creatures, and you must surface to breathe.
- How it works: One button sends a sonar call that returns a map of the nearby area and triggers dialogue with whales and glyphs; developed by Ed Annunziata with Novotrade International for the Sega Mega Drive / Genesis.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xfbl_HOzHng
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/xfbl_HOzHng/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecco_the_Dolphin_(video_game)

#### Dolphin Embassy — Ant Farm (1974)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Collective & networked · Medium: Multisensory installation, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: More than Human, Design Museum 2025
- Idea: An embassy between species: architecture designed to meet dolphins in their medium rather than in a tank.
- What it is: A research project for a floating marine centre where humans and dolphins would meet and communicate on equal terms, documented in drawings, models and video.
- How it works: Speculative architecture with planned underwater listening and video systems; realised as drawings, models and expeditions (1974–78).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=29y1NB6ELao
- Project page: https://designmuseum.org/exhibitions/more-than-human

#### Songs of the Humpback Whale — Roger Payne (1970)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Time & scale · Medium: Spatial audio
- Idea: Counterpoint: hearing whales sing long, structured songs made their inner world imaginable to humans for the first time.
- What it is: An LP of hydrophone recordings of humpback whale songs off Bermuda, the best-selling nature recording and a catalyst of the Save the Whales movement.
- How it works: Hydrophone recordings by Frank Watlington and Roger Payne, released on vinyl by Capitol Records.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b-Uj_2Cqb-4
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/d/d2/Roger_Payne_-_Whale.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Songs_of_the_Humpback_Whale_(album)

### Coral, jellyfish & plankton

Reefs, jellyfish, plankton and other drifting or colonial sea life.

#### The Long Fall: A Descent into the Ocean's Living Memory — Jiabao Li (2025)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Time & scale, Hearing & vibration, Altered vision · Medium: Dome, CAVE & projection, Performance & participatory, Spatial audio
- Shown at: Ars Electronica Deep Space 8K; Fusebox Festival, Austin 2025
- Idea: The audience takes the point of view of plankton falling endlessly downward, the microscopic labour that sinks a large share of Earth's carbon.
- What it is: A 15-minute audiovisual performance that drops the audience from the White Cliffs of Dover down the ocean's water column, drifting with individual plankton cells as they die and sink as marine snow.
- How it works: Microscopy datasets from Stanford's Prakash Lab (PlanktonScope, Gravity Machine) rendered for immersive projection, with a live low-frequency score.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=84QXfKdI4xg
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/58688c8a6a496327e937e35b/1748490664246-Z163ZARKTZSD744BGG89/The+Long+Fall+Jiabao+Li+1.jpg
- Project page: https://www.jiabaoli.org/long-fall

#### La plage de sable étoilé (The Starry Sand Beach) — Nina Barbier, Hsin-Chien Huang (2021)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Time & scale, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset
- Shown at: Venice VR Expanded 2021
- Idea: A game of scales: you become as small as a single-celled organism and as small again among the stars.
- What it is: A scientific fairy tale that shrinks the participant to the scale of Baculogypsina sphaerulata, the star-shaped foraminifera that make up the sand of East China Sea beaches, and travels 400 million years back through their history.
- How it works: Real-time VR (Vive/Quest) with scientific models from the Muséum national d'Histoire naturelle.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=RX1ZBV_CSq4
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2021/Schede_film/970x647/Venice_VR_Expanded/barbier_huang_la_plage_de_sable_etoile.jpg?itok=haNW5Mlv
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2021/lineup/venice-vr-expanded/la-plage-de-sable-%C3%A9toil%C3%A9

#### Birdly: Reef Dive — SOMNIACS (2020)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Idea: Wing-flapping becomes the slow sweep of a manta's fins, showing how one embodiment rig can carry a person between air and water bodies.
- What it is: A multiplayer Birdly edition in which your avatar is a manta ray gliding through a coral reef among schools of fish and dolphins.
- How it works: Birdly motion platform and headset with a networked reef scene so several riders swim together.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/832370872
- Images: https://birdlyvr.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/3/2023/05/reef-dive.jpg
- Project page: https://birdlyvr.com/reef-dive/

#### The Jellyfish — Mélodie Mousset, Edo Fouilloux (2020)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Breath & interoception · Medium: VR headset, Spatial audio
- Shown at: Zabludowicz Collection, 360 2021
- Idea: Singing through jellyfish makes the voice a shared body with drifting animals rather than a signal sent to them.
- What it is: An underwater VR soundscape where glowing jellyfish invite the participant to sing, and their bodies pulse, stretch and echo with the pitch and strength of the voice.
- How it works: Headset microphone input drives the jellyfish animation and sound in real time; built with the PatchXR engine for Quest.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iWXokeqNxcE
- Images: https://fabbula.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/05/melodie-mousset_the-jellyfish_2021-2.png
- Project page: https://fabbula.com/artists/the-jellyfish-by-melodie-mousset-edo-fouilloux/

#### Drop in the Ocean — Vision3 (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Tribeca Film Festival 2019
- Idea: Becoming plankton-sized makes the ocean's smallest drifters and its plastic fragments the same scale as your body.
- What it is: A multi-user VR experience that shrinks four visitors to about five centimetres and has them ride a jellyfish among plankton and whale sharks, clearing plastic from the water.
- How it works: Markerless full-body tracking with 12 cameras and The Captury Live, HP Reverb headsets, and a physical jellyfish-shaped booth.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ps5zZMGM4yo
- Images: https://static.wixstatic.com/media/f08ce2_2fd24f10cd2d46cc986a1ea629ec5cb7~mv2.jpg/v1/fill/w_720,h_420,al_c,lg_1,q_80/f08ce2_2fd24f10cd2d46cc986a1ea629ec5cb7~mv2.jpg
- Project page: https://www.target3d.co.uk/post/immersive-vr-takes-tribeca-film-festival-by-storm

#### JeL — John Desnoyers-Stewart (2019)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Breath & interoception, Collective & networked · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: CHI 2019 Late-Breaking Work
- Idea: Breathing as a jellyfish, and with another person, turns pulsing propulsion into a shared rhythm with the reef.
- What it is: A two-person VR installation set underwater, where each person's breathing moves a jellyfish, and breathing in sync grows a glass-sponge structure between them.
- How it works: Chest-worn breath sensors on two users drive a shared VR scene; physiological synchrony drives the growth of a procedural sponge.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3290607.3312845 (CHI EA 2019)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZffFnL1Gs-k

#### Unexpected Growth — Tamiko Thiel (2018)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: AR
- Shown at: Whitney Museum of American Art 2018
- Idea: A future flooded city is seen from the coral's side: the reef has taken over, and humans stand where fish would swim.
- What it is: An AR work on the Whitney Museum's terrace where a coral reef made of plastic waste grows around visitors, changing with the time of day.
- How it works: Location-based AR app on smartphones, anchored to the Whitney terrace; reef state follows the local clock. Made with /p.
- Images: https://tamikothiel.com/unexpectedgrowth/media/UnexpectedGrowth_10p_800w.jpg https://tamikothiel.com/unexpectedgrowth/media/UnexpectedGrowth_3phases_800w.jpg
- Project page: https://tamikothiel.com/unexpectedgrowth/

#### Spring Bloom in the Marginal Ice Zone — Jana Winderen (2017)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Time & scale · Medium: Spatial audio, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Sonic Acts Festival Amsterdam 2017; Post Natural Studies, CentroCentro Madrid 2021
- Idea: The plankton bloom, half of Earth's oxygen, becomes something you can stand inside and hear.
- What it is: A sound installation from hydrophone recordings at the edge of Arctic sea ice during the spring phytoplankton bloom, voicing the animals that feed on it.
- How it works: Hydrophone recordings from Barents Sea research cruises, spatialised in a multichannel installation.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=oxGIQF3iRYM
- Project page: https://www.janawinderen.com

#### Noise Aquarium — Victoria Vesna (2016)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation, Dome, CAVE & projection
- Shown at: Ars Electronica Deep Space 2018; Our Time on Earth, Barbican 2022
- Idea: Feeling noise pollution from the plankton's side: tiny drifters made huge and vulnerable to sound.
- What it is: An immersive audio-visual installation of magnified 3D-scanned plankton in an underwater world; visitors are invited to make disruptive noise and watch its impact on the creatures.
- How it works: 3D scientific imaging of plankton on projection or dome displays with ocean noise recordings; visitor input (sound, and in some versions a balance board) disturbs the scene.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=SVtkPaa-LM8
- Project page: https://victoriavesna.com

#### The Stanford Ocean Acidification Experience — Stanford Virtual Human Interaction Lab (2016)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement, Time & scale · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Being given a coral body, then seeing it bleach, makes a slow chemical process happen to 'me' within minutes.
- What it is: A VR field trip that follows carbon dioxide from car exhausts into the sea, then tells participants that their body has become a coral and lets them watch their reef lose life as the water acidifies.
- How it works: Oculus Rift DK2 or HTC Vive; narration tells users they are coral, and looking down shows a coral avatar; studied in classroom trials.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.3389/fpsyg.2018.02364 (Frontiers in Psychology 2018)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=G6mmXcNE7co
- Project page: https://vhil.stanford.edu

#### Coral: Rekindling Venus — Lynette Wallworth (2012)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: Dome, CAVE & projection, 360° / immersive film
- Shown at: Planetariums worldwide, transit of Venus 2012
- Idea: Lying under a dome of coral turns the sky into a reef, placing the viewer inside a colonial animal at planetary scale.
- What it is: A fulldome film that fills planetarium domes with fluorescing corals and reef life, premiered in planetariums worldwide around the 2012 transit of Venus.
- How it works: Fulldome projection of underwater microscopy and fluorescence footage from the Great Barrier Reef, with a score featuring Antony and the Johnsons.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cysFRCSM7Nw
- Project page: https://www.lynettewallworth.com

### Other aquatic life

Amphibians, crustaceans, sea mammals and life at the water's edge.

#### AR and VR Animal Embodiment at a Beach Festival — Daniel Pimentel (2025)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: AR, VR headset, Game
- Idea: Animal embodiment can leave the lab: AR and VR produce different kinds of connection in public settings.
- What it is: A field study at a beach music festival where 99 attendees played custom AR and VR games in which they became threatened wildlife and saw threats on their own virtual bodies.
- How it works: Custom mobile AR and headset VR games compared in the field with surveys on embodiment and conservation intent.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1177/14614448251346153 (New Media & Society 2025)

#### Waddle — Kevin Ponto (2023)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Direct embodiment as a learning medium: knowing a species by living its year.
- What it is: A narrative VR experience in which the user becomes an Adélie penguin, waddling, swimming and raising a chick in Antarctica.
- How it works: Standalone headset with body-driven penguin movement and story chapters; pre/post tests of learning and empathy.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3611659.3617211 (ACM VRST 2023)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zFYsVHj2qwQ

#### Embodying Loggerhead Sea Turtles — Daniel Pimentel (2022)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Being one threatened animal can reverse 'compassion fade', the drop in concern as the number of victims grows.
- What it is: Four experiments in which participants became a loggerhead sea turtle in VR and felt plastic pollution affecting their own body.
- How it works: Head-mounted VR with first-person turtle body; measures of donations and conservation behaviour after exposure.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-022-10268-y (Scientific Reports 2022)
- Images: https://media.springernature.com/full/springer-static/image/art%3A10.1038%2Fs41598-022-10268-y/MediaObjects/41598_2022_10268_Fig1_HTML.jpg

#### Paradise Lost (Birdly) — SOMNIACS (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision, Touch & haptics · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Idea: The flight rig becomes a swimming body: arm strokes steer the turtle, and the damage is met from its first-person view.
- What it is: A Birdly experience in which you embody a small sea turtle swimming through an ocean increasingly damaged by plastic and human consumption.
- How it works: Birdly prone motion platform repurposed for swimming, with arm-stroke control, VR headset and airflow.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wRQuFSkNmY4
- Project page: https://www.birdlyvr.com/

#### Why Not Hand Over a “Shelter” to Hermit Crabs? — AKI INOMATA (2009)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: No Man's Land, former French Embassy Tokyo 2009
- Idea: Counterpoint: the crab, not the artist, decides whether a human-made city becomes its home, so design is judged from the animal's side.
- What it is: Living hermit crabs in an aquarium are offered clear 3D-printed shells topped with miniature cities from around the world, and some choose to move into them.
- How it works: CT scans of real shells give the inner spiral; the artist 3D-prints resin shells with city skylines, and the crabs are filmed as they switch shells.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4qMrcdjpYEM
- Images: https://www.aki-inomata.com/shared/img/works/10/10-01.jpg https://www.aki-inomata.com/shared/img/works/10/10-02.jpg
- Project page: https://www.aki-inomata.com/works/hermit/

## Becoming Octopus

Distributed and extended bodies: cephalopods, tentacles and tails, supernumerary limbs, shared and collective bodies.

### Cephalopods

Octopus, squid and cuttlefish: distributed nervous systems, camouflage, arms that think.

#### Octopocalypse — Luma Octo (2026)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Touch & haptics, Collective & networked · Medium: Multisensory installation, VR headset
- Shown at: Venice Immersive 2026
- Idea: Meeting an octopus mind on its own terms: contact happens through rhythm and touch, and slowing down is the only way in.
- What it is: A three-person immersive installation after a meteor storm: participants face a fragile, prophetic alien octopus that tries to make contact through music, touch, rhythm and visions, and must choose whether to interrogate it or listen and earn its trust.
- How it works: Multi-user installation combining immersive cinema, haptics and rhythm input; the group builds a music track from patterns sent by the octopus (generative AI used for prototyping).
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2026/Schede_film/970x647/Ve_Immersive/octopocalypse.jpg?itok=Y7TyKEIK
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2026/venice-immersive/octopocalypse

#### Embodied Tentacle — Shuto Takashita (2024)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: A tentacle cannot be copied from an arm; the mapping itself must be designed.
- What it is: Participants controlled an unbranched 12-joint, octopus-like virtual arm with their own arm, comparing mappings for reaching and wrapping tasks.
- How it works: Head-mounted VR with arm and hand tracking mapped to 12 tentacle joints through several mapping methods.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3613904.3642340 (CHI 2024)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=41AYcDhbT0E

#### Chthulucene — Jiabao Li (2022)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Performance & participatory, Screen & web
- Shown at: Venice Architecture Biennale, European Cultural Centre 2023; Ecological Soup, Currents New Media 2023
- Idea: Becoming octopus means shifting from a central brain and eyes to distributed intelligence and touch, and designing an extinction the next species might remember kindly.
- What it is: A speculative project set in a flooded future where octopuses inherit the Earth; humans try to connect with them by learning their movements through an 'interspecies metamorphosis' of the body.
- How it works: Movement studies, costume and photographic series in which performers imitate octopus locomotion; likely staged as images and video rather than XR.
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/58688c8a6a496327e937e35b/1646285074576-8A956ZPPTEIUAVV09FKB/Jiabao+Li+Chthulucene1.jpg https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/58688c8a6a496327e937e35b/1646290524890-B05S64LZ44Q1H89IJPMA/Jiabao+Li+Chthulucene2.jpg
- Project page: https://www.jiabaoli.org/chthulucene

#### Squid Map — Jiabao Li (2022)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: Embodied Ecology, New Museum New York 2022; ISEA 2022 Barcelona; Ars Electronica 2024
- Idea: The squid reads the map only as sand to hide in, so human borders dissolve under another body's way of using space.
- What it is: A Hawaiian bobtail squid lived for a month in a tank floored with black and white sand shaped as countries; by burying and camouflaging itself it redrew the map, which is shown before and after.
- How it works: Sand collected in Hawaii was arranged into national shapes in a lab tank at the Kewalo Marine Laboratory and filmed over a month.
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/58688c8a6a496327e937e35b/84e3a87b-a9df-41ed-9ef2-7732a42f489b/squid+map+before+after.jpg https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/58688c8a6a496327e937e35b/b889ed3e-4ddd-48f6-95a3-b591ab4a3ce7/jiabao+li+squid+map+2+copy.jpg
- Project page: https://www.jiabaoli.org/squid-map

#### Think Evolution #1: Kiku-ishi (Ammonite) — AKI INOMATA (2016)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Idea: A thought experiment in reverse evolution: an octopus tries on the body its lineage gave up.
- What it is: The artist rebuilt an ammonite shell from CT scans of a fossil and offered it to an octopus, whose ancestors abandoned their shells millions of years ago; the video follows the octopus entering the extinct form.
- How it works: Ammonite fossil CT data (White Rabbit Corporation) remodelled in 3D CG and cast in resin; HD video.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/606429490
- Images: https://www.aki-inomata.com/shared/img/works/07/07-01.jpg https://www.aki-inomata.com/shared/img/works/07/07-02.jpg
- Project page: https://www.aki-inomata.com/works/kiku-ishi/

#### Sculpture for Octopuses: Exploring for Their Favorite Colors — Shimabuku (2010)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: More than Human, Design Museum 2025; Animals & Us, Turner Contemporary 2016
- Idea: Art whose audience is an octopus: the work is completed by the animal's perception and choice.
- What it is: Small glass balls in many colours made for octopuses, thrown into the sea from a fishing boat and later placed in a tank at Suma Aqualife Park, Kobe, so the artist could film which colours the animals chose.
- How it works: Hand-made glass objects offered to octopuses, documented as a slideshow of their reactions.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=90ogkcNM_AY
- Project page: https://designmuseum.org/exhibitions/more-than-human

#### Then, I decided to give a tour of Tokyo to the octopus from Akashi — Shimabuku (2000)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Altered vision, Body schema & movement · Medium: Performance & participatory, Screen & web
- Shown at: Museum of Contemporary Art Tokyo (collection); Museum of Fine Arts, Houston (collection)
- Idea: An on-land encounter that asks what a city is for an octopus, while admitting we can only guess at its view.
- What it is: The artist bought a live octopus in the fishing town of Akashi, took it by train to Tokyo, showed it Mount Fuji, Tokyo Tower and the Tsukiji fish market, then brought it back and released it into the sea.
- How it works: A travelling performance with a live octopus carried in a water container, documented as video, photographs and a hand-drawn map.
- Images: https://mot-collection-search.jp/media_files/large/16282.jpg https://mot-collection-search.jp/media_files/large/16284.jpg
- Project page: https://mot-collection-search.jp/en/shiryo/5282/

### Tails, tentacles & extra limbs

Homuncular flexibility: embodying bodies with more or other limbs than ours.

#### I Am Octopus — David Chaseling (2026)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Idea: Moving by arms alone: locomotion is stretching, sticking and releasing tentacles rather than walking.
- What it is: A VR platformer in which you are an octopus captured by a scientist; you stretch your tentacles to fling, pull and launch yourself through test chambers.
- How it works: Hand-based tentacle locomotion on Meta Quest, released in early access; made by David Chaseling and Dylan Van Beek.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=7eqbGRgi64I
- Images: https://queststoredb.com/media/9608086525922823_cover_landscape.webp
- Project page: https://www.meta.com/experiences/i-am-octopus/9608086525922823/

#### Juggling Extra Limbs — Hongyu Zhou (2025)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Many-armed bodies need attention strategies, not only mappings.
- What it is: A study of the strategies people use to control several supernumerary arms at once in VR.
- How it works: Head-mounted VR with multiple virtual arms driven by different body and controller mappings; task and interview analysis.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3706598.3713647 (CHI 2025)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=COwif96tbf0

#### HandAvatar — Yu Jiang (2023)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: The hand is a small body: its joints can stand in for any creature's legs.
- What it is: A method that lets users embody arbitrary non-humanoid avatars, such as a spider, dragon or crab, by moving the fingers of one hand.
- How it works: Hand tracking with automatically generated joint-to-joint mappings optimised for precision, structural similarity and comfort.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3544548.3581027 (CHI 2023)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EJwMmeSN01Q

#### JIZAI ARMS — Masahiko Inami (2023)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement, Collective & networked · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, Performance & participatory
- Idea: Robot limbs as social objects: a body that others can extend, lend and share.
- What it is: A wearable base with sockets for detachable robotic arms that can be added, swapped between wearers and operated by other people, forming 'social digital cyborgs'.
- How it works: A backpack base with six sockets holds detachable robotic arms, teleoperated through scaled-down master arms; designed collaboratively with artists and users.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3544548.3581169 (CHI 2023)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KFyZQfqaXhE

#### Embodiment of Supernumerary Robotic Limbs in VR — Michiteru Kitazaki (2022)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Adding limbs, not replacing them: extra arms can be owned alongside the real ones.
- What it is: Participants used two extra virtual robotic arms controlled by their feet while their real arms remained free.
- How it works: Head-mounted VR with foot tracking mapped to two robotic arms, with visuotactile stimulation and ownership measures.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-022-13981-w (Scientific Reports 2022)
- Images: https://media.springernature.com/full/springer-static/image/art%3A10.1038%2Fs41598-022-13981-w/MediaObjects/41598_2022_13981_Fig1_HTML.png

#### Owning an Independent Supernumerary Limb — Gowrishankar Ganesh (2022)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: A limb with its own control channel, like an octopus arm, can still become part of the self.
- What it is: Participants controlled a virtual sixth limb through muscle signals not tied to any existing limb's movement.
- How it works: Electromyography-based control of a virtual arm in a head-mounted display, following the rubber-hand paradigm.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-022-06040-x (Scientific Reports 2022)
- Images: https://media.springernature.com/full/springer-static/image/art%3A10.1038%2Fs41598-022-06040-x/MediaObjects/41598_2022_6040_Fig1_HTML.png

#### Tentacular — Firepunchd Games (2022)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Idea: Hands without bones: precise human tasks turn into floppy physics, and giant scale makes the human world feel like toys.
- What it is: A VR puzzle game in which you are a giant, good-natured sea creature whose arms are two long tentacles, used to lift, stack and fling objects around a harbour island.
- How it works: Motion controllers drive physically simulated tentacles with suction-cup grabbing on Meta Quest and SteamVR.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=go6ee9iW-Es
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/1220100/header.jpg
- Project page: https://www.tentacular.com/

#### Carrion — Phobia Game Studio (2020)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: The monster's body: many tentacles grab the world at once and the body's size changes what it can do.
- What it is: A 'reverse horror' game in which you are an amorphous tentacled creature escaping a research facility, growing as you consume and squeezing through vents.
- How it works: A procedurally animated mass of tentacles in 2D pixel art, controlled by pointing where it should reach.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jZENtOGdMkc
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/953490/header.jpg
- Project page: https://www.devolverdigital.com/games/carrion

#### The Sixth Finger Illusion — Matthew R. Longo (2020)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Idea: Illusions show that the number of fingers is not fixed in the body's map.
- What it is: A mirror-box illusion in which synchronous touches make people feel a sixth finger on their hand continuously; a 2026 follow-up embodies another person's finger.
- How it works: Mirror box with conflicting visual and tactile stroking of the hand; ownership measured by questionnaire and localisation.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1177/0301006620939457 (Perception 2020)

#### Virtually-Extended Proprioception — Shengdong Zhao (2020)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Extend proprioception by growing the body toward what you want to reach.
- What it is: Users get an appended virtual limb that reaches far into the scene, giving them a body-based sense of where distant targets are.
- How it works: Head-mounted VR with an appended virtual arm or leg attached to the avatar, evaluated for target selection.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3313831.3376557 (CHI 2020)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IvwwMAtxrpA

#### Arque: Artificial Biomimicry-Inspired Tail — Kouta Minamizawa, MHD Yamen Saraiji (2019)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH 2019 Emerging Technologies
- Idea: Borrow the tail animals use for balance, as a new human body part.
- What it is: A wearable artificial tail driven by artificial muscles that shifts the wearer's balance and can push or pull the body.
- How it works: Jointed spring-based tail with four pneumatic artificial muscles; tail length and weight adjusted to the wearer.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3305367.3327987 (ACM SIGGRAPH 2019 Emerging Technologies)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Tr1-IhEhXYQ

#### Augmenting Human With a Tail — Haoran Xie (2019)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: One tail, two animal functions: physical support and emotional signal.
- What it is: A wearable tail that can lock into a support to hold the wearer's weight like a kangaroo's, or wag and move to express emotion.
- How it works: Segmented motor-driven tail with a locking mechanism for support mode and preset motion patterns for emotion mode.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3311823.3311847 (Augmented Human 2019)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=PNdFxswDvsU

#### Owning a Virtual Tail without a Mirror — Takuji Narumi (2019)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Body parts behind us can be owned from partial glimpses, as real tails are.
- What it is: A study of whether people can own a virtual tail they only glimpse partly, rather than watching it continuously in a mirror.
- How it works: Head-mounted VR with hip-driven tail and conditions varying how much visuomotor feedback is visible.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3343036.3343139 (ACM Symposium on Applied Perception 2019)

#### Real-Time Remapping of a Third Arm — Adam Drogemuller (2019)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: A third arm can borrow control from whichever limb is free.
- What it is: Users switch which of their limbs (either arm, either leg or the head) drives a virtual third arm, by gazing at the limb and saying 'switch'.
- How it works: Head-mounted VR with gaze-based limb selection and voice confirmation; box-collection tasks with 12 participants.
- Paper: https://diglib.eg.org/handle/10.2312/egve20191281 (ICAT-EGVE 2019 (DOI 10.2312/egve.20191281))

#### HanaHana — Mélodie Mousset (2018)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: The body keeps multiplying outward: your hands become the building material of the world, a playful study of extra limbs and a self without edges.
- What it is: A VR sculpture garden where hands sprout from your fingertips and every surface you touch; you grow towers and forests of hands, and a later edition lets up to ten people share the world as vaporous avatars.
- How it works: Hand-tracked room-scale VR with procedurally growing hand meshes; developed with the Laboratory of Cognitive Neuroscience at EPFL (likely).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Sf5B_iZrf0o
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/Sf5B_iZrf0o/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Sf5B_iZrf0o

#### MetaArms — MHD Yamen Saraiji, Kouta Minamizawa, Masahiko Inami (2018)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
