- Idea: Remapping feet to arms shows how quickly the body schema adopts machine limbs.
- What it is: A pair of robotic arms worn on the back and controlled by the wearer's feet, so that the body gains two extra arms driven by remapped legs.
- How it works: Foot position and toe bending, tracked by sensors, drive two backpack-mounted anthropomorphic arms; haptic feedback from the robot hands is returned to the feet.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3242587.3242665 (UIST 2018)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hcnF3RkiItc

#### Supernumerary Arms for Gestural Communication — Ehud Sharlin (2018)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Extra limbs can speak: a body with more arms has more gestures.
- What it is: Wearable extra robotic arms used not for tasks but for gestures, to explore how a cyborg might communicate with added limbs.
- How it works: Shoulder-mounted robotic arms driven by preset and user-controlled gesture motions.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3170427.3188683 (CHI 2018 Late-Breaking Work)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CwnPO8OpI_I

#### MetaLimbs — Kouta Minamizawa, MHD Yamen Saraiji (2017)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH 2017 Emerging Technologies
- Idea: Limb substitution: legs become arms, and the body plan is rearranged.
- What it is: A backpack with two robotic arms controlled by the wearer's legs and toes, so that a seated person has four arms.
- How it works: Motion tracking of legs and feet relative to the torso and of toe bending, mapped to robot arm and gripper, with force feedback to the feet.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3084822.3084837 (ACM SIGGRAPH 2017 Emerging Technologies)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NIuIiI5mVhI

#### Third Thumb — Dani Clode, Tamar Makin (2017)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: An extra digit can be learned in days, and it changes how the brain represents the hand.
- What it is: A 3D-printed extra thumb worn beside the little finger and controlled by pressure sensors under the toes; users learn to hold, carry and play with it.
- How it works: Motorised thumb on the hand with wireless toe-pressure control; the 2021 study used fMRI before and after five days of training.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1126/scirobotics.abd7935 (Science Robotics 2021)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GKSCmkCE5og

#### "Wow! I Have Six Fingers!" — Anatole Lécuyer (2016)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: A structurally different hand can be accepted as one's own.
- What it is: Participants saw a realistic six-fingered virtual hand move with their own hand and rated how much it felt like theirs.
- How it works: Hand motion capture driving a six-digit virtual hand in a head-mounted display; ownership and agency questionnaires.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.3389/frobt.2016.00027 (Frontiers in Robotics and AI 2016)
- Images: https://www.frontiersin.org/files/Articles/181118/frobt-03-00027-HTML/image_m/frobt-03-00027-g001.jpg

#### Control Schemes for a Third Arm — Stanford Virtual Human Interaction Lab (2016)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: An extra limb is only as good as the scheme that drives it.
- What it is: A comparison of ways to control an avatar's third arm, from bimanual to head- and gaze-based mappings, for task performance and ownership.
- How it works: Within-subjects VR study using 3D-interaction techniques to map existing limbs onto a third arm.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1162/pres_a_00251 (Presence: Teleoperators and Virtual Environments 2016)

#### The Human Octopus — Jaan Aru (2016)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: More hands do not mean more skill; the octopus body has to be learned.
- What it is: Participants controlled different numbers of virtual supernumerary hands through hand tracking, with delays and scaling added to test their use.
- How it works: Leap Motion hand tracking in a head-mounted display, with extra virtual hands copying or transforming real hand motion.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1101/056812 (bioRxiv 2016)

#### Homuncular Flexibility in Virtual Reality — Stanford Virtual Human Interaction Lab (2015)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: The brain's body map can learn a new body plan within minutes (Lanier's homuncular flexibility).
- What it is: Participants hit targets with avatars whose limbs were remapped, for example controlling a third arm by rotating their wrists, or swapping arms and legs.
- How it works: Full-body tracking in a head-mounted display with remapped motions: legs to arms, wrist rotation to a third arm's reach.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1111/jcc4.12107 (Journal of Computer-Mediated Communication 2015)

#### Octodad: Dadliest Catch — Young Horses (2014)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: A boneless body forced into human routines: the controls make the mismatch between body and task felt.
- What it is: An octopus disguised as a suburban father tries to do chores, shop and go to the aquarium without his family noticing he is an octopus.
- How it works: Each leg and the arm are controlled separately by different buttons and mouse motion, over a physically simulated body; follows the 2010 DePaul student game Octodad.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=03rP_O2k8XM
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/224480/header.jpg
- Project page: https://store.steampowered.com/app/224480/

#### Human Tails — Mel Slater (2013)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Dome, CAVE & projection
- Idea: People learn to own and use a body part that humans do not have.
- What it is: Participants in a CAVE controlled a humanoid avatar with a long tail, moving it with their hips, and later had to use it to block falling objects.
- How it works: CAVE-like projection with full-body tracking; hip movement mapped to tail motion, compared with a randomly moving tail.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/tvcg.2013.32 (IEEE TVCG 2013 (IEEE VR 2013))

#### The Very Long Arm Illusion — Konstantina Kilteni (2012)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Body ownership stretches: a grossly asymmetric body can still be one's own.
- What it is: Participants saw their virtual arm stretch up to four times its length while it stayed synchronised with their real arm.
- How it works: Head-tracked stereo head-mounted display with a virtual body coincident with the real one; arm length manipulated across five conditions.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0040867 (PLoS ONE 2012)
- Images: https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article/figure/image?size=inline&id=10.1371/journal.pone.0040867.g001

#### shippo — neurowear (2012)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Shown at: Tokyo Game Show 2012
- Idea: A tail for humans makes mood a shared, animal signal rather than a private feeling.
- What it is: A concept for a brainwave-controlled tail that wags faster when the wearer is excited and slowly when calm, and shares the wearer's mood on a map.
- How it works: EEG headset linked to a motorised tail and a smartphone app.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FwSTLm7cy_s
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/FwSTLm7cy_s/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FwSTLm7cy_s

### Shared & collective bodies

One body shared by many people, or many bodies moving as one.

#### TentacUs — Botao 'Amber' Hu, Danlin Huang, Reality Design Lab (2025)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Touch & haptics, Collective & networked, Body schema & movement · Medium: Mixed reality, Wearable & sensory device, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH Asia 2025 XR
- Idea: Becoming octopus means intelligence without a centre: many bodies sense and move as arms of one being.
- What it is: A mixed-reality ritual in which dancers join left hands in a ring and each becomes one tentacle of a collective octopus-like being; what one right hand senses nearby is passed as vibration to its neighbours.
- How it works: Soft-textile tentacle gloves hold smartphones whose LiDAR senses proximity and whose vibration motors broadcast haptic waves to neighbours; touch data are drawn as point clouds over a 3D Gaussian-splat scan on a public display.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3761667.3761962 (SIGGRAPH Asia 2025 XR)
- Images: https://arxiv.org/html/2511.12533v3/images/tentacus.jpg https://images.spr.so/cdn-cgi/imagedelivery/j42No7y-dcokJuNgXeA0ig/afd62d02-7102-4a26-b5b4-03df6cc2744b/image2/public
- Project page: https://amber.botao.hu/design/tentacus
- Code: https://github.com/realitydeslab/tentacus

#### GravField — Botao 'Amber' Hu, Reality Design Lab (2024)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement, Collective & networked, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Mixed reality, Performance & participatory, Spatial audio
- Shown at: ICLC 2024 Shanghai; SIGGRAPH Asia 2024 XR; IEEE VR 2025 XR Gallery; NIME 2025
- Idea: Bodies become masses in a shared field: the self is felt as a pull between people rather than a point.
- What it is: A co-located mixed-reality performance where participants in AR headsets make music together through their distances, formations and head movements, while live-coders shape the virtual fields of attraction and repulsion between them.
- How it works: HoloKit headsets networked with Multipeer Connectivity stream intercorporeal signals (distance, area, height difference, head sync) to live-coders, who map them to AR visuals and sound.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3772318.3790651 (CHI 2026)
- Video: https://vimeo.com/955522087
- Images: https://reality.design/media/_resources/GravField/project-gravfield-figure-01.jpg https://reality.design/media/_resources/GravField/project-gravfield-figure-03.jpg
- Project page: https://reality.design/project/gravfield
- Code: https://github.com/realitydeslab/gravfield

#### Parasitic Body — Michiteru Kitazaki (2019)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: One body, two minds: a third limb can belong to someone else.
- What it is: A shared-body VR system in which one person is the main body and another, the 'parasite', controls a third arm from their shoulder.
- How it works: Networked VR in which the parasite operator's view is attached to the host's body, with varying perspective dependency.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/vr.2019.8798351 (IEEE VR 2019)

## Becoming Bird

Flight and avian senses: flying as a bird, magnetoreception, ultraviolet vision, birdsong and the flock.

### Flight

Flying with wings and a bird's body.

#### HapticWings — Seungwoo Je (2025)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Extra body parts feel real when they have weight.
- What it is: A backpack that shifts weight across the back so that flapping or folding virtual wings is felt as a moving load.
- How it works: Back-worn 2D weight-shifting mechanism synchronised with wing animation in VR; three user studies.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3715336.3735755 (ACM DIS 2025)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=O6CCWw-jXak

#### I Am Bird — New Folder Games (2025)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Idea: Arms as wings: flight is produced by flapping, so the player's own effort becomes lift.
- What it is: A VR sandbox in which you are a city bird that flaps its arms to fly, steals food and causes chaos in the streets.
- How it works: Arm-flapping motion tracked by the controllers generates lift and steering on Meta Quest.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LpwfHtfIuII
- Images: https://queststoredb.com/media/25148042068216938_cover_landscape.webp
- Project page: https://www.meta.com/experiences/i-am-bird/25148042068216938/

#### Virtual Animal Embodiment for Actor Training — Rachel McDonnell (2024)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, Performance & participatory
- Idea: Animal work in acting, long done through imagination, can be done inside the animal's body.
- What it is: A real-time system that lets actors drive a photorealistic eagle avatar with their own bodies as part of movement training.
- How it works: Motion capture of the actor retargeted to an eagle rig and shown in a head-mounted display.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/vrw62533.2024.00083 (IEEE VR 2024 Workshops)

#### Virtual Transcendent Dream — Bernhard E. Riecke (2022)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement, Breath & interoception · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Embodied flight can evoke the empowerment and self-transcendence of flying dreams.
- What it is: A VR flying experience modelled on flying dreams, in which participants fly with their bodies through dream-like landscapes.
- How it works: Head-mounted VR with leaning-based embodied flight and self-motion cues, compared with a controller interface.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3491102.3517677 (CHI 2022)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TOJN18MxFsM

#### Aery - Little Bird Adventure — EpiXR Games (2020)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Game, VR headset
- Idea: Bird flight as rest: no combat, just gliding through places.
- What it is: A calm flying game in which you are a small bird exploring dreamlike landscapes; a VR edition followed in 2023.
- How it works: Third-person flight with collectibles on PC and consoles, plus a VR-supported edition with head-steered flight.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mRkG9156FgE
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/1424770/header.jpg
- Project page: https://store.steampowered.com/app/1424770/

#### Aria (空氣頌) — Kingsley Ng, Eugene Birman (2020)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Altered vision, Hearing & vibration, Breath & interoception · Medium: 360° / immersive film, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: New Vision Arts Festival (ReNew Vision) 2020
- Idea: Listening to air from where birds and insects are: the climate crisis heard from nonhuman heights.
- What it is: A VR dance-and-music work filmed in the conservatory of Hong Kong Park, where cameras take the unusual viewpoints of an insect or a bird in flight as holograms of the Danish ensemble Theatre of Voices float in mid-air and dancers and the Hong Kong Children's Choir perform.
- How it works: 360° video with hologram projections and a score driven by air-quality data, streamed on YouTube for the festival's online platform.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Mcd-hhhlAAw
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/Mcd-hhhlAAw/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://www.vrtuoluo.cn/521799.html

#### Do You Feel Like Flying? — Soroosh Mashal (2020)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Idea: Most people already imagine wings on their backs; design flight around that body image.
- What it is: A survey of how people imagine wings attached to their bodies and which movements they make to fly, used to build a winged-avatar flight prototype.
- How it works: Questionnaire with 76 participants, motion analysis of imagined flight, and a VR prototype with wing avatar (Valkyrie Project).
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/mcg.2020.2997870 (IEEE Computer Graphics and Applications 2020)

#### Mare — Visiontrick Media (2020)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Shown at: Venice VR Expanded 2021
- Idea: The player never has hands: seeing, moving and helping all happen through a bird's gaze and flight.
- What it is: A VR puzzle game in which the player wakes up embodying an artificial bird and flies across strange landscapes to guide and protect a vulnerable AI companion.
- How it works: Gaze-directed flight on standalone VR (Oculus Quest); the bird is steered by head direction and perching points.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iJouYmaHOuk
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2021/Schede_film/970x647/Venice_VR_Expanded/guerreiro_mare.jpg?itok=RzlLB4KG
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2021/lineup/venice-vr-expanded/mare

#### Perch to Fly — Bernhard E. Riecke (2019)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: A bird's perch as interface: the legs, not the hands, steer flight.
- What it is: A flying interface in which the user sits in a flexible 'perching' stance and steers flight with their lower body.
- How it works: Flexible perching stool with leg and body leaning tracked to control VR flight; compared with sitting and standing.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3322276.3322357 (ACM DIS 2019)

#### Transformation to a Bird — Akimi Oyanagi (2019)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Becoming a bird changes how height feels: the Proteus effect of a body that belongs in the sky.
- What it is: Participants with fear of heights flew in VR with either a bird avatar or a human avatar.
- How it works: Head-mounted VR flight with a first-person bird body; fear of heights measured by questionnaire and physiology.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3313950.3313976 (ICIGP 2019)

#### Body Ownership of a Bird Avatar — Akimi Oyanagi (2018)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement, Hearing & vibration · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Specific animal features, not just synchrony, build ownership of a non-human body.
- What it is: Experiments on which bird features, such as flapping, a short body and wing sounds, make a bird avatar feel like one's own.
- How it works: Head-mounted VR with arm-tracked flapping and flapping sounds; ownership compared between bird and human avatars.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.17706/jcp.13.6.596-602 (Journal of Computers 2018)

#### Feather — Samurai Punk (2018)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Flight without objectives: being a bird is the whole activity.
- What it is: A relaxed multiplayer game in which you are a bird gliding over an island, with no goals, score or competition.
- How it works: Third-person gliding with an adaptive soundtrack that responds to where and how the player flies.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NaaaHuZ3Pno
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/NaaaHuZ3Pno/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://samuraipunk.com/

#### JediFlight — Taiwoo Park (2018)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Idea: One human limb can drive two virtual limbs if the mapping splits elbow and hand.
- What it is: A VR game in which each arm controls both a wing and a hand at once, so the player flies while interacting with the world.
- How it works: Hand and elbow trackers: elbow position drives the wing, hand position drives the hand.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3270316.3273043 (CHI PLAY 2018 Extended Abstracts)

#### Eagle Flight — Ubisoft Montreal (2016)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Idea: Flight steered by the head: the eagle's body is mapped onto the steadiest part of yours, so looking is flying.
- What it is: A VR game in which you fly as an eagle over a Paris abandoned by humans and overgrown by nature, racing and fighting other eagles.
- How it works: Head orientation steers the eagle on Oculus Rift, HTC Vive and PlayStation VR, with a comfort vignette that narrows the view in turns.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=C7NAySeF4Y8
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/408250/header.jpg
- Project page: http://eagleflight.ubisoft.com/

#### Embodied Flying with Kinect — Bernhard E. Riecke (2016)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Idea: Ask people how they would fly with their bodies before deciding what they become.
- What it is: A VR game in which participants fly by body gestures tracked by a Kinect, with a study of which gestures and forms people choose for flying.
- How it works: Microsoft Kinect body tracking mapped to flight controls in a head-mounted display.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/mixra.2016.7858996 (IEEE Mixed Reality Art (MRA) 2016)

#### Extending the Human Body with Virtual Wings — Mie C. S. Egeberg (2016)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Moving the wings, not being touched on them, is what makes them part of the body.
- What it is: Participants controlled a humanoid avatar with a pair of wings under visuomotor, visuotactile or no synchronous feedback.
- How it works: Head-mounted VR with tracked arms driving wing motion; visuotactile strokes on the back in one condition.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2927929.2927940 (Virtual Reality International Conference (VRIC) 2016)

#### Eagle POV over Mont Blanc — Freedom Conservation (2015)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Altered vision, Body schema & movement · Medium: Screen & web
- Idea: A camera on the bird gives the bird's path and speed, but not its vision, which shows both the promise and the limit of animal POV.
- What it is: Footage from small cameras carried by a white-tailed eagle and a bald eagle flying over the Mont Blanc massif, showing peaks, glaciers and the trail below from the bird's own flight.
- How it works: Trained eagles fitted with lightweight action cameras (Sony Action Cam Mini) flew from the summits during the Ultra-Trail du Mont-Blanc.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IkRhq-stMS4
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/@FREEDOMCONSERVATION

#### Wings and Flying in Immersive VR — Stefania Serafin (2015)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement, Hearing & vibration · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Wings feel owned when the shoulders do the flapping; sound adds little.
- What it is: Participants flew an obstacle course with virtual wings, flapping by shoulder movement or with a game controller, with varying self-produced wing sounds.
- How it works: Motion-tracked avatar with wings in a head-mounted display; shoulder-driven versus controller-driven flight, with an earlier study (Audio Mostly 2014) on wing sounds.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/vr.2015.7223405 (IEEE VR 2015)

#### Birdly — SOMNIACS, Max Rheiner (2014)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision, Touch & haptics · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH 2014 Emerging Technologies
- Idea: Becoming a bird starts from the posture: lying prone and steering with your arms and hands makes flight a bodily act rather than a camera move.
- What it is: A full-body flight simulator: you lie face down on a padded frame, spread your arms like wings and fly as a red kite over a city or landscape, with wind blowing in your face.
- How it works: A motorised prone platform tilts with the rider's arm and hand movements, synchronised with a VR headset, spatial sound and a fan whose speed follows flight velocity.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2614066.2614101 (SIGGRAPH 2014 Emerging Technologies)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cqBCd0VnN7A
- Images: https://birdlyvr.com/wp-content/uploads/sites/3/2017/03/homepage-birdly-BHP-2868-2.jpg
- Project page: https://www.birdlyvr.com/

#### The Treachery of Sanctuary — Chris Milk (2012)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: The Creators Project San Francisco 2012
- Idea: Birth, death and transfiguration as a bird: the participant's own silhouette turns into flight.
- What it is: A three-panel interactive installation in which visitors' shadows disintegrate into birds, are pecked apart by birds, and finally sprout giant wings that let them take flight.
- How it works: Kinect depth sensing and custom software project real-time silhouettes onto three large screens above a reflecting pool.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=I5__9hq-yas
- Project page: http://milk.co/treachery

#### Humphrey II — Ars Electronica Futurelab (2003)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Ars Electronica Center 2003
- Idea: Flying is made with the whole body instead of a joystick, an early public version of the arms-as-wings posture later used by Birdly.
- What it is: A flight simulator at the Ars Electronica Center in which a visitor hangs face-down in a harness and flies over a virtual landscape by moving arms and body, feeling wind, turbulence and impact through force feedback.
- How it works: Body suspended in a motion-controlled harness with pneumatic force feedback, head-mounted display and body tracking; successor to the 1994 Humphrey simulator.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8l8JCpMEviM
- Project page: https://ars.electronica.art/futurelab/

#### OpenSky — Kazuhiko Hachiya (2003)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Performance & participatory
- Idea: Becoming a bird is taken literally: the body lies on a wing and steers by shifting its weight.
- What it is: An art project to build and fly a one-person jet-powered glider modelled on the bird-shaped Möwe from Hayao Miyazaki's Nausicaä of the Valley of the Wind, flown by the artist lying on its back.
- How it works: Custom flying-wing glider (M-02, later jet-powered M-02J) developed over a decade with test flights from 2006.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nwZkRGxbWDg
- Project page: https://www.petworks.co.jp/~hachiya/works/OpenSky.html

#### Flying Machine — Carsten Höller (1996)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Decision, Hayward Gallery 2015
- Idea: Flight as a physical sensation rather than an image: the body feels lift, speed and the view down on others, without a headset.
- What it is: A visitor is strapped face-down into a harness on a motorised arm and swung in wide circles through the gallery, arms free, like a bird gliding over the crowd.
- How it works: Harness suspended from a rotating steel arm driven by a motor, with the rider's weight and position setting the ride.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=v5pvNtMWRgI

#### Pigeon photography — Julius Neubronner (1907)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Screen & web
- Idea: An early answer to 'what does the bird see': a mechanical eye strapped to the animal, returning images humans had never seen.
- What it is: Homing pigeons carried miniature timed cameras on breast harnesses and photographed the towns and landscapes they flew over; the images were sold as postcards and shown at the 1909 Dresden photography exhibition.
- How it works: Lightweight aluminium cameras with a pneumatic timer took one or more exposures during the flight home; patented in 1908.
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/9/94/Bundesarchiv_Bild_183-R01996%2C_Brieftaube_mit_Fotokamera_cropped.jpg https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/5/54/Julius_Neubronner_with_pigeon_and_camera_1914_cropped.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pigeon_photography

### Avian senses

Magnetic sense, ultraviolet and wide-field vision, birdsong.

#### Seeing with a Bird's Perception — Shuran Fan (2026)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Altered vision, Hearing & vibration, Touch & haptics · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Share a bird's attention to notice the nature already around you.
- What it is: A nature walk with Perch, a sparrow-like robot worn on the shoulder that translates birds' patterns of attention into subtle cues to the wearer.
- How it works: Zoomorphic wearable robot with multimodal cues (movement, sound, vibration) triggered by environmental events.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3772363.3798350 (CHI 2026 Extended Abstracts)

#### Kestrel Drone — Jooyoung Oh (2022)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Body schema & movement · Medium: Game, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: ZER01NE DAY 2022; Displaced Impossibility, Post Territory Ujeongguk, Seoul 2024
- Idea: Borrowing a raptor's eye through a machine: the drone becomes a way to ask how technology looks from the bird's side.
- What it is: An installation combining a PC game, an AI tracking camera and a bird-shaped drone that re-reads drone vision from the viewpoint of a kestrel living in the city.
- How it works: PC game, AI tracking camera, bird drone and neon signs; the player likely steers or watches through the drone's kestrel-like view.
- Images: https://k-artnow.com/data/content/684f7e300877a0.27253385__thum.png
- Project page: https://k-artist.com/Artist_Works.php?tab_num=tab1&artist_num=170&works_num=1014

#### Machine Auguries — Alexandra Daisy Ginsberg (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation, Spatial audio
- Shown at: 24/7, Somerset House London 2019; Toledo Museum of Art 2023; Bildmuseet Umeå 2025
- Idea: Listening for birds becomes listening for their absence: the machine chorus is a false memory of a soundscape that is disappearing.
- What it is: A room under an artificial dawn sky where a local dawn chorus is recreated from natural and machine-generated birdsong, gradually taken over by artificial birds.
- How it works: Birdsong synthesised with generative adversarial networks trained on field recordings, played on multichannel sound with programmed light.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MMBGElLQMG4
- Images: https://www.daisyginsberg.com/img/work/machine_auguries_london_bildmuseet_gallery.jpg https://www.daisyginsberg.com/img/work/machine_auguries_5_web.jpg
- Project page: https://www.daisyginsberg.com/work/machine-auguries

#### Untitled Goose Game — House House (2019)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Shown at: D.I.C.E. Awards 2020 (Game of the Year); Game Developers Choice Awards 2020 (Game of the Year)
- Idea: Counterpoint: the animal body is used for mischief and slapstick, not perception, and it works because the goose stays a goose.
- What it is: A stealth comedy in which you are a goose who ruins villagers' days by stealing, honking and hiding.
- How it works: Top-down third-person controls with a dedicated honk button, wing flap and beak grab.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HGO7vJtlX0I
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/837470/header.jpg
- Project page: https://goose.game

#### HORUS EYE: Bird and Snake Vision for AR — Neven A. M. ElSayed (2016)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Altered vision, Heat & infrared · Medium: AR
- Idea: Animal vision as a lens for seeing what humans cannot.
- What it is: An AR visualization technique that filters the live scene the way a bird's or snake's vision might, to highlight data of interest in the world.
- How it works: Video see-through AR with image filters inspired by raptor acuity and snake infrared sensing, driven by user queries.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/ismar-adjunct.2016.0077 (IEEE ISMAR 2016 Adjunct)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8mg1VngCdpA

#### Berlin Bülbül — David Rothenberg (2015)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Hearing & vibration · Medium: Performance & participatory, Spatial audio
- Idea: Becoming part of the bird's song means entering a real-time exchange whose rules only the bird knows.
- What it is: A duo album and live sessions in which the musician plays clarinet with wild nightingales in Berlin's parks at night, answering and following their songs.
- How it works: Clarinet and live electronics (with Korhan Erel) improvised with singing nightingales, recorded outdoors at night.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=72PKRimqL3c
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Rothenberg_(author)

#### Bird Song Diamond — Victoria Vesna (2014)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: Large Space, Empowerment Informatics (EMP), University of Tsukuba
- Idea: Bird communication is learned from the inside, by trying to produce and answer calls in a shared space.
- What it is: An art-science project with evolutionary biologists on how birds such as Cassin's vireos communicate; in the Japanese version, visitors use their voices and bodies to act as birds in a large projected space.
- How it works: Built on acoustic sensor-array recordings of bird networks; installations used motion tracking, projection and voice interaction in the Large Space at EMP Tsukuba.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HyUaqDa_6Vg
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0gwRb6Kt8hA

#### The Great Silence — Allora & Calzadilla (2014)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration · Medium: Screen & web
- Idea: A related screen work, included for its nonhuman first-person voice: the listening telescope is judged from the parrot's point of view.
- What it is: A video set at the Arecibo radio telescope and the forest nearby, where subtitles give the first-person voice of an endangered Puerto Rican parrot reflecting on humans' search for alien intelligence while ignoring the vocal beings beside them.
- How it works: Single-channel video with subtitled narration written with Ted Chiang, combining footage of the Arecibo Observatory and the Río Abajo forest parrots (likely).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=n0QhHa1aG4I
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=n0QhHa1aG4I

#### Crowbot Jenny — Sputniko! (2010)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, Screen & web
- Shown at: Talk to Me, MoMA New York 2011; 14th Japan Media Arts Festival 2011; Tokyo Art Meeting Transformation, MOT 2010
- Idea: Speaking another species' language through a prosthetic voice box: the device lets the human enter the crows' acoustic society.
- What it is: A music video and wearable device about a solitary girl who prefers animals to people and builds the Crowbot, an instrument that reproduces crow calls so she can talk to her army of crows.
- How it works: Wearable call-synthesising device developed with advice from crow researcher Nicola Clayton (Cambridge), presented with a pop music video.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rdU1F54FEOU
- Images: https://storage.googleapis.com/production-os-assets/assets/18f187ff-8e8a-4f0d-9634-d7808c54c4d1
- Project page: https://sputniko.com/Crowbot-Jenny

#### Dawn Chorus — Marcus Coates (2007)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: Fabrica, Brighton 2015
- Idea: Slowing birdsong down to human pace lets people learn it with their own throats; speeding them up returns them to bird time.
- What it is: A 14-screen video installation of people in cars, offices and bedrooms singing birdsong; each voice is sped back up so that the room fills with a convincing dawn chorus.
- How it works: Individual wild birds were recorded with up to 14 microphones at dawn, slowed by up to 20 times, learned by singers who were filmed at high frame rates, then played back at bird speed.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zF1uihdcZmY
- Images: https://a75hkzli.twic.pics/marcus-coates/images/_1200x630_crop_center-center_none/fabrica.jpg
- Project page: https://marcuscoates.co.uk/projects/68-dawn-chorus

#### from here to ear — Céleste Boursier-Mougenot (1999)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration · Medium: Multisensory installation, Spatial audio
- Shown at: Barbican Curve Gallery London 2010; Jupiter Artland 2018
- Idea: The visitor enters the birds' space as a guest, and the soundscape is composed by the birds' own landings and pecking.
- What it is: Visitors walk into an aviary where free-flying zebra finches land on electric guitars and cymbals laid out as perches, so the birds' movements become live, ever-changing music.
- How it works: Amplified electric guitars and bass fixed horizontally with perches, food and water, played by the finches' weight and beaks.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_wwKlJdojb0
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HyyeXOW5xPk

#### Cockatoo Mask (Performances II) — Rebecca Horn (1973)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: Performance & participatory, Wearable & sensory device, Screen & web
- Idea: An early body extension that borrows a bird's feathers as a new skin for sensing and approaching others.
- What it is: In a filmed performance, a performer wears a mask of white cockatoo feathers that closes and opens around the face, so that touching another person happens through a bird's plumage.
- How it works: Handmade feather mask on a hinged frame, performed for camera and shown in the 16 mm film Performances II.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ekmovwo0e2A
- Project page: https://www.tate.org.uk/art/artists/rebecca-horn-2585

### Flocks & migration

Murmurations, migration routes and moving as a flock.

#### NEST — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Spatial audio
- Shown at: Waltham Forest London Borough of Culture 2019
- Idea: Light and sound move through the park like a flock, suggesting a murmuration rather than letting visitors become one.
- What it is: A kinetic light installation in the trees and moat of Lloyd Park, Waltham Forest, with a 15-minute multichannel soundscape by composer Erland Cooper inspired by starling murmurations.
- How it works: Programmed moving-head lights, custom software and spatial audio mapped onto trees, water and foliage.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/478588817
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2025/11/DRONE_rushes_grbas_fullres.01_06_35_08.Still092.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2025/11/Welcome_To_The_Forest-17.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/nest-kinetic-light-sculpture/

#### Franchise Freedom — DRIFT (2017)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Collective & networked, Altered vision · Medium: Performance & participatory
- Shown at: Art Basel Miami Beach 2017; Amsterdam, above the IJ 2018; Burning Man 2018
- Idea: Machines borrow a bird flock's rules, so the audience perceives flocking behaviour, not individual birds, as the being on stage.
- What it is: A night-time performance in which hundreds of lit drones fly as an autonomous swarm following an algorithm derived from starling murmurations, first over Art Basel Miami Beach.
- How it works: Software modelling starling flight behaviour, from research begun in 2007 with Flylight, embedded in Intel Shooting Star drones.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=wCyNjt8TABk
- Images: https://studiodrift.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/1.-Studio-Drift_Franchise-Freedom_ABurning-Man-Festival_USA_2018_Rahi-Rezvani.jpg
- Project page: https://studiodrift.com/work/franchise-freedom/

#### Moon Goose Analogue: Lunar Migration Bird Facility — Agnes Meyer-Brandis (2011)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Collective & networked · Medium: Multisensory installation, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: FACT Liverpool 2011; VIDA 15.0 (second prize) 2013
- Idea: Joining a flock means being accepted as kin: imprinting turns the human into the geese's mother and flight trainer.
- What it is: The artist raised eleven geese, named after astronauts, from eggs, imprinted them on herself and trained them to fly in a 'Moon analogue' habitat in Italy, shown to visitors through a live video control room.
- How it works: Hand-rearing and imprinting from hatching, daily flight training, and remote cameras streaming the geese's habitat into a gallery control room.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pIKNbDWdS_0
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnes_Meyer-Brandis

#### PigeonBlog — Beatriz da Costa (2006)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Smell & taste, Collective & networked · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, Screen & web
- Shown at: ISEA 2006 / ZeroOne San Jose
- Idea: The city's air is sensed at pigeon altitude: the birds become co-researchers and the map shows the world as they breathe it.
- What it is: Homing pigeons carry small backpacks that measure carbon monoxide and nitrogen oxides as they fly over Southern California and send readings to a live online map.
- How it works: Custom GPS, gas-sensor and GSM/SMS backpacks built with Cina Hazegh and Kevin Ponto, streaming to a Google Maps interface.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XXNh5dKIh18

#### Swamped! — MIT Media Lab Synthetic Characters Group (1998)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation, Game
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH 98 Enhanced Realities
- Idea: A 'sympathetic interface': you steer an animal through a toy version of its body, acting as its conscience rather than its puppeteer.
- What it is: A visitor squeezes, waves and tilts an instrumented plush chicken to guide an animated chicken as it protects its eggs from a hungry raccoon in a barnyard.
- How it works: Plush toy with accelerometers, gyros, squeeze and bend sensors; gestures recognised with HMMs and fed to autonomous behaviour-based characters.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/302979.303028 (CHI 1999)
- Images: https://characters.media.mit.edu/images/swamped.gif
- Project page: https://characters.media.mit.edu/projects/swamped.html

## Becoming Insect

Small scales and many eyes: bees and pollinators, ants and colonies, butterflies, spiders and compound vision.

### Bees & pollinators

Seeing flowers in ultraviolet, the waggle dance, the hive.

#### Visión de Abeja: Experiencia Floral Inmersiva — Viviana Álvarez Chomón, Jaime Martínez Harms (2026)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Museo de Historia Natural de Valparaíso 2026
- Idea: Shrinking to bee size and seeing in ultraviolet reveals flower patterns that exist for pollinators, not for us.
- What it is: A guided VR experience in which visitors fly at roughly bee scale over a garden of two native flowers of Chile's flowering Atacama desert, switching between human vision, ultraviolet vision and a model of bee colour and optics.
- How it works: VR headset with a modelled garden of Cistanthe longiscapa and Argylia radiata; view modes likely based on UV photography and bee colour-vision models from the INIA La Cruz pollination research.
- Images: https://radiofestival.b-cdn.net/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/MINISTRA-s.jpeg
- Project page: https://www.radiofestival.cl/ministerio-de-ciencia-lanzo-en-valparaiso-vision-de-abeja-experiencia-inmersiva-en-realidad-virtual-sobre-la-percepcion-de-las-abejas/

#### Animal-View Video Camera — Vera Vasas (2024)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Screen & web
- Idea: Moving images of another animal's colour world, accurate to its photoreceptors.
- What it is: A camera system and software that record video in the colours bees, birds and other animals see, including ultraviolet.
- How it works: Beam-splitter camera recording UV and visible channels simultaneously, converted to animal photoreceptor catches in Python.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pbio.3002444 (PLOS Biology 2024)
- Images: https://journals.plos.org/plosbiology/article/figure/image?size=inline&id=10.1371/journal.pbio.3002444.g001

#### home — Temsüyanger Longkumer (2023)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Collective & networked, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, 360° / immersive film
- Shown at: Venice Immersive 2023
- Idea: Living inside the hive: the colony as a single organism whose choices mirror our own habitat-making.
- What it is: A VR work filmed with a bee colony in Gaili, Nagaland, that takes the viewer inside a beehive as a model of humanity's relationship with its planet.
- How it works: Stereoscopic footage inside and around a living hive combined with animated sequences (VR headset).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1-Xy4B7MbzA
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2023/Schede_film/970x647/Ve_Immersive/longkumer.jpg?itok=k7tAqi6V
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2023/venice-immersive/home

#### Pollinator Pathmaker — Alexandra Daisy Ginsberg (2021)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Collective & networked · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: Eden Project 2021; LAS Art Foundation, Berlin
- Idea: Designing from the pollinators' point of view means handing aesthetic choices to their needs: flower shape, colour and bloom time.
- What it is: A living artwork and online tool: an algorithm designs gardens for as many pollinator species as possible rather than for human taste, and anyone can generate and plant their own.
- How it works: An algorithm built with pollination scientists selects and arranges plant species by the needs of pollinator groups; commissioned by the Eden Project.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IN3YzdziqBY
- Project page: https://www.pollinator.art/

#### DOON (Over There) — Issay Rodriguez (2020)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Collective & networked · Medium: VR headset
- Shown at: Art Fair Philippines 2020
- Idea: You communicate as a bee does, with the body rather than words: the dance itself is the message to the hive.
- What it is: A VR artwork that places the viewer inside a beehive, where they guide fellow bees to sources of nectar and pollen by performing the waggle dance.
- How it works: Interactive VR environment in which the viewer's movement is likely mapped to the waggle dance that directs virtual hive mates.
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/67c1754c89592f0c27fddb0b/bf9663bb-6482-4bde-bdd8-13bc146a4b7e/Guiding+virtual+bees+in+the+VR+environment+of+Issay+Rodriguez%E2%80%99s+DOON+%28Over+There%29%2C+2020.+Image+courtesy+of+%E2%80%98DOON%E2%80%99+VR+Team.?format=1500w https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/67c1754c89592f0c27fddb0b/0ec2220a-faf3-4e75-a3f5-6c24fe8b37ad/Issay%2BRodriguez%2C%2BVR%2Benvironment%2Boverlaid%2Bscreenshots%2Bof%2B%E2%80%98DOON%2B%28Over%2BThere%29%E2%80%99%2C%2B2020.jpeg?format=1500w
- Project page: https://www.artandmarket.net/analysis/2020/1/5/on-the-possibilities-of-virtual-reality-art

#### Bee Simulator — VARSAV Game Studios (2019)
- Type: Game · Senses: Altered vision, Smell & taste · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Bee senses gamified: a 'bee vision' mode highlights pollen and scent paths, and the waggle dance is a rhythm mini-game.
- What it is: An open-world game in which you are a honeybee in a city park, collecting pollen, dancing to share flower locations and defending the hive.
- How it works: Third-person flight with a toggleable sense mode, collection tasks and a dance-matching mini-game.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NSEQIvAgH9o
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/914750/header.jpg
- Project page: https://beesimulator.com/

#### The Hive — Wolfgang Buttress (2015)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Touch & haptics, Collective & networked · Medium: Multisensory installation, Spatial audio
- Shown at: Expo Milano 2015 UK Pavilion; Royal Botanic Gardens Kew 2016
- Idea: Sensing a colony as one body: the hive's vibrations become light, sound and a signal felt through the skull.
- What it is: A 17-metre aluminium lattice sculpture whose 1,000 LED lights and sound pulse in real time with the vibrations of a live honeybee colony; visitors can bite on a stick to hear the bees by bone conduction.
- How it works: Accelerometers in a hive at Kew stream vibration data that drive LEDs and a composed soundscape; bone-conduction listening posts.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kEsq8GREX9A
- Project page: https://www.wolfgangbuttress.com

#### a better nectar — Jessica Rath (2015)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: University Art Museum, California State University Long Beach 2015
- Idea: Seeing ultraviolet and losing red is the entry point to a bee's world of flowers.
- What it is: An exhibition made with bee scientists that puts visitors in a bee's position: 'Bee Purple' shows flowers in the colours bees see, and 'Resonant Nest' is a sculpted nest large enough to walk into.
- How it works: Sculpture, ultraviolet-shifted colour works and a walk-in nest structure, informed by research with pollination scientists.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=B7DjD9JlGqo
- Images: http://jessicarath.com/files/2014/09/web_nest-720x720.jpg http://jessicarath.com/files/2014/09/web_purple-720x720.jpg
- Project page: http://jessicarath.com/blog/projects/a-better-nectar/

#### The Honeybee Ballet — Jonathon Keats (2008)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Collective & networked, Body schema & movement · Medium: Performance & participatory, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Yerba Buena Center for the Arts 2008
- Idea: Instead of making humans dance like bees, the work writes in the bees' own language, the waggle dance, by shaping what they perceive.
- What it is: A ballet choreographed for honeybees: by planting flowers in chosen patterns on a university farm, the artist set the routes the foragers would report, so the bees' own waggle dances in the hive performed his choreography.
- How it works: Flower plantings arranged by distance and direction from the hive to 'compose' the waggle dances that foragers perform to recruit nestmates.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3OPKKbW85Jg
- Project page: https://rhizome.org/community/13178/

#### Bee's — Susana Soares (2007)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Smell & taste, Breath & interoception · Medium: Multisensory installation, Wearable & sensory device
- Shown at: Science Gallery Dublin
- Idea: The bee's nose becomes a human organ of perception, a diagnostic sense borrowed from another species.
- What it is: Hand-blown glass objects hold trained honeybees; a person breathes into the glass and the bees fly into a small chamber if they smell the chemical markers of disease.
- How it works: Bees conditioned with Pavlovian training to respond to target odours, housed in two-chamber glass vessels.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NcWyf20aMv0

#### Live-In Hive — Mark Thompson (1976)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Smell & taste, Hearing & vibration, Touch & haptics · Medium: Performance & participatory, Multisensory installation
- Idea: Immersion before VR: the hive's heat, hum and smell surround the head, and the human becomes a passive member of the colony.
- What it is: The artist put his head through the floor of a glass-walled hive and stayed inside for long periods while a swarm of honeybees built comb around him and flew in and out through a tube.
- How it works: Wooden box with glass and metal walls, a head opening in the base and a wire tube to the outside for foraging bees.
- Images: https://www.artribune.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Mark-Thompson-Live-In-Hive-1976.-Performance.jpg
- Project page: https://www.artribune.com/arti-visive/2017/01/miele-artisti-beuys-huyghe-thompson/

### Ants & colonies

Superorganisms, pheromone trails, swarm intelligence.

#### Empire of the Ants — Tower Five (2024)
- Type: Game · Senses: Collective & networked, Time & scale · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Ant scale made photoreal: leaves, drops and moss become architecture, and one ant directs a superorganism.
- What it is: A photorealistic strategy adventure in which you are ant no. 103,683, exploring the forest floor and commanding a colony through the seasons; based on Bernard Werber's novel.
- How it works: Unreal Engine 5 rendering of real forest scenes at ant scale, combining third-person exploration with real-time strategy.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4FoxT1FtnBI
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/2287330/header.jpg
- Project page: https://www.microids.com/empire-of-the-ants/

#### SimAnt — Maxis (1991)
- Type: Game · Senses: Collective & networked, Smell & taste · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Shown at: Codie Award, Best Simulation Game 1992
- Idea: One ant and the colony at once: the player switches between an individual body and a superorganism, guided by pheromone trails.
- What it is: A simulation in which you control one yellow ant and, through it, a whole colony competing with red ants for a suburban backyard and house.
- How it works: Colony simulation with pheromone trails, castes and recruitment; designed by Will Wright, drawing on Hölldobler and Wilson's The Ants.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FiAGLMpjRIs
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/FiAGLMpjRIs/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SimAnt

#### The World Flag Ant Farm — Yukinori Yanagi (1990)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Collective & networked, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Aperto, Venice Biennale 1993
- Idea: The colony does not perceive flags at all, so its ordinary labour dissolves the symbols humans fight over.
- What it is: National flags made of coloured sand in linked plastic boxes are colonised by ants, whose tunnels carry sand across the borders until the flags blur into one another; the series continues in later works such as ASEAN +3.
- How it works: Coloured sand flags in interconnected acrylic boxes joined by tubes, inhabited by live ant colonies over the exhibition period.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WfAH19potOE
- Images: https://yanagistudio.net/wp-content/uploads/2025/03/18.Eurasia-2001-600x600.jpg
- Project page: https://yanagistudio.net/category/archives/ant-farm/

### Butterflies, spiders & others

Butterflies and metamorphosis, spiders and vibration, flies, mosquitoes and dragonflies.

#### The Spider's Perspective — Barbara Schuler (2026)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Touch & haptics, Hearing & vibration, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Idea: Arthropod conservation through the spider's own senses: air, vibration and sight.
- What it is: A multisensory VR experience in which visitors become a hunting spider exploring an oak tree for prey, sensing airflow from fans and vibrations through haptics.
- How it works: Head-mounted VR with fans for airflow, haptic vibration, spatial sound, walking and jumping via controllers and a rotating chair.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3788851.3815016 (ACM IMX 2026)

#### Controlling Virtual Spiders — Martin Kocur (2025)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: An alien anatomy needs its own control scheme; there is no neutral mapping.
- What it is: A study comparing four ways to drive an eight-legged spider avatar: controller, hands, half-body and full-body control.
- How it works: Head-mounted VR with controller, hand-tracking and body-tracking mappings to spider legs.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3756884.3765989 (ACM VRST 2025)

#### Wings Against the Veil of Light — Jiabao Li (2024)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Body schema & movement · Medium: Performance & participatory, Wearable & sensory device
- Shown at: The Contemporary Austin, Laguna Gloria 2024
- Idea: Butoh empties the human body so a cricket can move in it; the wings give the dancer the cricket's way of making sound.
- What it is: A Butoh performance in which dancers wear cricket wings made from a zipper and a ruler and chirp by rubbing them, telling how artificial light makes male crickets call at the wrong time.
- How it works: Wearable wings built like a cricket's file and scraper (zipper and ruler) are rubbed to stridulate; live music and lighting stage light pollution.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V-doIkBdPkg
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/58688c8a6a496327e937e35b/c3932ec7-6a6b-4671-96ce-bc5808b54847/Jiabao+Li_Cricket_Butoh+0.jpg
- Project page: https://www.jiabaoli.org/wings-against-the-veil-of-light

#### Birdly Insects — SOMNIACS (2022)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision, Touch & haptics · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: NYX Game Awards 2022 (Gold)
- Idea: Shrinking the flyer to insect scale turns an ordinary meadow into a vast and dangerous landscape.
- What it is: A Birdly experience in which you fly as a butterfly through a flower meadow, feeling the wind, hearing your own wings and meeting other animals, including predators.
- How it works: Runs on the Birdly prone motion platform with a VR headset and headwind fan; content developed with Kevuru Games.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EYO9mGG9qTE
- Project page: https://blooloop.com/animals/news/somniacs-birdly-insects/

#### Free the Air: How to hear the universe in a spider/web — Tomás Saraceno (2022)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Touch & haptics, Collective & networked · Medium: Multisensory installation, Spatial audio
- Shown at: The Shed, New York 2022
- Idea: The audience lies in the web's position: sound arrives as vibration through the net, the way a spider reads its world.
- What it is: Visitors lie together on a net suspended inside a vast sphere and listen, and feel through the body, a composition made from the vibrations of a spider and its web.
- How it works: A spherical chamber with a suspended net and low-frequency sound derived from recorded web vibrations, played through a multichannel system.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EG6_8boQQ-w
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/EG6_8boQQ-w/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=EG6_8boQQ-w

#### Micro Monsters with David Attenborough — Alchemy Immersive (2021)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: VR headset, 360° / immersive film
- Shown at: Venice VR Expanded 2021
- Idea: Insect scale as the first step of becoming: the everyday ground turns into a vast, dangerous world.
- What it is: A five-episode VR series that shrinks the viewer to insect size among scorpions, centipedes, aphids, weaver ants and trapdoor spiders, narrated by David Attenborough.
- How it works: Stereoscopic macro live-action combined with CG, delivered on Meta Quest (director Elliot Graves).
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2021/Schede_film/970x647/Venice_VR_Expanded/graves_micro_monsters_with_david_attenborough.jpg?itok=hNtopxHL
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2021/lineup/venice-vr-expanded/micro-monsters-david-attenborough

#### Webbed — Sbug Games (2021)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Silk as an extended body: the web is a tool, a path and a limb at once.
- What it is: A 2D physics platformer in which you are a small peacock spider who spins webs to swing, build bridges and move objects to rescue a partner.
- How it works: Physically simulated silk strands that can be anchored, tensioned and cut.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=xcSo912bT44
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/1390350/header.jpg
- Project page: https://store.steampowered.com/app/1390350/Webbed/

#### Animalia Sum — Bianca Kennedy, The Swan Collective (2020)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, 360° / immersive film
- Shown at: Sundance New Frontier 2020
- Idea: Humour as a way into insect bodies: switching roles between eater and eaten questions how we rank animals.
- What it is: A comic VR mockumentary about a future in which insects are humanity's main protein: the viewer becomes a bug, then a farmer milking bugs, and watches a boxing match between a bug and a whale.
- How it works: Hand-sculpted miniatures captured with photogrammetry, animated with a Perception Neuron suit, delivered as interactive 360° on Oculus Go.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vCmypkilkEY
- Project page: https://voicesofvr.com/901-sundance-animalia-sum-blends-humor-with-a-unique-aesthetic-of-photogrammetry-captured-sculptures/

#### Spider/Web Pavilion 7 — Tomás Saraceno (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: May You Live in Interesting Times, Venice Biennale 2019
- Idea: The web is treated as a sensing instrument and a way of reading the world, shared between spider and human.
- What it is: A pavilion at the Venice Biennale devoted to spiders and their webs, with living webs and oracle readings in which visitors' questions are answered through spider/web divination.
- How it works: Living spiders and webs, arachnomancy cards and oracle sessions, developed with collaborating arachnologists.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QSbzZ-b6eEI
- Images: https://studiotomassaraceno.org/files/002-1920x1280.jpg
- Project page: https://studiotomassaraceno.org/spiderweb-pavilion-7/

#### UUmwelt — Pierre Huyghe (2018)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Collective & networked · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: Serpentine Gallery London 2018–2019
- Idea: The title doubles Uexküll's umwelt: humans, flies and a machine each perceive the same room in their own world, and none of them sees it whole.
- What it is: An exhibition in which thousands of flies bred in the gallery move freely among LED screens showing images generated by a neural network from human brain scans; the flies' movements and the room's conditions keep changing the images.
- How it works: Deep image reconstruction from fMRI data (with Kamitani Lab, Kyoto University), environmental sensors that alter the image stream, and a live population of flies.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=enx-vyWn7UU
- Images: https://d37zoqglehb9o7.cloudfront.net/uploads/2020/04/1269-pierre-serpentine.jpg
- Project page: https://www.serpentinegalleries.org/whats-on/pierre-huyghe-uumwelt/

#### Webs of At-tent(s)ion — Tomás Saraceno (2018)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: ON AIR, Palais de Tokyo Paris 2018
- Idea: A web is a sensing organ outside the body; attending to it means attending to a sense built on tension and vibration rather than sight.
- What it is: Part of the exhibition ON AIR at Palais de Tokyo: in darkened rooms, spot-lit webs built by different spider species hang in space, each a record of how its maker senses and holds the world.
- How it works: Webs spun by living spiders in frames, lit in darkness; part of a studio practice that also records and sonifies web vibrations.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=spmNH9YKSTk
- Images: https://studiotomassaraceno.org/files/02_18FRA_PdT_Press_29_AR-1.jpg
- Project page: https://studiotomassaraceno.org/webs-of-at-tentsion/

#### Micro Giants — Yifu Zhou (2017)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Time & scale, Altered vision · Medium: 360° / immersive film, VR headset
- Shown at: Sundance New Frontier 2018; Kaohsiung VR FILM LAB 2020
- Idea: Becoming insect-sized: grass turns to forest and a water drop to a pool, so the viewer inhabits the scale at which insects live.
- What it is: A six-minute CG VR animation that shrinks the viewer to mosquito size inside a forest floor, where aphids, ladybirds, a spider, a parasitoid wasp and a hummingbird play out a food chain at giant scale.
- How it works: Fully CG 4K stereoscopic 360 animation rendered in a film pipeline from macro-photography references (about 300 8K textures per creature), viewed in a VR headset.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=a13wgYDLOQ4
- Project page: https://www.jiemian.com/article/1901935.html

#### Arachnid Orchestra. Jam Sessions — Tomás Saraceno (2015)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Touch & haptics · Medium: Multisensory installation, Performance & participatory, Spatial audio
- Shown at: NTU Centre for Contemporary Art Singapore 2015
- Idea: A spider lives in a world of vibration; amplifying the web lets humans hear and answer that world.
- What it is: Spiders spin webs in a darkened space; the vibrations of the webs are amplified as sound, and human musicians improvise with the spiders in public jam sessions.
- How it works: Contact microphones and vibration sensors on the webs, amplified and mixed live with human instruments.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hIuNu-dcQX8
- Project page: https://studiotomassaraceno.org/arachnid-orchestra-jam-sessions/

#### The Plan — Krillbite Studio (2013)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Time & scale · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: A life at fly scale: a slow ascent that makes a tiny, short life feel significant.
- What it is: A short, free game in which you fly as a housefly upward through a dark forest toward a light, accompanied by Grieg's In the Hall of the Mountain King.
- How it works: Simple 3D flight controls in a single continuous level scored to classical music.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tWEZ2cbdacc
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/250600/header.jpg
- Project page: http://krillbite.com/theplan

#### The Sound of Light in Trees — David Dunn (2006)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Time & scale · Medium: Spatial audio
- Idea: Listening from inside the tree puts you at beetle scale, in the sound world that decides whether a forest lives.
- What it is: Recordings made inside the trunks of piñon pines in New Mexico, where bark beetles chew, click and signal to each other during a drought-driven outbreak.
- How it works: Custom contact sensors inserted into phloem tissue, recorded and edited with minimal processing; research follow-up with physicist Jim Crutchfield on beetle acoustics.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gH6zUgeKY7o

#### Mister Mosquito — Zoom Inc. (2001)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: The human body as terrain: feeding is stealth, and the host's stress rises with every mistake.
- What it is: A stealth game in which you are a mosquito feeding on members of a Japanese family through the summer without being noticed or swatted.
- How it works: 3D flight around life-size human characters with a stress meter and pressure-sensitive analog control for how hard to suck.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KpaI7rE2AiA
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/KpaI7rE2AiA/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mister_Mosquito

#### Chaos and the Emergent Mind of the Pond — David Dunn (1991)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration · Medium: Spatial audio
- Idea: A pond heard from within sounds like a single thinking organism; listening becomes a way of entering insect time.
- What it is: A composition built from hydrophone recordings of aquatic insects in freshwater ponds, revealing a dense world of clicks, buzzes and rhythms beneath the surface.
- How it works: Hydrophone recordings from ponds in North America and Africa, edited into a continuous piece.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eZ6yDfx2new

#### Flyhead (Environment Transformer) — Haus-Rucker-Co (1968)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: A 1960s headset for compound vision: changing the senses changes the relation to the environment, no computer needed.
- What it is: A green double-bubble helmet whose split prisms break the view into many facets and whose headphones distort surrounding sound, so the wearer perceives the street like an insect.
- How it works: Polyethylene helmet with an eye-level prism array and stereo headphones fitted with acoustic filters.
- Images: https://arthur.io/img/art/jpg/00017344fb78826ea/haus-rucker-co/flyhead-environment-transformer-wien/large/haus-rucker-co--flyhead-environment-transformer-wien.jpg
- Project page: https://www.moma.org/explore/inside_out/2011/03/31/the-mind-expanderflyhead-helmet-a-mind-blowing-perception-transformer/

## Becoming Animal

Other animals: companion and farm animals, wild mammals, reptiles, and works that move between many species.

### Companion & farm animals

Dogs, cats, cows, pigs, chickens: the animals humans live with.

#### Having Dog Ears "for Real" — Omar A. Khan (2026)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Social VR users already wear animal parts; touch is what makes them part of the body.
- What it is: Participants touched virtual dog ears on their heads with vibrotactile gloves, a physical headband, both or neither.
- How it works: Survey of social VR communities, then a 2×2 within-subjects study with vibrotactile gloves (active haptics) and a headband with ear props (passive haptics).
- Paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26364 (arXiv 2026)
- Images: https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/2606.26364/assets/figures/teaser.png

#### Taking the Perspective of Dairy Cows — Erin B. Ryan (2026)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: 360° / immersive film, Screen & web
- Idea: Point-of-view footage lets animals' experiences enter human decisions about their welfare.
- What it is: University students learned about cow–calf separation either from a verbal description or from immersive video filmed from the animals' point of view, then discussed it in focus groups.
- How it works: Video recorded at cow and calf eye height, compared with text; thematic analysis of focus-group transcripts.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1017/awf.2026.10078 (Animal Welfare 2026)

#### If I Could See a Cat (猫が見えたら) — Atsushi Wada (2025)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision, Touch & haptics · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Shown at: Venice Immersive 2025; IFFR 2026; Kaohsiung Film Festival XR Dreamland 2026
- Idea: Grief seen from below, through a cat's body: the viewer comforts a human by being the animal he has lost.
- What it is: An interactive VR animation about a boy, Naoki, grieving his dead cat: the viewer lives through three cats — the phantom of his pet, a stray and a neighbour's cat — and uses hand tracking to move with feline gestures.
- How it works: Hand-drawn style animation built for standalone VR with hand tracking mapped to paw and body gestures; released on Steam in 2026.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gBRs46LtzMQ
- Images: https://images.ludens.com.tw/uploads/images/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/15685-1.png
- Project page: https://www.ludens.com.tw/atsushi-wada-vr-story-if-i-could-see-a-cat-steam-p/

#### Dog Code: Human to Quadruped Embodiment — Rachel McDonnell (2024)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Translate between bodies through a shared vocabulary of movement rather than joint by joint.
- What it is: A deep-learning method that maps human motion to high-quality dog motion through a shared codebook, for real-time VR embodiment.
- How it works: Rule-based retargeting to an intermediate skeleton, then finite scalar quantization to a codebook shared by human and dog motion.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3677388.3696339 (ACM SIGGRAPH Motion, Interaction and Games (MIG) 2024)

#### I Am Cat — New Folder Games (2024)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Idea: Cat scale: shrinking the player to cat height and turning hands into paws makes an ordinary home into terrain.
- What it is: A VR sandbox in which you are a house cat in an old woman's home: you climb furniture, knock objects off shelves and hide from the owner.
- How it works: Hand-driven paw controls with arm-based climbing and jumping on Meta Quest and SteamVR.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WUnEWydv5jE
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/3016840/header.jpg
- Project page: https://newfolderstudio.com/

#### Little Kitty, Big City — Double Dagger Studio (2024)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: The city at kitten scale: street furniture, birds and people are obstacles and playmates.
- What it is: An open-world game in which you are a small black kitten who has fallen from a window and explores a Japanese-inspired city to find the way home.
- How it works: Third-person climbing, napping and object interactions tuned to a kitten's body.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-39kuBXyKWo
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/1177980/header.jpg
- Project page: https://linktr.ee/littlekittybigcity

#### iStrayPaws — Yao Xu (2024)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement, Multisensory · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Empathy for urban animals through living a day of their hardship at their height.
- What it is: A first-person VR journey as a stray cat or dog facing rain, hunger and illness in the city.
- How it works: Head-mounted VR at animal eye height with audio-visual and kinesthetic feedback for weather, hunger and injury.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3641825.3687729 (ACM VRST 2024)

#### A Chicken's-Eye VR Film — Iffa Nurlatifah (2023)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: 360° / immersive film
- Idea: Lowering the camera to a farm animal's eye can change who the viewer identifies with.
- What it is: An immersive VR film shot from the height and viewpoint of a chicken, tested for its effect on empathy towards non-human beings.
- How it works: 360° camera placed at chicken eye level, viewed in a headset, with before-and-after empathy measures.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3632776.3632804 (ARTECH 2023)

#### NeuroDog: Quadruped Embodiment with Neural Networks — Rachel McDonnell (2023)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: A believable animal body needs animal motion, not a human skeleton stretched onto four legs.
- What it is: A system that turns a person's movement into natural dog motion in real time, so that a VR user can walk, sit and play as a dog.
- How it works: Neural network trained on dog motion capture maps tracked human poses to quadruped animation instead of inverse kinematics.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3606936 (Proceedings of the ACM on Computer Graphics and Interactive Techniques (SCA) 2023)

#### Now I Wanna Be a Dog — Rachel McDonnell (2023)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement, Hearing & vibration, Touch & haptics · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Sound and touch can make up for the mismatch between a human body and a dog body.
- What it is: Participants embodied a dog in VR and moved on all fours while hearing paw steps and feeling vibrations under their hands.
- How it works: Head-mounted display with hand and body tracking; footstep audio and audio-tactile actuators under the hands.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/ismar59233.2023.00107 (IEEE ISMAR 2023)

#### Squeeker: The Mouse Coach — Jiabao Li (2023)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: IDFA DocLab
- Idea: Reversing the lab-mouse relationship, the human body is trained on a mouse's daily half-marathon rhythm.
- What it is: An app, running programme and installation in which a pet mouse's smart running wheel sets your training: when the mouse runs you are prompted to run, and matching its distance earns it a treat and you social-media scrolling time.
- How it works: Sensor-equipped running wheel linked to a mobile app that matches human running distance to the mouse's and unlocks scroll distance.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=UvfkYPLJAaw
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/58688c8a6a496327e937e35b/03dabe22-2bce-40aa-9646-b4c1157818b1/Jiabao+Li+Squeeker+Mouse+Coach+1.jpeg
- Project page: https://www.jiabaoli.org/mouse-coach

#### Stray — BlueTwelve Studio (2022)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Shown at: The Game Awards 2022 (Best Independent Game, Best Debut Indie Game)
- Idea: Cat affordances: every ledge, pipe and doormat is read the way a cat would read it, including a dedicated meow button.
- What it is: A third-person adventure in which you are a stray cat separated from its family in a walled cybercity inhabited by robots; you climb, meow, scratch and squeeze through gaps.
- How it works: Context-sensitive jumps between surfaces, cat animation likely based on reference footage of real cats, and a camera kept at cat height.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4uP2MyUL49s
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/1332010/header.jpg
- Project page: https://stray.game

#### Ex Anima — Bartabas, Pierre Zandrowicz (2019)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Body schema & movement, Breath & interoception · Medium: VR headset
- Shown at: Venice Virtual Reality 2019
- Idea: Becoming a horse through breath and rhythm rather than story: an ode to the breathing of animals.
- What it is: Horses emerge from the dark, breathing and dancing on sand; as the piece unfolds the viewer moves with them until they increasingly become the horse.
- How it works: VR film built from Théâtre Zingaro's equestrian performance, shot with stereoscopic capture of the horses (likely volumetric/CG hybrid).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=H4TyjO6UiDI
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2019/Schede_film/970x647/Venice_VR/ex-anima-experience.jpg?itok=pp2569cx
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2019/venice-virtual-reality/ex-anima-experience

#### Sweet Dreams — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Smell & taste, Multisensory · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: Sundance New Frontier 2019; Factory International, Manchester 2024; Ars Electronica 2025
- Idea: A mostly human-centred satire: the chicken appears only as a cartoon mascot, which is the point about how food culture hides the animal on the plate.
- What it is: First a VR dining experience in which visitors ate and drank real food while a virtual feast responded (Sundance 2019), then a 60-minute walk-through show at Factory International (2024) following Chicky Ricky, the chicken mascot of a failing fast-food company, through the food chain.
- How it works: 2019: tracked cutlery, food and drink with sensing of eating and drinking inside a theatrical VR set with live actors; 2024: real-time graphics, a custom VR puppeteering pipeline, motion capture, sculpture and spatial audio across eight rooms.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Pea9dROtxb4
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/SweetDreams_1920x1080.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/MLF_FI_SweetDreams_DSC01435_2k-1.jpg https://filmsandfestivals.britishcouncil.org/storage/project/images/SweetDreams.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/sweet-dreams/

#### Opale — Behnaz Farahi (2017)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
