- How it works: Bio-sonification modules turn micro-fluctuations in the mycelium's conductivity into MIDI for synthesizers, streamed into a VRChat world built with Sara Lisa Vogl (_ROOT_) and the Metaverse Crew.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=J4je5bzyx6s
- Images: https://voicesofvr.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/mycelia-1000x473.jpg https://mycelialive.wordpress.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/mycelia_poster_festival.jpg https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2021/Schede_film/970x647/Venice_VR_Expanded/mycelia.jpg?itok=XGt_dNhR
- Project page: https://mycelialive.wordpress.com/

#### Symbiosis/\Dysbiosis — Tosca Terán (2021)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Hearing & vibration, Collective & networked · Medium: VR headset, Mixed reality, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Goethe-Institut New Nature 2021; FIVARS 2025
- Idea: Touching the fungus is how you enter its world; the forest responds to the organism, not only to you.
- What it is: A mixed reality installation in which you touch living mycelium that is also visible in VR; its biodata changes the sound, birdsong and look of a virtual forest around you.
- How it works: Electrodes on living mycelium feed custom biosensing instruments (with Lorena Salomé) that drive sound and a VR forest built from point clouds of Canadian forests, co-developed with Sara Lisa Vogl and Brendan Lehman.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3uySqCqE-A4
- Images: https://fivars.net/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/Symbiosis-Dysbiosis-Terrarium-landscape-1024x576.png https://fivars.net/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/Symbiosis-Dysbiosis-underground2.jpg
- Project page: https://fivars.net/stories/official-selections-2025/symbiosis-dysbiosis/

#### The Mycorrhizal Rhythm Machine — Tosca Terán (2021)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Collective & networked · Medium: Multisensory installation, Spatial audio
- Shown at: NAISA North 2021
- Idea: The fungus-root partnership is heard as rhythm, a symbiosis you can listen to.
- What it is: An interactive sculpture that turns a grow room into a fungus-and-sprout music generator: biodata from roots of mycorrhizal plants triggers actuators that strum and rattle.
- How it works: Electrodes in the roots of endo- and ectomycorrhizal plants send biodata to microcontrollers driving mechanical actuators.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ez_keTa5-Mg
- Project page: https://www.toscateran.com/mycorrhizal-rhythm-machine

#### Hypha — Natalia Cabrera (2020)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: VR headset
- Shown at: Sundance New Frontier 2020
- Idea: Being a fungus means having a branching body that feeds, connects to roots and repairs soil.
- What it is: A VR story in which you embody the full life cycle of a mushroom: a spore from space finds water, grows underground as hyphae and mycelium, cleans toxic soil and fruits as a mushroom whose cap and gills block your view.
- How it works: Six-DoF VR with hand tracking that turns the arms into stringy branching limbs; developed by Nanai Studio with Sebastian Gonzalez and Juan Ferrer.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/356115943
- Images: https://docubase.mit.edu/wp-content/uploads/2020/07/hypha.jpg https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/654d529f2df68e6aa2c35f5b/51192710-fec7-41a0-8952-03fbfd50760d/Hypha_6.png
- Project page: https://docubase.mit.edu/project/hypha/

#### Sound for Fungi. Homage to Indeterminacy — Theresa Schubert (2020)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Hearing & vibration, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: Futurium Berlin (Mind the Fungi) 2020; Experimenta Life Forms 2021
- Idea: Fungal growth responds to sound and touch, and so does the simulation, making the visitor a force in the mycelium's world.
- What it is: A generative simulation of growing mycelium, based on experiments that exposed fungal hyphae to sound; visitors steer its growth with their hands.
- How it works: Simulation (with Sage Jenson) derived from treating arboreal mushroom mycelium with sound at 80–440 Hz; hand-tracking sensor for interaction.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zzrYc4v5bjE
- Images: https://www.theresaschubert.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/06/ExperimentaLifeForms_TheresaSchubert_Remi_Sound-for-fungi-1.jpg
- Project page: https://www.theresaschubert.com/works/sound-for-fungi/

#### On mycohuman performances: fungi in current artistic research — Regine Rapp (2019)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Collective & networked · Medium: Performance & participatory, Multisensory installation
- Idea: Fungi appear in these works as co-performers rather than material.
- What it is: A survey of artists who perform with fungi, including Saša Spačal's Myconnect and Theresa Schubert's forest walks, framed as mycohuman performance.
- How it works: Art-historical analysis of works shown at Art Laboratory Berlin and elsewhere.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1186/s40694-019-0085-6 (Fungal Biology and Biotechnology 2019)
- Project page: https://fungalbiolbiotech.biomedcentral.com/articles/10.1186/s40694-019-0085-6

#### PLANET ∞ — Momoko Seto (2017)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Time & scale, Altered vision · Medium: 360° / immersive film
- Shown at: Cannes Film Festival 2017 (VR); Locarno Film Festival 2017 (Virtual Reality)
- Idea: A post-human planet seen at the scale of mould: macro time-lapse makes fungi and tadpoles the inhabitants of the world.
- What it is: A 360° organic tale set after human extinction: the viewer, at the scale of tiny organisms, sees fungi and mould growing among giant dried insect bodies until rain floods the planet and giant carnivorous tadpoles appear.
- How it works: Macro and time-lapse photography of real fungi, mould and tadpoles composited into stereoscopic 360° video with 3D audio (mk2 VR).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gKcmHYc4BtA
- Project page: https://mk2films.com/en/film/planet-%E2%88%9E/

#### Myconnect — Saša Spačal (2013)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Hearing & vibration, Breath & interoception · Medium: Multisensory installation, Wearable & sensory device
- Shown at: Art Laboratory Berlin (Nonhuman Networks)
- Idea: A human-fungus-human feedback loop: you feel your own body as modulated by another organism.
- What it is: A visitor lies in a closed wooden capsule wearing a heartbeat sensor, headphones and vibration motors; their heartbeat passes through living mycelium and returns to the body transformed as sound, light and touch.
- How it works: Heart-rate sensor drives a signal through oyster or shiitake mycelium whose changing electrical resistance offsets it in time; output via headphones, lights and vibration motors. With Mirjan Švagelj and Anil Podgornik.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/238714637
- Images: https://www.agapea.si/wp-content/uploads/2015/05/S3D3092-WB2.jpg https://www.agapea.si/wp-content/uploads/2015/05/S3D2572-1024x683.jpg
- Project page: https://www.agapea.si/en/projects/myconnect

#### Infinity Burial Suit — Jae Rhim Lee (2011)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Time & scale · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Shown at: TEDGlobal 2011
- Idea: The last becoming of the body is to be taken up by fungi and returned to soil.
- What it is: A burial suit embroidered with mushroom spores that are trained to decompose the wearer's body and neutralise its toxins after death.
- How it works: Suit with a spore-infused netting; the artist trained mushrooms on her own hair, skin and nails to recognise human tissue.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_7rS_d1fiUc
- Project page: https://coeio.com

### Slime mould

Physarum and other brainless problem-solvers.

#### Symbiosis — Polymorf (2021)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Smell & taste, Multisensory · Medium: VR headset, Wearable & sensory device, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: IDFA DocLab, Amsterdam (world premiere); Eye Filmmuseum, Cinema Ecologica 2021; Holland Festival 2022; PAM CUT, Portland Art Museum 2022–23; SXSW XR Experience Spotlight 2023
- Idea: Inspired by Donna Haraway's Camille Stories: becoming another species is done with the whole body, including taste, smell and skin.
- What it is: A multi-user VR performance set 200 years in the future in which each visitor, wearing a custom haptic suit, inhabits a slime mould, a toad, a genetically altered orchid or a human–anglerfish hybrid, tasting and smelling their world as the characters' stories converge.
- How it works: Individual haptic suits with soft robotics, VR audio and visuals, scent delivery and vegetarian snacks by the chefs of Karpendonkse Hoeve matched to each creature.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-hwpnyWSXb0
- Images: https://polymorf.nl/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/BG-Symbiosis-new-04.jpg https://polymorf.nl/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/creature.jpg
- Project page: https://polymorf.nl/symbiosis/

#### Mushroom 11 — Untame (2015)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Movement by self-erasure: a body with no fixed shape, steered by removing cells rather than moving limbs.
- What it is: A puzzle-platformer in which you control an amorphous green organism by erasing parts of it; the lost cells regrow elsewhere, so it moves by being destroyed.
- How it works: A mouse eraser deletes cells and the organism regrows the same number of cells on its other side; soundtrack by The Future Sound of London.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KVe76XebJcw
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/243160/header.jpg
- Project page: http://mushroom11.com

#### Being Slime Mould — Heather Barnett (2013)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Collective & networked, Body schema & movement · Medium: Performance & participatory
- Shown at: BioDesign, Het Nieuwe Instituut Rotterdam 2013
- Idea: To feel collective intelligence without a brain, humans enact it with their own bodies.
- What it is: A participatory experiment in which a group of strangers tries to behave as the single-celled slime mould Physarum polycephalum: moving as one body, exploring, and deciding without a leader.
- How it works: Rule-based group enactment in a physical space, with food sources and obstacles, devised with Daniel Grushkin.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1201/9781003339540-3 (Slime Mould in Arts and Architecture (2022))
- Images: https://heatherbarnett.co.uk/wp-content/uploads/2015/07/Being-Slime-Mould-Enactment-2013-%C2%A9-film-still-by-Tim-Grabham-2-495x400.jpg https://heatherbarnett.co.uk/wp-content/uploads/2015/07/Being-Sliem-Mould-BOM-1-495x400.jpg
- Project page: https://heatherbarnett.co.uk/work/being-slime-mould/

### Microbes & cells

Bacteria, viruses, cells, microbiomes and molecular worlds.

#### Liminal Lands — Jakob Kudsk Steensen (2021)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Hearing & vibration, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, Screen & web
- Shown at: LUMA Arles 2021
- Idea: Perception is scaled down to the slow, microbial processes of a salt landscape humans cannot normally sense.
- What it is: A multiplayer VR installation built from fieldwork in the salt marshes of the Camargue, moving between micro and macro scales of algae, salt crystals and foam.
- How it works: Visual and sound recordings from the Salin de Giraud rendered in a real-time game-engine world, shown as multiplayer VR and 2D video.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/591022554
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/573604122b8ddea9122c6ee9/1706361881595-EZ08BL6M3S2CREH2J5KP/jks21_liminallands_foam7_web.jpg
- Project page: https://www.jakobsteensen.com/liminal-lands

#### Thrive — Revolutionary Games Studio (2021)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Time & scale · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Being a cell with real biology: metabolism, membranes and organelles are what limit and shape the body.
- What it is: An open-source evolution game that starts in the microbe stage: you are a single cell in a tide pool, absorbing compounds, engulfing prey and redesigning your membrane and organelles between generations.
- How it works: A cell editor with organelles, membrane types and compound balances simulated per patch of an auto-generated planet; free open-source builds, Steam early access from 2021.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LmIwSBvXGQA
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/1779200/5273442273cffbfef2f30c451d9f2cec91e5e3c6/header.jpg
- Project page: https://revolutionarygamesstudio.com/

#### Earthlink — Saša Spačal (2018)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Smell & taste · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Match Gallery, Museum and Galleries of Ljubljana 2018
- Idea: Breathing is an exchange with soil microbes; the work makes that exchange technologically dosed and felt.
- What it is: A system of installations about breathing with the planet; in one of them, Inspiration, visitors inhale the soil 'happiness bacterium' Mycobacterium vaccae in a rhythm set by resuscitator bags.
- How it works: Breathing stations with living soil cultures and resuscitator bags pacing inhalation and exhalation.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/411971525
- Images: https://www.agapea.si/wp-content/uploads/2019/02/Inspirij_II_Miha-Godec.jpg https://www.agapea.si/wp-content/uploads/2019/02/Biomi_II_Miha-Godec.jpg
- Project page: https://www.agapea.si/en/projects/earthlink

#### MICROBIhOME — MICROBIhOME (University of Salford) (2018)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation, VR headset
- Shown at: Manchester Science Festival 2018; Manchester Science Festival 2019; Royal Society Summer Science Exhibition
- Idea: Shrinking the visitor to bacterial scale makes the body an ecosystem you are inside.
- What it is: A mixed-media installation about the human microbiome; in its VR lung, visitors are inhaled into the airways and watch bacteriophages attack bacteria at microbial scale.
- How it works: Mobile VR headsets within an immersive installation built with artist Paul Miller and Reflex Arc from microbiology research at the University of Salford.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/296994011
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/573e23a5c2ea51d838c57506/1549825000876-FJ0VJKELBEMH6SSYIDAD/MBH-pics-7.jpg
- Project page: https://hub.salford.ac.uk/microbihome/

#### nimiia cétiï — Jenna Sutela (2018)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Altered vision · Medium: Screen & web, Spatial audio
- Shown at: Somerset House Studios / Google Arts & Culture 2018
- Idea: The movement of bacteria is treated as a voice that a machine can learn to speak with.
- What it is: An audiovisual work in which a neural network learns from recordings of a channelled 'Martian' language and footage of Bacillus subtilis bacteria, producing a new language spoken between machine, microbe and human.
- How it works: Machine learning trained on 19th-century Martian language audio (Hélène Smith) and microscope footage of Bacillus subtilis nattō.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NaoZV7jPo10
- Images: https://admin.somersethouse.org.uk/images/2VSzwd-SKAaTJvtSbRePm9TYN1U=/956/width-600/JennaSutela_CO4YzSr5JUTwlIFU.jpg
- Project page: https://www.somersethouse.org.uk/whats-on/jenna-sutela-nimiia-cetii

#### Plague Inc. — Ndemic Creations (2012)
- Type: Game · Senses: Collective & networked, Time & scale · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: The world seen from a microbe's point of view is a map of hosts, routes and climates.
- What it is: A strategy game in which you play a pathogen, such as a bacterium, virus or fungus, evolving transmission and symptoms to spread across the world.
- How it works: Epidemiological simulation on mobile and PC; players pick traits from an evolution tree.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pSat_gLDXPc
- Images: https://shared.fastly.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/246620/4c67f0dc09d833b843cf5c3834d95bef246ccd49/header.jpg?t=1779100239
- Project page: https://store.steampowered.com/app/246620/Plague_Inc_Evolved/

#### Osmos — Hemisphere Games (2009)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Time & scale · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Movement costs body: a cell-like being in which every push shrinks the self.
- What it is: A physics game in which you are a floating mote that grows by absorbing smaller motes; to move you must eject part of your own mass.
- How it works: Newtonian physics with mass ejection as propulsion and adjustable time speed.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qGuieN5-6dI
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/29180/header.jpg
- Project page: http://www.hemispheregames.com/osmos

#### flOw — thatgamecompany (2006)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Shown at: The Museum of Modern Art collection (2012)
- Idea: Being a microorganism whose body grows with what it eats, paced by the theory of flow.
- What it is: A game in which you guide an aquatic microorganism that eats other organisms, grows new segments and dives through the layers of a primordial ocean.
- How it works: Made as Jenova Chen's master's thesis at USC as a Flash game, then rebuilt for PlayStation 3 with tilt control.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tTVDSOnPLns
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/tTVDSOnPLns/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: http://thatgamecompany.com/

### Symbiosis & lichen

Lichen, holobionts and life as entanglement.

#### Honey Fungus — Jonah King (2025)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: SXSW XR Experience 2025
- Idea: Intimacy with fungi as a model for relating to ecology: the body is asked to touch, feed and entangle rather than observe.
- What it is: A series of embodied VR experiments set inside a queer, sentient fungal network, mixing AI-generated field-research poetry with actions that cultivate intimacy with ecology.
- How it works: Four-headset VR installation with embodied hand interactions and AI-remixed text (Stevens Institute of Technology, Hybrid Studio).
- Images: https://images.stevens.edu/mviowpldu823/3ShJAmNI9K9uuEqoTm0pA3/6c5d44432a6df69cddf1227ccee796cd/king_1.png
- Project page: https://www.stevens.edu/news/honeyfungus

#### Symbiotica — Natalia Cabrera (2021)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Collective & networked, Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, Screen & web
- Shown at: CPH:DOX 2021; NewImages Festival 2022
- Idea: Symbiosis is learned by being one of the partners, not by watching the whole.
- What it is: A multi-user VR experience in which participants embody different microorganisms and cooperate, from primitive cells onward, to discover what lichen, a collective of many species, has to tell humanity.
- How it works: Online multi-user hub accessible by VR headset, PC, phone or tablet.
- Images: https://xrmust.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/04/XRMust_symbiotica_Poster.jpg
- Project page: https://xrmust.com/all-experiences/symbiotica/

#### Symbiome – Economy of Symbiosis — Saša Spačal (2016)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Breath & interoception · Medium: Multisensory installation, Spatial audio
- Shown at: Museum of Contemporary Art Metelkova (MSUM) Ljubljana 2016
- Idea: Symbiosis is heard as a continuous negotiation, and the visitor's breath becomes part of it.
- What it is: A hydroponic chamber in which red clover and root-nodule bacteria trade carbon and nitrogen; the exchange sets the rhythm of dripping water, whose ripples are turned into sound, and visitors' breath feeds the clover.
- How it works: Indirect measurements of the plant-bacteria exchange control a water drip; ripples are sensed and synthesised in real time through phase modulation. With microbiologist Mirjan Švagelj.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/243696887
- Images: https://www.agapea.si/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Najavna-fotka_I_HD.jpg
- Project page: https://www.agapea.si/en/projects/symbiome-the-economy-of-symbiosis

#### Mycophone_unison — Saša Spačal (2015)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Collective & networked · Medium: Multisensory installation, Spatial audio
- Idea: The body is not one but many; the instrument lets you hear a microbial multitude as a single voice.
- What it is: A living sound instrument in which fungi, bacteria and other microbial communities, like those that make up the human microbiome, generate sound together.
- How it works: Sensors read electrical changes in living microbial cultures and translate them into sound in real time.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/205934235
- Images: https://www.agapea.si/wp-content/uploads/2015/05/mycophone_unison_1_0.jpg
- Project page: https://www.agapea.si/en/projects/mycophone_unison

## Becoming Tree

Plants and forests: trees, flowers and roots, photosynthesis, plant time and forest ecosystems.

### Trees

Growing, breathing and living for centuries as a tree.

#### Of the Oak — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2025)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Breath & interoception, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web, Spatial audio
- Shown at: Royal Botanic Gardens, Kew 2025; Yorkshire Sculpture Park 2025–26; Shapes of Becoming, Jing'an Sculpture Park, Shanghai 2026
- Idea: An oak is presented as a web of more than 2,300 species rather than a single tree, and breath is the audience's way in.
- What it is: A 12-minute interactive video installation that follows Kew's Lucombe oak through four seasons, revealing the fungi, insects, birds and lichens it hosts, with multichannel sound, open-eyed breathing meditations and an online field guide to oak-associated species.
- How it works: Photogrammetry and LiDAR of the Lucombe oak, CT scans of soil samples, ground-penetrating radar of the roots and 24-hour sound recordings made with Kew's Tree Gang, with species drawn from the OakEcol dataset; meditations written by Daisy Lafarge, Merlin Sheldrake, Ella Saltmarshe and Laline Paull.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/1078013028
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2025/04/MLF_Of_the_Oak_BarneySteel_01329-1.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2025/04/0279_An_Ode_to_an_Oak_comp_merged_v013.011411.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2025/04/LUCOMBE-OAK-I_0279_Oak_print_lidar_COMP_NLD_v003_SIDE_05_FINAL.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/of-the-oak/

#### Embodying nature in immersive virtual reality: Are multisensory stimuli vital to affect nature connectedness and pro-environmental behaviour? — Pia Spangenberger (2024)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Multisensory, Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Asks which senses matter when a human is asked to feel like a tree.
- What it is: A follow-up study on embodying a tree in VR that tests whether added multisensory stimuli change nature connectedness and pro-environmental behaviour.
- How it works: Controlled VR experiment comparing visual-only and multisensory tree embodiment conditions.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.compedu.2023.104964 (Computers & Education 2024)
- Project page: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.compedu.2023.104964

#### Peupler — Maya Mouawad, Cyril Laurier (2023)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation, Mixed reality
- Shown at: Venice Immersive 2023
- Idea: The tree as a witness of the city: taking its point of view makes cohabitation a question of who populates space.
- What it is: An interactive installation that lets visitors see and feel the city from the point of view of a tree, and feed that tree through their own presence.
- How it works: Photogrammetric digital tree with generative sound, responding to visitors' movement via sensors (Fisheye Immersive).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=t3No7MRp-MU
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2023/Schede_film/970x647/Ve_Immersive/mouawad-laurier.jpg?itok=xzhsmdLl
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2023/venice-immersive/peupler

#### Becoming nature: effects of embodying a tree in immersive virtual reality on nature relatedness — Pia Spangenberger (2022)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Only VR participants described the experience in the first person as the tree, and only they reflected on their role as humans toward nature.
- What it is: An experiment (N = 28) in which participants embodied a tree through its life, in immersive VR or on a desktop screen, with small branch movements via controllers.
- How it works: HMD vs. desktop between-subjects study measuring nature relatedness, perspective-taking and immersion with mixed methods.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-022-05184-0 (Scientific Reports 2022)
- Images: https://media.springernature.com/full/springer-static/image/art%3A10.1038%2Fs41598-022-05184-0/MediaObjects/41598_2022_5184_Fig1_HTML.png
- Project page: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-022-05184-0

#### Sanctuary of the Unseen Forest — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2022)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale, Breath & interoception · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: Our Time on Earth, Barbican, London 2022; TED 2023; Outcrop, 180 The Strand, London 2023; Lux: Poetic Resolution, Seoul 2023; Fungi – In Art and Science, Nobel Prize Museum, Stockholm 2023–24; Our Time on Earth, Musée de la civilisation, Québec 2023–24; Works of Nature, ACMI, Melbourne 2023–24; Our Time on Earth, Peabody Essex Museum 2024; Museum of the Amazons, Belém 2025
- Idea: A response to 'plant blindness': the tree's inner flows are made visible so that it reads as a living, breathing being.
- What it is: A large-scale video installation built around a giant Ceiba pentandra (kapok) from the Colombian Amazon, peeling back its layers to show water, carbon and nutrients pulsing from crown to roots and into the mycorrhizal network, in time with the viewer's heartbeat.
- How it works: LiDAR, photogrammetry, ecological surveys and ambisonic field recordings of a Ceiba pentandra near Leticia, Colombia (2020), rendered as single-channel 4K video with 10.1-channel audio.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MWhyhUVQ4lI
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/MLF_Barbican-2-1.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/0214_Barbican_Sanctuary_ceiba_comp_v039.5251.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/MLF_Sanctuary_Strand_03.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/sanctuary-of-the-unseen-forest/

#### One Tree ID – How To Become A Tree For Another Tree — Agnes Meyer-Brandis (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Smell & taste, Collective & networked · Medium: Multisensory installation, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: Ars Electronica Festival 2019; KONTEJNER Zagreb; Kersnikova Ljubljana
- Idea: Trees talk in scent; wearing a tree's scent lets a human join, speculatively, in that chemical conversation.
- What it is: The volatile organic compounds of one specific tree are collected and made into a perfume; visitors wear it next to the tree and so carry its chemical identity.
- How it works: Headspace sampling of the tree's VOCs, analysed with atmospheric chemists and recomposed by a perfumer into a wearable scent.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/328989340
- Images: https://ars.electronica.art/outofthebox/files/2019/08/One-Tree-ID-How-To-Becoma-A-Tree-For-Another-Tree-Agnes-Brandis.jpg http://onetreeid.ffur.de/wp-content/uploads/2020/11/Start_1440x690_OneTreeID_5.jpg
- Project page: http://onetreeid.ffur.de/

#### We Live in an Ocean of Air — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2018)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Multisensory, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Salon 009, Saatchi Gallery, London 2018–19; Observations on Being, Coventry 2021; Phi Centre, Montreal 2021–22; ArtScience Museum, Singapore 2022; Plásmata: Bodies, Dreams, and Data, Onassis Foundation, Athens 2022; Works of Nature, ACMI, Melbourne 2023–24; Breathe | Mauri Ora, Te Papa, Wellington 2025–26
- Idea: Breath is shared with trees: each exhale feeds the sequoia and each inhale comes from it, so the boundary between body and plant becomes a loop.
- What it is: A multi-user VR installation in which visitors stand beneath a giant sequoia and see their own exhaled breath as particles that flow into the tree, while its oxygen flows back into them. Later editions present it as a large video installation.
- How it works: Untethered VR with backpack PCs, hand tracking, breath sensors and heart-rate monitors that turn each visitor's breathing and pulse into real-time particles; binaural sound, scent diffusion and wind machines; sequoia data from the Treehugger LiDAR scans.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/303589503
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/Copy-of-OceanOfAir_Saatchi_-12-of-34_sml.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/Copy-of-OceanOfAir_Saatchi_-26-of-34.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2024/02/We-Live-in-an-Ocean-of-Air-by-Marshmallow-Laser-Feast-Works-of-Nature-ACMI-2023-image-by-Eugene-Hyland_13295.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/we-live-in-an-ocean-of-air/

#### Tree — Milica Zec, Winslow Porter (2017)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics, Smell & taste · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Sundance New Frontier 2017; Tribeca Film Festival 2017; World Economic Forum Davos; TED 2017
- Idea: Becoming a tree means feeling growth, rootedness and helplessness in your own body, not watching deforestation from outside.
- What it is: A room-scale VR experience in which you grow from a seed into a giant Amazon rainforest tree, your arms becoming branches, until the forest around you burns.
- How it works: HTC Vive headset with a haptic vest, subwoofer floor, fans, heat and scents of earth and smoke; participants first plant a real seed.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ERffRXjTAqM
- Images: https://docubase.mit.edu/wp-content/uploads/2018/11/Tree-02-Still.jpg https://voicesofvr.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/01/tree-vr-1102x473.jpg https://s3-us-west-2.amazonaws.com/treeofficial/img/desktop-tree-backup.png
- Project page: https://www.treeofficial.com

#### TreeSense — Xin Liu, Yedan Qian (2016)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement, Time & scale · Medium: VR headset, Wearable & sensory device
- Shown at: MIT Media Lab exhibition 2016; Core77 Design Awards 2017 (Interaction, Student Runner-up)
- Idea: Tactile illusions on the forearm are enough to make a branch feel like part of your own body.
- What it is: A sensory VR system that turns you into a tree from seedling to full size to its felling; electrical muscle stimulation lets you feel branches growing and a bird landing on your arm.
- How it works: VR headset with Leap Motion hand tracking and an EMS unit with electrodes on the forearms, timed to growth, wind, worms and birds.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Kbx29NMgbOM
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/5ad5566e96e76f1dbbd692b4/1526317496399-ZEEQEAU6E2P8234HYDD4/large.jpg https://s3files.core77.com/blog/images/619606_75884_64289_roLeAXei2.jpg
- Project page: https://slowimmediate.com/treesense

#### Treehugger: Wawona — Marshmallow Laser Feast, Natan Sinigaglia (2016)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Touch & haptics, Smell & taste · Medium: Mixed reality, VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Cinekid Festival 2016; Southbank Centre, London 2016; STRP Biennale 2017; Tribeca Film Festival Storyscapes Award 2017; Migrations Festival, Cardiff 2017; Future of Storytelling 2017; Phi Centre, Montreal 2018; VR Arles Festival Best VR Film 2018; OMM, Eskişehir 2019; Kaohsiung Film Festival 2019; Science Friction, CCCB Barcelona 2021; Frankfurter Kunstverein 2021; Breathing with Trees, Tai Kwun, Hong Kong 2022
- Idea: Becoming a 3,000-year-old tree means slowing down to its time and feeling its sap move, and the hug is the interface.
- What it is: A mixed-reality installation built around a large tactile sculpture of a giant sequoia: visitors hug the trunk, put their head into a knot wearing a headset, and follow water rising from roots to canopy. The longer they hug, the deeper they drift into accelerated 'tree time'.
- How it works: LiDAR and white-light scans of the Wawona sequoia in Sequoia National Park, made with the Natural History Museum and University of Salford, drive a real-time particle simulation of water and CO2 flow; a tactile sculpture, scent, haptics and a binaural soundscape of biosignals by Mileece I'Anson complete it.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/195539105
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2024/08/5.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2024/08/TreeHuggers.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2024/08/Treehugger_Wawona_BTS046.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/treehugger-wawona/

#### ListenTree — Edwina Portocarrero, Gershon Dublon (2015)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Touch & haptics · Medium: Multisensory installation, Spatial audio
- Shown at: CHI 2015
- Idea: Listening through the wood of a tree puts the listener in bodily contact with it; the tree becomes the medium.
- What it is: Living trees fitted with bone-conduction transducers become near-silent speakers: a passer-by hears the voices inside a tree only when she embraces or leans on it.
- How it works: Bone-conduction transducers attached to the tree (likely at the root flare) vibrate the wood; the sound is felt as much as heard, and content can be live environmental audio.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2702613.2725437 (CHI EA 2015)
- Video: https://vimeo.com/125915832
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/5ad5566e96e76f1dbbd692b4/1526257603302-K52NIYW4XD2EGWO13ITZ/IMG_3569-2-edited.jpg
- Project page: https://slowimmediate.com/listentree

#### trees: Pinus sylvestris — Marcus Maeder (2015)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation, Spatial audio
- Shown at: COP21 Paris 2015
- Idea: A thirsty tree makes sounds humans cannot hear; amplified, they let us listen to drought from inside the tree.
- What it is: An installation that plays the acoustic emissions of a Scots pine in the Swiss Alps together with sonified measurements of its sap flow, trunk diameter and soil moisture.
- How it works: Contact and ultrasonic sensors on the trunk plus ecophysiological sensors, from the SNSF research project 'trees: Rendering Ecophysiological Processes Audible'.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0KfrcUQdpr4
- Project page: https://www.researchcatalogue.net/view/215961/215962

#### Forest Symphony — Ryuichi Sakamoto, YCAM InterLab (2013)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Time & scale, Collective & networked · Medium: Spatial audio, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: YCAM 10th Anniversary, Yamaguchi 2013
- Idea: Hearing trees as live performers: slow electrical rhythms of wood become a music humans can follow.
- What it is: Sensors on 24 trees in Japan and abroad record their bioelectric potential, which drives an ever-changing composition by Ryuichi Sakamoto, played back in galleries and gardens so that visitors listen to forests performing.
- How it works: Custom bioelectric sensing devices built by YCAM InterLab stream data over the internet to a generative score.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1-jvZTqvI8E
- Images: https://special.ycam.jp/forestsymphony/images/tree_color.jpg
- Project page: https://special.ycam.jp/interlab/en/projects/forestsymphony.html

#### Years — Bartholomäus Traubeck (2011)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Multisensory installation, Spatial audio
- Idea: A tree's lifetime of growth becomes audible as a piece of music, one ring per year.
- What it is: A turntable that plays slices of tree trunk: a camera reads the year rings and a generative system turns their thickness and texture into piano music.
- How it works: Modified turntable with a camera and vvvv software mapping ring strength, thickness and growth rate to a harmonic scale.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/30501143
- Images: https://traubeck.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/11/years_2-640x433.jpg
- Project page: https://traubeck.com/works/years

#### Tree Listening — Alex Metcalf (2007)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation, Spatial audio
- Idea: The tree's own drinking becomes audible to a human pressed close to it.
- What it is: A listening installation on a living tree: visitors put on headphones connected to the trunk and branches and hear the popping and crackling of water moving inside the tree.
- How it works: Sensitive contact microphones on branches and trunk amplify cavitation and water transport and route it to headphones.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=u07Z7BnMenY
- Images: https://treelistening.co.uk/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/sound-of-trees.png
- Project page: https://treelistening.co.uk/

#### Biopresence — BCL (Shiho Fukuhara & Georg Tremmel) (2004)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: ITV Morning News 2004
- Idea: Continuing as a tree: a human life carried on at a tree's pace and lifespan.
- What it is: A proposed service that writes a person's DNA into the DNA of a tree, creating 'Human DNA Trees' as living memorials or transgenic tombstones.
- How it works: Joe Davis's DNA Manifold method encodes human DNA in silent base-triplet mutations of a tree genome, so the tree's genes are not altered.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=p4fiha3Qvno
- Images: https://www.biopresence.com/img/human-DNA-trees.jpg
- Project page: https://www.biopresence.com/description.html

### Plants & flowers

Plants, flowers, roots and seeds; plant sensing and signalling.

#### Green Rhythms — Yuting Xue, Elke Reinhuber (2026)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Breath & interoception, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Idea: Breathing with plants: visitors enter the plant's slow day-night timing through a shared exchange of light and air.
- What it is: An interactive installation that translates plants' circadian rhythm into light and air, with the carbon dioxide exhaled by visitors feeding a shared metabolic cycle.
- How it works: Environmental light and visitors' exhaled CO2 are sensed and used to stage photosynthesis and nocturnal gas exchange as light and airflow.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3731459.3779145 (TEI 2026)

#### Bend to — Yuting Xue (2025)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Phototropism becomes a proprioceptive sense the participant shares with the plant.
- What it is: An immersive work set in a future of strong UV radiation where participants track and anticipate how plants bend toward and away from light, using their own hand and body position.
- How it works: 3D scans and time-lapse photography of real plants' light responses played back in VR with hand tracking.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3698061.3726945 (C&C 2025)
- Project page: https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3698061.3726945

#### Plant-Centric Metaverse: A Biocentric-Creation Framework for Ecological Art and Digital Symbiosis — Ze Gao (2025)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Collective & networked · Medium: VR headset, Screen & web
- Idea: Moves digital ecological art from human-centred narratives toward plant agency and plant-algorithm co-creation.
- What it is: Proposes the Biocentric-Creation Transformation Ideology framework and tests it on bio-art, NFT and VR ecosystem case studies from 2013 to 2023.
- How it works: Framework building with multimodal case-study analysis.
- Paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.04391 (arXiv 2025)
- Project page: https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.04391

#### The Great Escape — Joren Vandenbroucke (2025)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset
- Shown at: Venice Immersive 2025
- Idea: Being rooted: embodying a potted plant turns immobility and a windowsill view into the whole of life.
- What it is: An interactive VR comedy in which the viewer is the third of three bored geraniums on a lonely man's windowsill, rooted in place while they plot to see the world.
- How it works: Animated interactive VR with the participant fixed in the pot's position; interaction through gaze and limited hand gestures.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jH5bGRsqn4k
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2025/Schede_film/970x647/Ve_Immersive/the_great_escape.jpg?itok=2-fTzReL
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2025/venice-immersive/great-escape

#### Encountering Human-Plant Relations (Plant Radio, Plant Sensorium) — Lone Koefoed Hansen (2024)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Multisensory, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Idea: Design can tune human attention to plants rather than simulate being one.
- What it is: A study of two design experiments, Plant Radio and Plant Sensorium, and the kinds of sensibility toward plants that arose when people interacted with them.
- How it works: Interviews and observations around two interactive plant installations.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3643834.3661586 (DIS 2024)
- Project page: https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3643834.3661586

#### Forest Bathing: Lupuna — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2024)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Heat & infrared, Multisensory · Medium: Multisensory installation, Dome, CAVE & projection
- Shown at: Immersive Sky, Badewelt Euskirchen (permanent, 2024–)
- Idea: Plant time is sped up to human attention: a day of epiphytes, lianas and a night-blooming flower pollinated by bats and moths unfolds in minutes.
- What it is: A permanent two-room installation in a German spa: visitors step through a waterfall into a simulated tropical downpour, then lie on heated lily-pad loungers beneath a 4.5 m projection that compresses 24 hours in the canopy of a Lupuna (kapok) tree into five minutes, including the night bloom of a Queen of the Night flower.
- How it works: Rain machines, spatial audio and lighting in the first room; temperature-controlled loungers and a three-level magnification (tree, branch, flower) of footage and scans from Leticia, Colombia, in the second.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fxksr0D1QhI
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2024/03/A7408288.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2024/04/Forest-Bathing-Lupuna-3.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/forest-bathing-lupuna/

#### Kingdom of Plants with David Attenborough — Alchemy Immersive (2022)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Time & scale, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, 360° / immersive film
- Shown at: Venice Immersive 2022
- Idea: Plant time made watchable: time-lapse at plant scale turns slow growth into drama you are inside.
- What it is: A three-part immersive series that places the viewer at the scale and pace of plants: sitting inside rare blooms, watching a sundew hunt and being consumed by fungi.
- How it works: Stereoscopic time-lapse and macro photography from the Royal Botanic Gardens, Kew, released on Meta Quest (director Iona McEwan).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vwDrTZs41SE
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2022/Schede_film/970x647/Venice_Immersive/mcewan_.jpg?itok=zPmXq-PX
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2022/venice-immersive/kingdom-plants-david-attenborough

#### Patterns and Opportunities for the Design of Human-Plant Interaction — Michelle Chang (2022)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Touch & haptics, Multisensory · Medium: Multisensory installation, Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Plants are sensors and actuators in their own right; the survey maps how designs couple their senses with ours.
- What it is: A survey of human-plant interaction projects across HCI, art, architecture and bioengineering, organised by system architecture, plant I/O coupling and scale.
- How it works: Systematic review and design-space analysis.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3532106.3533555 (DIS 2022)
- Project page: https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3532106.3533555

#### Queer Ecology — Institute of Digital Fashion, Brigitte Baptiste (2022)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Collective & networked · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: Our Time on Earth, Barbican 2022
- Idea: 'There is nothing more queer than nature': your mirrored body becomes a plant being that grows, decays and merges beyond binary identity.
- What it is: An interactive body-mapped installation in which visitors face two celestial beings made of twigs and plant growth that move with their bodies; the figures merge with the person next to them, grow, die and dissolve into pollen or star dust.
- How it works: Real-time body tracking (with Target3D) mapped onto generative 3D plant-bodies on a large screen, with growth and decay cycles.
- Images: https://cms.showstudio.com/images/HNQBYufe-PrUaVHJzif27CRwecg=/478522/width-1440/IoDF_Queer_Ecology_at_Barbican_Our_Time_on_Earth_Exhibition___Still_1.jpg https://cms.showstudio.com/images/ws4nDndy7DiRXRN3_Q6-n9euN8s=/478523/width-1440/86AFB5F8-87BE-4F2C-B39A-CE6AB8A498D3_1_201_a.jpeg
- Project page: https://www.showstudio.com/news/institute-of-digital-fashion-imagine-a-planet-beyond-the-binary

#### Helpless — Tae Yeun Kim (2021)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Shown at: BIFAN Beyond Reality 2022 (Beyond Science)
- Idea: Being a plant means being unable to flee: the work asks the visitor to take the position of a rooted body that is hurt.
- What it is: An interactive VR work that places the visitor in a virtual space to experience how plants respond to pain and damage.
- How it works: Interactive VR in a headset made with PPPLab and VR Crew; plant stress responses are likely visualised as the visitor's own sensations.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=S0WSsZotfcU
- Project page: https://www.screendaily.com/features/bifans-xr-showcase-beyond-reality-reflects-post-pandemic-changes/5172480.article

#### Messages to a Post Human Earth — Anagram (2021)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Multisensory · Medium: AR, Spatial audio, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: Orleans House Gallery, London
- Idea: Plants as the hearing, sensing witnesses of a post-human Earth; the two partners' synchronised actions become a choreography for each other and for the plants.
- What it is: A two-person audio and AR journey through a garden or forest that asks what plants, rather than people, would witness after humans are gone, drawing on Monica Gagliano's plant-memory research and a Stanisław Lem essay.
- How it works: Location-based binaural narration and AR overlays on a handheld device with physical props, designed for two synchronised participants.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=w4mP6QDA7Ak
- Images: https://weareanagram.co.uk/wp-content/uploads/2023/12/mtaphe.png
- Project page: https://weareanagram.co.uk/project/messages-to-a-post-human-earth/

#### VR Plant Journey — Breakpoint One (2021)
- Type: Game · Senses: Time & scale, Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Idea: Plant physiology becomes a set of tasks you perform from inside the plant's organs.
- What it is: A VR game set inside a canola plant: in three chapters (root, leaf, seed) you regulate nutrients, feed chloroplasts with CO2 and water, and grow the plant to flowering.
- How it works: Room-scale VR game for PC VR and Meta Quest, developed with plant researchers at IPK Gatersleben.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HUmYauXEclM
- Images: https://shared.fastly.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/1487650/header.jpg?t=1628582017
- Project page: https://breakpoint.one/vr-plant-journey/

#### PL'AI — Špela Petrič (2020)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Touch & haptics · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Kersnikova Institute Ljubljana 2020
- Idea: Play is proposed as a shared condition of plants and machines, at plant speed.
- What it is: Over several months, cress seedlings and an AI robot whose whole perceptual world is those plants play with each other, each shaping the other's behaviour and form.
- How it works: Robot arm with camera and machine-learning model interacting with growing plants; documented as time-lapse.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/561021288
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/5aeca48a506fbe863b23a8b6/1621255859914-VEJU1B7LODW2FEE7YDOR/KERSNIKOVA_Spela_Petric_PLAY_20201206_HanaJosic-48.jpg
- Project page: https://www.spelapetric.org/plai/

#### PlantWave — Data Garden (2019)
- Type: Product · Senses: Hearing & vibration · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, Spatial audio
- Idea: Plant music as an everyday way of tuning in to a plant's physiology, at home or outdoors.
- What it is: A consumer device that clips electrodes to a plant's leaves and turns its changing electrical signals into music in real time through a phone app.
- How it works: Electrodes measure variations in the plant's conductivity; the signal is graphed as a wave and mapped to pitches and instruments; successor to the MIDI Sprout (2015).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=j_EyNEbdELI
- Images: http://plantwave.com/cdn/shop/files/plantwave.png?v=1678040631
- Project page: https://plantwave.com/

#### Institute for Inconspicuous Languages: Reading Lips — Špela Petrič (2018)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Kapelica Gallery / Kersnikova 2018; European ARTificial Intelligence Lab
- Idea: A plant's 'lips' (stomata) are treated as a language; conversing with it takes years at the plant's pace.
- What it is: An attempt to hold a conversation with a ficus tree: computer vision reads the opening and closing of the stomata on its leaves, and the human answers with a code of light.
- How it works: Microscope camera and computer-vision model tracking stomatal aperture on Ficus benjamina leaves, paired with controllable light signals.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/457493103
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/5aeca48a506fbe863b23a8b6/1600016130132-1NOFOTHIQXG2OIOKVMUM/190418+E%CC%82pela+Petriu%CC%88+-+Nociceptor_branje+ustnic_foto.miha.fras_030.JPG
- Project page: https://www.spelapetric.org/institute-for-inconspicuous-languages/

#### Confronting Vegetal Otherness: Strange Encounters — Špela Petrič (2017)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: Performance & participatory, Multisensory installation
- Idea: Plant subjectivity is approached through human affect in specific encounters, not by claiming to know the plant.
- What it is: The second piece of the series: a set of staged encounters that test the artist's own affective responses to plants as different as algae, grass and birch.
- How it works: Live performative experiments with plants documented on video during a two-week exhibition.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/198560496
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/5aeca48a506fbe863b23a8b6/1526322878983-UO0158JOGJKC9A2GFXYI/15877652_10154849002759603_1268027415_o.jpg
- Project page: https://www.spelapetric.org/strange-encounters/

#### Pando Endo — Jakob Kudsk Steensen (2017)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Idea: A clonal root system (named after the aspen colony Pando) is shown as an agent that seeks light and breaks containment.
- What it is: A virtual root organism, built from photos of aspen bark, moss and roots, that grows through glass cabinets toward light while four virtual drones inspect it.
- How it works: Real-time procedural simulation of textures and tentacle-like roots programmed to move toward light sources.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/242398982
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/573604122b8ddea9122c6ee9/1519256845242-RUJXBP50XRS8WQQKCQCH/HighresScreenshot00000.jpg
- Project page: https://www.jakobsteensen.com/pando-endo-1

#### Confronting Vegetal Otherness: Phytoteratology — Špela Petrič (2016)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: Prix Ars Electronica 2016 (Award of Distinction, Hybrid Art)
- Idea: Kinship between human and plant is made molecular: the artist's hormones shape a plant body.
- What it is: Plant embryos of thale cress are grown in an artificial womb from tissue fed with steroid hormones extracted from the artist's urine, producing human-plant 'monsters'.
- How it works: Somatic embryogenesis in sterile gel medium, supplemented with hormones isolated from the artist's urine.
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/5aeca48a506fbe863b23a8b6/1534189733741-1EUWR4JLBX43M635NE6J/MG_6180.jpg
- Project page: https://www.spelapetric.org/phytoteratology/

#### Pteridophilia — Zheng Bo (2016)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: Screen & web, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: Manifesta 12, Palermo 2018; 11th Taipei Biennial 2018; Liverpool Biennial 2021
- Idea: Eco-queer kinship: the human body gives up its distance and meets the plant as a partner of equal sensitivity.
- What it is: An ongoing video series in which young queer men in a Taiwanese forest enter intimate, bodily relations with ferns, learning from the plants through touch rather than words.
- How it works: 4K video shot with non-professional performers in forests in Taiwan; sound heard on headphones in the installation.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=OpjQokRE9bs
- Images: http://zhengbo.org/2016_PP1/ZHENG_2016_PP1_01.jpg http://zhengbo.org/2016_PP1/ZHENG_2016_PP1_03.jpg
- Project page: http://zhengbo.org/2019_PP4.html

#### Somatic Drifts — Cat Jones (2016)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement, Smell & taste · Medium: Performance & participatory, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: PICA Radical Ecologies, Perth 2016; The Art of Pain, Adelaide 2015
- Idea: Trans-species empathy through illusion: the felt boundary of the body is loosened until it drifts toward plant and animal bodies.
- What it is: A one-to-one live work in which the artist uses touch, sound and body illusions to guide a participant into feeling the body of another entity, followed by an accumulating video installation of the participants' 'donated' bodies.
- How it works: Visuo-tactile body illusions (in the manner of the rubber-hand illusion), binaural sound by Melissa Hunt, video and a limited-edition scent.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/299341088
- Images: https://catjones.net/wp-content/uploads/2015/06/somatic-drifts-live-remix-lge2-copy1.jpg
- Project page: https://catjones.net/2016/08/11/somatic-drifts/

#### Confronting Vegetal Otherness: Skotopoiesis — Špela Petrič (2015)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Time & scale, Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: Performance & participatory
- Shown at: Trust Me, I'm an Artist (Kapelica Gallery) 2015; Prix Ars Electronica 2016 (Award of Distinction, Hybrid Art, series)
- Idea: Communication with a plant happens through light and its absence, on the plant's timescale rather than ours.
- What it is: A durational performance in which the artist stands facing germinating cress for hours, her shadow blanching the seedlings into a pale imprint of her body.
- How it works: Light projection onto cress; the artist's shadow triggers etiolation via phytochrome light sensors, while she herself shrinks slightly from standing.
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/5aeca48a506fbe863b23a8b6/1525552300442-FEP7QGQK17Y9ULKP8HCH/scotopoiesis_press_04.jpg
- Project page: https://www.spelapetric.org/scotopoiesis/

#### Floating Flower Garden: Flowers and I are of the Same Root, the Garden and I are One — teamLab (2015)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Smell & taste, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: teamLab Planets TOKYO
- Idea: The title promises oneness with the garden; in practice the plants respond to your presence, which is closer to being noticed by them than to becoming them.
- What it is: A room packed with more than 13,000 living orchids that rise away from visitors as they approach, carving an open dome around each person; when two people meet, their spaces join into one.
- How it works: Suspended orchids on motorised lines driven by visitor tracking; the scent changes over the day as the flowers do.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=atWwHZHGkLE
- Images: https://teamlab-site.imagewave.pictures/b5EBo9Uo-OK6SM09ZTkEZQ/89JNH3JuCgHZcGbACeD2vU/width=1200,format=jpeg
- Project page: https://www.teamlab.art/w/ffgarden/

#### PSX Consultancy (Plant Sex Consultancy) — Špela Petrič (2014)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: BIO50, Museum of Architecture and Design, Ljubljana 2014
- Idea: Human-centred design is turned on plants: the designer has to 'feel into' a client with no voice and no first-person report.
- What it is: A speculative design consultancy that treats plants as clients and designs reproductive aids for them, objects that hover between medical devices and erotic hardware.
- How it works: Biofiction prototypes based on pollination biology, presented as a consultancy with objects and documentation.
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/5aeca48a506fbe863b23a8b6/1534191081939-DQXHKQECKO7IZYFVDMP4/IMG_2824.jpg https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/5aeca48a506fbe863b23a8b6/1534191167722-257XI6LQZSXY2K4OZ9T6/IMG_2732.jpg
- Project page: https://www.spelapetric.org/plant-sex-consultancy/

#### Humalga: Towards the Human Spore — Špela Petrič (2013)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Idea: Imagines humans surviving collapse by becoming, every other generation, a simple photosynthetic organism.
- What it is: A speculative art-research project, with Robertina Šebjanič, proposing a genetically hybridised human-alga species that alternates between human and algal generations.
- How it works: Speculative biotech proposal presented through laboratory props, diagrams and texts.
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/5aeca48a506fbe863b23a8b6/1534193649598-TEPJUL8Q67UC8QHP04UM/humalga-03.jpg
- Project page: https://www.spelapetric.org/humalga/

#### Botanicula — Amanita Design (2012)
- Type: Game · Senses: Altered vision, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: The tree is a whole world seen at the scale of its seeds, leaves and fungi.
- What it is: A point-and-click adventure played as five small plant creatures (a seed, a twig, a mushroom and others) who journey through a giant tree to save its last seed from parasites.
- How it works: Hand-drawn 2D adventure game with a soundtrack by DVA; PC, Mac and mobile.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=UxeaS4Pq4EY
- Images: https://shared.fastly.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/207690/header.jpg
- Project page: https://amanita-design.net/games/botanicula.html

#### Natural History of the Enigma (Edunia) — Eduardo Kac (2009)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Weisman Art Museum 2009; Prix Ars Electronica 2009 Golden Nica (Hybrid Art)
- Idea: The artist becomes partly plant; the line between human and flower is drawn in a single gene.
- What it is: A 'plantimal': a genetically engineered petunia that expresses the artist's own DNA in the red veins of its petals.
- How it works: An immunoglobulin gene sequence isolated from the artist's blood was inserted into petunia and expressed only in the flower veins.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dJmsSmM_9ns
- Images: https://www.ekac.org/kac.nat.hist.enigma.01.jpg
- Project page: https://www.ekac.org/nat.hist.enig.html

#### Breathing — Guto Nóbrega (2008)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Touch & haptics · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: FILE Festival
- Idea: Breath becomes the shared channel between human and plant: the visitor's exhalation is felt by the leaf, and the plant answers in a body it does not normally have.
- What it is: A hybrid creature made of a living pothos plant and a robotic body of servos, fibre optics and LEDs; visitors interact with it by breathing, and the plant's electrical response drives the creature's movement, light and sound.
- How it works: A galvanic skin response circuit adapted to measure electrical resistance in the leaves of an Epipremnum plant feeds an Arduino that controls servos, fibre optics and LEDs.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/9860198
- Images: https://payload.cargocollective.com/1/3/114170/1619978/_MG_2827_o.jpg
- Project page: https://cargocollective.com/gutonobrega/Breathing

#### Akousmaflore — Scenocosme (2007)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Idea: Plants are sensitive to us all the time; the work makes that sensitivity audible.
- What it is: A small garden of living plants that sing when touched or approached; each plant has its own voice and responds to the electrical charge of a visitor's body.
- How it works: Capacitive sensing through the plants' own tissue triggers individual sound compositions.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1hae2Fwrqn8
- Project page: http://www.scenocosme.com/akousmaflore_en.htm

#### Teleporting an Unknown State — Eduardo Kac (1996)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Collective & networked, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Idea: The participant does not look at a plant but serves it as a light source, living for weeks at the pace of photosynthesis.
- What it is: In a dark room a single seed sits in a bed of earth; the only light it gets is video light sent over the internet by remote participants, so the plant grows only if people around the world keep 'being its sun'.
- How it works: A projector above the soil shows live videoconference streams of sky images from several countries, used only as photons for the seedling.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sD3rbN_9D2E
- Images: https://www.ekac.org/handsky.gif
- Project page: https://www.ekac.org/teleporting.html

#### Trans Plant — Christa Sommerer, Laurent Mignonneau (1995)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation, Dome, CAVE & projection
- Shown at: Tokyo Metropolitan Museum of Photography 1995
- Idea: The visitor's body becomes a gardener and a garden: stillness, the tempo of plants, is what makes a forest.
- What it is: Walking into a half-round room, visitors see their image on screen as grass and trees grow wherever they step; standing still makes plants grow taller, and body size and speed change what grows.
- How it works: 3D video key extracts the visitor's body and position; a growth algorithm renders plants around it on a large projection.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gduXrdsQG-w

#### Interactive Plant Growing — Christa Sommerer, Laurent Mignonneau (1992)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: ZKM Karlsruhe collection
- Idea: The living plant is the interface: human touch is felt by a plant and translated into plant growth.
- What it is: Visitors touch or approach five real potted plants, and each touch makes a different species of virtual plant grow, twist or stop on a projection screen in real time.
- How it works: Electrical potential between the visitor's hand and each plant is measured and drives an algorithmic plant-growth program.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JXX7JNFD2X8

### Forests & ecosystems

Forests, meadows and whole ecosystems as the one you become.

#### Boreal Dreams — Jakob Kudsk Steensen (2025)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web, Spatial audio
- Idea: The world's largest forest is presented as a regulator of light, heat and rest that humans share.
- What it is: A live simulation and online artwork that travels the boreal forest from North America to Scandinavia, linking changes in the forest to how humans sleep and dream.
- How it works: Game-engine simulation with spatialised sound, plus an interactive online experience.
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/573604122b8ddea9122c6ee9/1736942325438-DGP6W3X1FGRO6R5RSP6R/HighresScreenshot00186.png
- Project page: https://www.jakobsteensen.com/boreal-dreams

#### Breathing with the Forest — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2023)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: Shifting Landscapes, Emergence Magazine, Oxo Tower Wharf, London 2023; Compton Verney 2025; Entwined, bitforms gallery, New York 2025
- Idea: Breathing in time with the forest extends the body into the ecosystem, so the forest reads as a larger lung the visitor is part of.
- What it is: A three-channel video installation and open-eyed meditation set around a capinuri tree in the Colombian Amazon, in which visitors synchronise their breath with audiovisual cues as the flows of carbon, water, oxygen and nitrogen through the forest are revealed.
- How it works: LiDAR scans, photogrammetry and ambisonic recordings of a capinuri tree (Maquira coriacea) made in 2020, rendered as a 4-minute 3-channel video on 4.8 m screens with 20.2-channel audio.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/912279953
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/0247_Emergence_Light_v065_QCM_BTY_B_2.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/BWTF_MLF_SandraCiampone_20231201_2.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/breathing-with-the-forest/

#### Gondwana — Ben Joseph Andrews, Emma Roberts (2022)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Hearing & vibration, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Sundance New Frontier 2022; MIFF 2022; Wales Millennium Centre
- Idea: You live inside the forest's timescale and watch it change; the longer audiences stay, the more resilient it becomes.
- What it is: A 24-hour durational VR simulation of the Daintree rainforest in which one year passes every 14 minutes, running climate projections from 1990 to 2090.
- How it works: Multiplayer real-time simulation of 40,000–50,000 forest assets over 30 virtual acres with generative sound from 40 hours of field recordings.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Gtzm-QQ9vhM
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/5b66737e1137a610ce01c2e3/90b64393-a27c-4cb7-842d-5683b54d1186/Gondwana_Sunrise.png https://voicesofvr.com/wp-content/uploads/2022/01/gondwana-vr-1102x473.jpg
- Project page: https://gondwanavr.com

#### The HEAL Institute, Museum of the Future — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2022)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Multisensory, Touch & haptics · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Museum of the Future, Dubai (permanent, 2022–)
- Idea: Visitors act as ecosystem repairers rather than becoming another being; the Biosynth works as a lens into plant and soil processes.
- What it is: The climate-focused floor of Dubai's Museum of the Future, where visitors use a handheld 'Biosynth' device to scan, magnify and repair simulated ecosystems, from a plant nursery 'Living Wall' to rainforest regeneration around a Ceiba pentandra.
- How it works: Custom handheld device with object recognition and motion tracking, projection, generative visuals and multichannel audio, developed with scientists including Dr Lucy Rowland and the Royal Botanic Garden Edinburgh.
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2026/02/SUF1-3-26.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2026/02/MLF_MoTP_Lab-13-1-1.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/museum-of-the-future-immersive-exhibition/

#### Dream — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2021)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement, Collective & networked · Medium: Performance & participatory, Screen & web, Game
- Shown at: Royal Shakespeare Company / Manchester International Festival online 2021
- Idea: Mainly a theatre experiment; the forest and its sprites are the world the audience moves through, from canopy to roots.
- What it is: A live online performance inspired by A Midsummer Night's Dream, set in a virtual forest where Puck and the sprites Cobweb, Mustardseed, Peaseblossom and Moth are played live by motion-captured actors, and online audiences help regrow the forest before dawn.
- How it works: Live motion capture in Unreal Engine (with Epic Games), an interactive symphonic score by the Philharmonia Orchestra responding to the actors, and audience interaction via phone or browser; 10 performances reached 65,000 people.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/622344847
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/Dream_TreeWithRoots-1.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/EM-Williams_Dream_Copyright-RSC_Photographer-Stuart-Martin-2.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/dream/

#### Forest of Us — Es Devlin (2021)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Every Wall is a Door, Superblue Miami 2021
- Idea: Breathing with trees: the lungs are shown as an inverted tree that completes the one outside.
- What it is: A mirrored walk-through installation whose branching forms reveal the likeness between the bronchial tree of human lungs and the branching of trees, with a soundscape of breath.
- How it works: Mirrored maze, projected light and spatial sound composed around breathing and photosynthesis.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9n-hBp3th4E
- Images: https://cdn.sanity.io/images/5olj48ug/production/fa21c3ed3ccdd9b1423db475848878c60093cc26-4758x3244.jpg?rect=0,379,4758,2486&w=1200&h=627&fit=crop
- Project page: https://esdevlin.com/work/forest-of-us

#### Catharsis — Jakob Kudsk Steensen, Matt McCorkle (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Hearing & vibration, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web, Spatial audio
- Shown at: Pinchuk Art Centre Kyiv 2019; Serpentine Galleries (Connect BTS) 2020
- Idea: Moving vertically through a single forest, root to crown, gives the visitor the tree's axis and timescale instead of a walker's path.
- What it is: A large-scale simulation of an imaginary old-growth forest, undisturbed for centuries, which the camera traverses in one continuous shot from watery roots to the canopy.
- How it works: Game-engine environment built from 3D textures and field recordings of North American forests with synchronised spatial audio by Matt McCorkle.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/354440480
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/573604122b8ddea9122c6ee9/1592830825054-H2OACWH4WCJIP1WQZI9R/HG4_3835.jpg https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/573604122b8ddea9122c6ee9/1580933640745-O2BZLKORG89FGFEYTAWW/6.jpg
- Project page: https://www.jakobsteensen.com/catharsis

#### Confronting Vegetal Otherness: Deep Phytocracy — Špela Petrič (2019)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Collective & networked, Body schema & movement · Medium: Performance & participatory
- Idea: Plant communities are treated as political bodies that govern space; humans learn to read their governance.
- What it is: A participatory walk in urban wilderness in which the audience explores feral plant communities with 'recombinant tools' built from ecology, forestry, myth and politics.
- How it works: Guided group performance with custom handheld objects and field exercises.
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/5aeca48a506fbe863b23a8b6/1558468356376-STD1MYWLNNX4I8GJ5UNH/_85A1325_Photo_Miha_Godec-3.jpg
- Project page: https://www.spelapetric.org/deep-phytocracy-feral-songs/

#### The Deep Listener — Jakob Kudsk Steensen (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: AR, Spatial audio
- Shown at: Serpentine Augmented Architecture commission 2019
- Idea: The park is re-heard through its nonhuman residents, with the London plane tree as the infrastructure everything else depends on.
- What it is: An augmented reality walk through Hyde Park and Kensington Gardens that reveals five species usually beyond human notice: London plane trees, bats, parakeets, reedbeds and azure damselflies.
- How it works: Smartphone AR app with location-triggered 3D models and spatial audio from recordings of each species, including ultrasonic bat calls made audible.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/351470817
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/573604122b8ddea9122c6ee9/1614095935755-M0XR28UWTQMZZAGJ1EOJ/1.hero.png
- Project page: https://www.jakobsteensen.com/the-deep-listener

#### Awavena — Lynette Wallworth (2018)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Altered vision, Multisensory · Medium: VR headset, 360° / immersive film
- Shown at: Sundance New Frontier 2018; Venice Virtual Reality 2018
- Idea: The forest seen as alive: VR stands in for a medicine vision that lets outsiders glimpse how the Yawanawa perceive plants and spirits.
- What it is: A VR documentary made with the Yawanawa of the Amazon and their first woman shaman, Hushahu; the forest is shown as it appears in a medicine vision, with plants and beings glowing with life.
- How it works: 360° live action shot in the Amazon combined with real-time rendered particle visions of the forest (VR headset).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Eo_BQGVzd18
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2018/Schede_film/970x647/Venice-VR/awavena.jpg?itok=cXc408uH
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2018/lineup/venice-virtual-reality/awavena

#### RE-ANIMATED — Jakob Kudsk Steensen, Matt McCorkle (2018)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: VR headset, Screen & web
- Shown at: Future Generation Art Prize, Venice Biennale 2019; Tranen Contemporary Art Center
- Idea: An extinct ecosystem can be re-entered only as a distorted digital memory, heard through the call of its last inhabitant.
- What it is: A VR and video work that rebuilds the lost Kauaʻi forest habitat of the ʻōʻō, a bird declared extinct in 1987, around the last recording of its unanswered mating call.
- How it works: Photogrammetry and field recordings from Kauaʻi assembled into a real-time game-engine world, shown as room-scale VR and multichannel video.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/291992820
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/573604122b8ddea9122c6ee9/1559670358502-6MH7CSTUHA5XIJ84S8L0/second+Venice+21.jpg
- Project page: https://www.jakobsteensen.com/re-animated

#### Selyatağı (Floodplain) — Deniz Tortum (2018)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Altered vision, Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, 360° / immersive film
- Shown at: Venice Virtual Reality 2018
- Idea: Asks whether we can become a forest, a stone or a multitude of organisms when we can no longer be what we were.
- What it is: A VR film in which a search party lost in a forest slated for construction falls under the spell of an old tree, and the humans slowly merge with the forest.
- How it works: 360° volumetric and photogrammetric forest scenes built on the world of the feature film Yuva (Emre Yeksan).
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2018/Schede_film/970x647/Venice-VR/selyatagi.jpg?itok=dbGz_le-
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2018/lineup/venice-virtual-reality/selyata%C4%9Fi-floodplain

#### Uýra, the Tree That Walks — Uýra Sodoma (2016)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics, Multisensory · Medium: Performance & participatory, Screen & web
- Shown at: PIPA Prize 2022
- Idea: The human body turned into forest matter speaks as the damaged land itself; 'everything that lives, changes'.
- What it is: An ongoing series of performances and photographs in which the artist spends about two hours assembling a body of leaves, bark, seeds, shells, fibres and natural dyes and appears as Uýra in forests, polluted streams, cities and schools of the Amazon.
- How it works: Body assemblage from gathered plant and animal materials, staged in site-specific performances and photo series such as Mil (Quase) Mortos.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iOkFPeTkCxE
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/3AnIteg88-Y/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U%C3%BDra_Sodoma

#### Forest (Laser Forest) — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2013)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Touch & haptics · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: STRP Biennale, Eindhoven 2013; HABITATS, The Old Market, Brighton 2017
- Idea: A playful, human-centred forest: trees become musical instruments that answer touch, an early MLF step toward forests as responsive environments.
- What it is: A 450 m² interactive installation of more than 150 springy rods, each tuned to a note and tipped with a laser, which visitors tap, shake and pluck to set off swinging patterns of light and spatial sound.
- How it works: Tuning-fork-like vibrating rods with sensors and lasers, each mapped to a tone and played through surround sound.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/64652497
- Images: https://i.vimeocdn.com/video/549669290-9a43e15fe6778272e264c471c9c42d8f8f7acbeb8fda8cb5841c176554c4def3-d_1280?region=us
- Project page: https://vimeo.com/64652497

## Becoming River

Elemental and planetary worlds: rivers, creeks and oceans, ice, air and breath, rock and soil, planets and deep time.

### Rivers, oceans & ice

Becoming a river, a creek, the sea, a glacier or rain.

#### La Laguna Parla — Steye Hallema (2026)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Collective & networked · Medium: Performance & participatory, Spatial audio
- Shown at: Venice Immersive 2026
- Idea: Speaking as the lagoon: a crowd's phones become one voice for a body of water that claims legal personhood.
- What it is: A collective smartphone ritual for hundreds of participants that gives voice to the Venice lagoon as a living being, adapted from The Waterbodies Orchestra made with the Confluence of European Water Bodies.
- How it works: Synchronised browser-based audio and light cues on participants' own smartphones, conducted live (The Smartphone Orchestra).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JfM0pfHVkAs
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2026/Schede_film/970x647/Ve_Immersive/la_laguna_parla.jpg?itok=zwQWuIim
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2026/venice-immersive/la-laguna-parla

#### Water Body — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2026)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
