- Shown at: ARTIS Aquarium, Amsterdam (permanent, 2026–); BFI London Film Festival Expanded 2026
- Idea: Following Robert Macfarlane, a river is treated as a living body with memory, and planetary water as an extension of our own circulation.
- What it is: A permanent installation in the ARTIS Aquarium, Amsterdam, that shows Earth's water as one circulating body: a year passes in ten seconds as ocean currents, swelling rivers, plankton blooms and the migrations of whales and seabirds move across the globe.
- How it works: Copernicus GLORYS12V1 ocean reanalysis, HydroSHEDS HydroRIVERS river networks and NASA/CNES SWOT satellite surface-water data, with animal tracking, visualised at large scale with spatial sound.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/1203391670
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2026/05/SC_00427-Edit-1-1.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2026/06/0317_Artis_v35_g05_16x9.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/water-body/

#### Glacier Dreams — Refik Anadol (2023)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Multisensory, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Dome, CAVE & projection
- Shown at: Art Dubai 2023; Art Basel 2023
- Idea: The glacier is rendered as a machine's memory of ice: an archive that moves like meltwater around the viewer.
- What it is: A room-scale AI data painting built from millions of images of Icelandic glaciers, with generated sound and scent.
- How it works: Generative AI model trained on glacier photographs and data, output on LED walls with spatial sound and diffused scent.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b_q7MAjmfto
- Project page: https://refikanadol.com

#### Once a Glacier — Jiabao Li (2022)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Time & scale, Hearing & vibration · Medium: VR headset, 360° / immersive film
- Shown at: IDFA DocLab; SXSW XR 2023; Raindance Immersive; Cannes World Film Festival (Best VR Short)
- Idea: Pairing a human life with the life of a piece of ice makes glacial time, once slow and now fast, something a viewer can feel.
- What it is: An interactive 15-minute VR film about a girl who keeps a piece of glacier ice in her freezer as she grows up, while its parent glacier disappears within her lifetime.
- How it works: Glacier forms modelled from satellite data of the Matanuska Glacier and sound recorded on Alaskan glaciers, in an interactive PC VR build.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=S1iY-nFp7EE
- Images: https://static1.squarespace.com/static/58688c8a6a496327e937e35b/t/622070b1dbbc750b6fe171e3/1646293174606/Jiabao+Li+Once+a+Glacier.jpg?format=1500w
- Project page: https://www.jiabaoli.org/once-a-glacier

#### Semi-Diurnal Spaces — Studio Above&Below (2022)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: Dome, CAVE & projection
- Idea: Sitting inside the dome you feel the channel's twice-daily tidal rhythm as the pace of the space around you.
- What it is: A site-specific fulldome installation in South Wales where live tide, wind and humidity data from the Bristol Channel shape a flowing particle sculpture around the audience.
- How it works: Real-time tidal and atmospheric data drive gravity, viscosity and flow in an NVIDIA FleX particle simulation projected in a dome.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/714540362
- Images: https://i.vimeocdn.com/video/1440159997-9a486cacf8e1963922d9667a4084d7bbcc34777c2e3b295a31ab187754b1080a-d_1280x720
- Project page: https://www.studioaboveandbelow.com/work/semi-diurnal-spaces

#### Tongues of Verglas / Les Langues de Verglas — Jakob Kudsk Steensen (2022)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: MIRE Project, Geneva 2022; Yet It Moves!, Copenhagen Contemporary 2023; Teylers Museum 2023
- Idea: Scale is the doorway: the camera shrinks from glacier to lichen so that ice, tree and symbiont read as one living body.
- What it is: A 24-minute looping simulation of the Arolla glacier's ice cave that travels from the glacial 'tongue' into a 3D-scanned Arolla pine, a drop of its sap and the wolf lichen living inside it.
- How it works: Photogrammetry and macro scans from two weeks of fieldwork at 2,300 m, assembled into a real-time Unreal Engine simulation.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/742306931
- Images: https://i.vimeocdn.com/video/1493577233-2d5a8f61d2f82964814ba9c7be1bb64c24ec31817ed31b1aec6bfebd6011fded-d_1280x720
- Project page: https://www.jakobsteensen.com/tongues-of-verglas-les-langues-de-verglas

#### Berl-Berl — Jakob Kudsk Steensen (2021)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: Halle am Berghain Berlin 2021
- Idea: The city is seen from the swamp it was built on: water, reeds and mud come back as the main character.
- What it is: A live-simulated swamp filling Halle am Berghain, built from Berlin's remaining wetlands, where the ecosystem grows and changes through day, night and seasons.
- How it works: Game-engine simulation from photogrammetry and field recordings of Berlin wetlands, shown on large screens and a sculptural water system.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BOreIFDsvCA
- Project page: https://www.jakobkudsksteensen.com

#### Thin Ice VR — Monkeystack (2021)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: VR headset, 360° / immersive film
- Shown at: Adelaide Film Festival 2021; South Australian Museum 2021
- Idea: The ice is revisited a century later, so the viewer measures its loss with their own presence.
- What it is: A VR documentary following explorer Tim Jarvis along Shackleton's 1914 Antarctic route to see how climate change has altered the ice.
- How it works: Photogrammetry and 360° video captured in Antarctica and South Georgia, assembled into an interactive VR experience.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NWcwqRn6rVc
- Images: https://www.thinicevr.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/05/ThinIce_MobileStatic-640x320px.png
- Project page: https://www.thinicevr.com

#### we are opposite like that — Himali Singh Soin (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale · Medium: Screen & web, Performance & participatory
- Idea: The ice is the elder narrator that has witnessed deep time, and the performer's body is transformed into it.
- What it is: An ongoing series (2017–2022) of films, performances and a book that tell mythologies of the poles from the perspective of the ice; in the 2019 video an alien figure crosses the white landscape and turns into glimmering ice.
- How it works: Single-channel video pairing poetry, archival material and an endangered Arctic soundscape with a costumed performance filmed in polar landscapes (likely during an Arctic ship residency).
- Video: https://vimeo.com/432044184
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/59f1eb9fd55b415293a291bf/1592991047705-DCH2NU3M8XHG4TJ4BMLH/Installation+view.JPG
- Project page: https://www.himalisinghsoin.com/we-are-opposite-like-that

#### Acoustic Ocean — Ursula Biemann (2018)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration · Medium: Screen & web, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Taipei Biennial 2018
- Idea: Counterpoint: the scientist becomes an amphibian listener, an assemblage of body and instruments tuned to the sea.
- What it is: A video installation in which a Sámi biologist-diver on the Lofoten coast listens to marine life with hydrophones, parabolic microphones and recording gear.
- How it works: Single-channel video with underwater and parabolic-microphone recordings, filmed on the Norwegian Arctic coast and shown as an installation.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pfM2YkNTcDc
- Images: https://geobodies.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/aav-ao-cover-large-aspect-ratio-770-433.jpg
- Project page: https://www.geobodies.org/art-and-videos/acoustic-ocean

#### A Colossal Wave — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2017)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Hearing & vibration, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, Mixed reality, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Quartier des Spectacles, Montreal 2017; Hull UK City of Culture 2017; SXSW 2018
- Idea: Human action is made to ripple outward as a wave, a physical metaphor for impact rather than an embodiment of water.
- What it is: A multi-location mixed-reality public artwork about human impact in the Anthropocene, using four VR 'umbrellas', a drop tower, a bowling ball, a gong and a sound-reactive gallery installation in which a physical impact sets off a virtual wave.
- How it works: Umbrella-mounted VR viewers, AR and a sound-reactive installation triggered by physical impacts, made with Presstube, Dpt. and Headspace Studio.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/244047652
- Images: https://i.vimeocdn.com/video/668342500-8d0de246271aff0c2cc33f7a963e714cc85da06ed916cba5c95e452b0021b761-d_1280?region=us
- Project page: http://acolossalwave.com/

#### Aquaphobia — Jakob Kudsk Steensen (2017)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Hearing & vibration · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: The water speaks as a jilted lover and reminds you that most of your body is water too, turning fear of water into kinship.
- What it is: A room-scale VR piece in which a morphing water microbe leads you through a virtual Red Hook park and pier, from mud tunnels to a bridge over future flood water, while the landscape narrates a breakup with you.
- How it works: HTC Vive room-scale VR built from satellite images and photographed soil, clay and rock textures, with spatial narration.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/244699310
- Images: https://static1.squarespace.com/static/573604122b8ddea9122c6ee9/t/5f9d99e01e3be610d60cac43/1589207723451/1.jpg?format=1500w
- Project page: https://www.jakobsteensen.com/aquaphobia

#### Greenland Melting — Emblematic Group (2017)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: VR headset, 360° / immersive film
- Shown at: Venice Film Festival VR 2017
- Idea: Walking along a glacier front as years of retreat play out compresses glacial time to human scale.
- What it is: A room-scale VR documentary with FRONTLINE and NOVA that takes viewers to Greenland's glaciers with NASA scientists and shows their retreat over time.
- How it works: Photogrammetry and volumetric capture combined with 360° video; glacier edge positions over decades animated in room-scale VR.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lxd9-GZqprE
- Images: https://emblematicgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/08/emblematic_slider_902x543_1.jpg
- Project page: https://emblematicgroup.com/experiences/greenland-melting/

#### Meandering River — onformative (2017)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: Contemporary Istanbul 2017; Funkhaus, Berlin 2018; National Design & Craft Gallery, Kilkenny 2018; Ars Electronica STARTS Exhibition 2019; AI for Good Global Summit, UN Geneva 2019
- Idea: River change, normally too slow to see, is sped up so its rhythm can be felt, a way of taking up a river's timescale.
- What it is: A multi-screen audiovisual installation that shows, from a bird's-eye view, rivers carving and shifting across a landscape over time, with music composed by an AI.
- How it works: Real-time visuals generated by a bespoke erosion algorithm across multiple screens, with a soundtrack composed by a machine-learning model.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/297819750
- Images: https://backend.onformative.com/assets/work/meanderingRiver_Funkhaus01.jpg https://backend.onformative.com/assets/work/meanderingRiver_detail02.png
- Project page: https://onformative.com/work/meandering-river

#### Melting Ice — Danfung Dennis (2017)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: 360° / immersive film
- Idea: Landmark climate VR that others respond to: it shows the ice rather than letting you become it.
- What it is: A 360° film from the This Is Climate Change series, presented by Al Gore, that stands the viewer on the Greenland ice sheet among meltwater rivers and moulins.
- How it works: Stereoscopic 360° video shot on the ice sheet by Condition One, distributed by Within.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MwSLTGjPqG8

#### You Are the Ocean — Özge Samancı (2017)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Wearable & sensory device
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH 2018 Art Gallery
- Idea: The boundary between body and planet dissolves: your inner weather literally becomes the weather and waves of the sea.
- What it is: An interactive installation in which a participant wearing an EEG headset controls a projected ocean with their mind: concentration raises storms, high waves and thick clouds, while a calm mind brings a quiet sea and sun.
- How it works: A consumer EEG headset estimates attention and meditation levels, which drive wave height, storminess and cloud cover in a real-time ocean simulation projected at large scale; made with Gabriel Caniglia.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/232792092
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/5d29ea0b8f0d170001041163/1563030017135-QXSWHESFQ2XI90NRAI6P/34831489_1914921635205619_6748758785960968192_o.jpg?format=1500w
- Project page: https://ozgesamanci.com/you-are-the-ocean

#### DEEP — Owen Harris (2016)
- Type: Game · Senses: Breath & interoception, Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Idea: Breathing like a diver becomes the only way through the water, so calm is not a message but the mechanics of the body.
- What it is: A meditative VR world under the sea where the player drifts among glowing rocks and corals, moving only by slow, deep breaths.
- How it works: Custom stretch-sensor belt measures diaphragm expansion and maps it to buoyancy and forward motion in a headset.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qIhZhdmPQ_8
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/56a1092da128e65fa42a11fa/1585418439909-AH0BEHDJN736S99KKYEG/Screenshot_102.3696.jpg
- Project page: https://www.exploredeep.com

#### Waterlicht — Daan Roosegaarde (2015)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Westervoort 2015; Museumplein Amsterdam 2016
- Idea: Standing under the light, people feel themselves underwater, on the seabed of a sea that is held back.
- What it is: A 'virtual flood' of shifting blue light lines drawn over people's heads at the height the water would reach without dikes.
- How it works: LED lines and lenses project wave patterns shaped by software and wind onto the night sky over a public site.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LWzPm_ponkI
- Images: https://cdn.prod.website-files.com/6683beecb76948bee8843558/66cfb15dd70d897cb22cfd9b_Waterlicht%20Roosegaarde.png_new.webp
- Project page: https://www.studioroosegaarde.net/project/waterlicht

#### Ice Watch — Olafur Eliasson (2014)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Copenhagen 2014; Place du Panthéon Paris, COP21 2015; Tate Modern London 2018
- Idea: Touching ice thousands of years old makes the glacier's time and its loss physically present.
- What it is: Twelve blocks of glacial ice from Greenland's fjords placed in a clock formation in a public square, left to melt while people touch, lean on and listen to them.
- How it works: Free-floating icebergs harvested from Nuup Kangerlua fjord, shipped in refrigerated containers and arranged as a clock face.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qd-JRGBKSXA
- Images: https://res.cloudinary.com/olafureliasson-net/image/private/q_auto:eco,c_fit,h_640,w_640/img/ice-watch_18019.jpg https://res.cloudinary.com/olafureliasson-net/image/private/q_auto:eco,c_fit,h_640,w_640/img/ice-watch_18017.jpg
- Project page: https://olafureliasson.net/artwork/ice-watch-2014/

#### River Listening — Leah Barclay (2014)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration · Medium: Spatial audio, Performance & participatory
- Idea: Listening underwater lets people judge a river's health by the sounds of the life inside it, which cannot be seen from the bank.
- What it is: A sound art and science project in which communities listen to four Queensland rivers through hydrophones in listening labs, sound maps, performances and installations.
- How it works: Hydrophone recordings with the Australian Rivers Institute on the Brisbane, Mary, Noosa and Logan rivers, shared through workshops and online sound maps.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dGAcUChycp4
- Images: http://leahbarclay.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/03/1.Leah_LoganRiver.jpg http://leahbarclay.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/03/UnderWater.png
- Project page: https://leahbarclay.com/portfolio_page/river-listening/

#### Become Ocean — John Luther Adams (2013)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Time & scale · Medium: Spatial audio, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: Seattle Symphony 2013; Pulitzer Prize for Music 2014
- Idea: Counterpoint: the title is an instruction; listening is meant to dissolve the listener into the sea as sea levels rise.
- What it is: A 42-minute orchestral work in which three groups of instruments swell and recede like tides and waves, meeting in great crests; Pulitzer Prize 2014.
- How it works: Orchestra split into three spatially separated ensembles, each cycling at different tempos to produce overlapping waves.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=yGIEvUOf-JU
- Project page: https://www.johnlutheradams.net

#### Universe of Water Particles — teamLab (2013)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation, Dome, CAVE & projection
- Shown at: teamLab Borderless Tokyo 2018
- Idea: Water flows around the visitor as around a stone, making the body an obstacle that shapes the stream.
- What it is: A digital waterfall rendered from hundreds of thousands of simulated water particles; in later versions it flows over a rock and around visitors who stand in its path.
- How it works: Particle-based fluid simulation of water interacting with virtual terrain, projected at room scale and responding to tracked bodies.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WWuDTBpPZbA
- Images: https://teamlab-site.imagewave.pictures/b5EBo9Uo-OK6SM09ZTkEZQ/83c311d3-0613-47f4-59ed-f060ce518200/width=1200,format=jpeg
- Project page: https://www.teamlab.art/w/waterparticles/

#### Rain Room — Random International (2012)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Barbican Curve London 2012; MoMA New York 2013; LACMA 2015
- Idea: The rain senses you: weather responds to the body, and visitors learn to move slowly so the water can read them.
- What it is: A hundred square metres of continuous falling water that stops wherever a person walks, so visitors move through rain without getting wet.
- How it works: 3D depth cameras track visitors and switch off overhead solenoid valves in a pattern around each body; water is recycled.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FslABAyj2OA
- Project page: https://www.random-international.com

#### River Sounding — Bill Fontana (2010)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration · Medium: Spatial audio, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Somerset House London 2010
- Idea: The river is brought indoors as sound, so visitors stand inside the Thames without getting wet.
- What it is: A sound installation that relayed underwater and structural sounds of the Thames into the rooms and light wells of Somerset House beside it.
- How it works: Hydrophones in the Thames (and likely vibration sensors on nearby structures) feed a multichannel speaker system in the building.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lSAshwqRfnA
- Project page: https://resoundings.org

#### Langjökull, Snæfellsjökull, Sólheimajökull — Katie Paterson (2007)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Time & scale · Medium: Spatial audio, Multisensory installation
- Idea: The glacier plays its own sound as it melts, so listening and disappearing are the same event.
- What it is: Three records cast in ice from the meltwater of three Icelandic glaciers, carrying recordings of those glaciers, played on turntables until they melted away.
- How it works: Meltwater frozen in record moulds, played simultaneously on three turntables; filmed as the records melted.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lfNDUjGqSVU
- Project page: https://katiepaterson.org/artwork/langjokull-snaefellsjokull-solheimajokull/

#### Vatnajökull (the sound of) — Katie Paterson (2007)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Time & scale · Medium: Spatial audio, Multisensory installation
- Idea: A phone call makes a glacier a being you can ring up and listen to, as it disappears.
- What it is: A live phone line to a microphone in Jökulsárlón lagoon under Vatnajökull: anyone could call a number and hear the glacier melting in real time.
- How it works: Underwater microphone connected to an amplifier and mobile phone in the lagoon, with the number displayed in neon in the gallery.
- Images: https://i0.wp.com/katiepaterson.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/Katie_Paterson_Vatnajokull_3-1.jpg?resize=1920%2C1442&ssl=1 https://i0.wp.com/katiepaterson.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/Katie_Paterson_Vatnajokull_5.jpg?resize=1920%2C1440&ssl=1
- Project page: https://katiepaterson.org/artwork/vatnajokull-the-sound-of/

#### Āniwaniwa — Rachael Rakena, Brett Graham (2007)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Hearing & vibration, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: Venice Biennale 2007 (collateral event)
- Idea: Perceiving from under the river: the audience shares the position of a village and people submerged by development.
- What it is: Visitors lie on their backs under large carved waka-like forms onto which underwater video is projected, sinking with Horahora, a Waikato River village drowned by a hydroelectric dam in 1947, to a Māori chant soundtrack.
- How it works: Five suspended sculptural forms with projected underwater footage and surround sound.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YJYzyIlcpAQ
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/YJYzyIlcpAQ/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GvpJaa37GGo

#### A Sound Map of the Danube — Annea Lockwood (2005)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Time & scale · Medium: Spatial audio, Multisensory installation
- Idea: A river is heard as one moving body across ten countries, with human voices as only one part of it.
- What it is: A multichannel installation and three-CD set tracing the Danube from the Black Forest to the Black Sea through underwater and shoreline recordings and the voices of people living on it.
- How it works: Hydrophone and microphone recordings made over several years along the river, arranged for surround installation.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qwsnWZ4dwz0
- Project page: https://www.annealockwood.com

#### Weather Report — Chris Watson (2003)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Time & scale · Medium: Spatial audio
- Idea: Time compression lets a human hear a glacier as a moving, cracking, melting being.
- What it is: An album of three long compositions from field recordings; its central track compresses weeks of the Vatnajökull glacier's movement in Iceland into eighteen minutes.
- How it works: Contact microphones and hydrophones placed in and under the ice over weeks, edited into a time-compressed piece released on Touch.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CH2o-FGrWdE
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chris_Watson

#### Kits Beach Soundwalk — Hildegard Westerkamp (1989)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration · Medium: Spatial audio, Performance & participatory
- Idea: Counterpoint: attention itself is the technology; filtering the city lets the shore's small lives be heard.
- What it is: A soundscape composition in which the composer's voice guides the listener along a Vancouver beach, filtering out city noise to bring forward the tiny sounds of barnacles and water.
- How it works: Stereo tape composition with narration; band-pass filtering removes traffic rumble to reveal barnacles clicking at low tide.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hg96nU6ltLk
- Project page: https://www.hildegardwesterkamp.ca

#### A Sound Map of the Hudson River — Annea Lockwood (1982)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Time & scale · Medium: Spatial audio, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Hudson River Museum 1982
- Idea: Counterpoint: listening to a river from source to mouth lets the listener travel at the river's pace and hear its changing voice.
- What it is: A sound installation and recording that follows the Hudson River from its source in the Adirondacks to the Atlantic, through recordings made at many points along its course.
- How it works: Field recordings with microphones and hydrophones along the river, edited into a continuous piece with a map for the installation.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=R_t3-cMyrkI
- Project page: https://www.annealockwood.com

### Air, breath & weather

Atmosphere, wind, clouds and breath shared with the more-than-human.

#### Breathing Planet: Atmospheric Wind Data — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2025)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation, Dome, CAVE & projection
- Shown at: Immersive Horizon, Thermen & Badewelt Sinsheim (permanent, 2025–)
- Idea: Breath is scaled up to the planet: the atmosphere is shown as co-created by all breathing beings, and the visitor breathes with it.
- What it is: A 10-minute breathwork installation in a mirrored steel half-tunnel where visitors breathe in with the day and out with the night while watching a month of Earth's atmosphere (January–February 2020) swirl across a rotating globe.
- How it works: NASA GEOS atmospheric reanalysis data on an 8K LED wall reflected in a mirrored tunnel, a 5-5 resonant breathing guide, and ambisonic and binaural sound by Carolyn Downing.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/1125548860
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2025/08/A7400696min.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2025/10/0284_WundBreathing_PlanetSq_v44.4494.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/breathing-planet-atmospheric-wind-data/

#### Flow — Adriaan Lokman (2023)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Heat & infrared, Breath & interoception · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Venice Immersive 2023
- Idea: Becoming the wind: the story is told by air currents, not by characters, and the body reads it through skin.
- What it is: A VR installation made entirely of air: the visitor surrenders to wind currents that carry the story of a woman's night, felt as gusts, warmth, breath and scent.
- How it works: Animated airflow simulation in VR synchronised with fans, heaters and scent diffusers around the seated visitor.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QxiIIZ7rP4I
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2023/Schede_film/970x647/Ve_Immersive/lokman.jpg?itok=5Yakke_C
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2023/venice-immersive/flow

#### Evolver — Marshmallow Laser Feast, Atlas V, Natan Sinigaglia (2022)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Time & scale, Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, Dome, CAVE & projection, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Tribeca Festival 2022; Geneva International Film Festival 2022; Sublime, Museum Wave, Seoul 2023; Teatr Wielki – Polish National Opera, Warsaw 2023; Works of Nature, ACMI, Melbourne 2023–24; Cannes Immersive Competition 2024; TED 2024; Adventures in Consciousness, University of Oxford 2024; Anatomy of Fragility, Frankfurter Kunstverein 2025–26; Flesh and Bones, ArtScience Museum, Singapore 2026; Wales Millennium Centre, Cardiff 2026
- Idea: The body is presented as a forest of branching vessels, so following oxygen inward ends up connecting the viewer outward to trees.
- What it is: A collective VR journey that follows a breath of oxygen from the exhale of a tree into the lungs, through the branching blood vessels and down to a single breathing cell, narrated by Cate Blanchett. It was later expanded into screen installations and a lie-down overhead projection, The Breathing Cell.
- How it works: Multi-user VR built from MRI and CT medical imaging processed with Fraunhofer MEVIS and cell models from the Allen Institute for Cell Science, with particles pulsing at a resonant breathing rhythm; music by Jonny Greenwood, Meredith Monk and Jon Hopkins; executive produced by Terrence Malick and Edward R. Pressman.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/718477724
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/Evolver_Seoul_2023_Full-6.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/MLF_Evolver_round1-3263.jpeg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/MLF_Evolver_Mediation-Room_01.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/evolver/

#### Atmos Sphaerae — Tamiko Thiel (2020)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Breath & interoception · Medium: VR headset
- Shown at: DiMoDa 4.0 Dis/Location 2020; Gazelli Art House 2022
- Idea: Atmospheres become objects you can hold at arm's length and then enter, making air a place rather than a void.
- What it is: A VR work of floating spheres, each holding a different atmosphere or waterscape, that visitors look into and pass through.
- How it works: Real-time VR built in a game engine, first released for DiMoDa 4.0 (Dis/Location) and later shown as an installation.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BGZrTPnNnwA
- Images: https://tamikothiel.com/atmos-sphaerae/media/AtmosSphaerae-Gazelli-Installation2022c_1024px.jpg
- Project page: https://tamikothiel.com/atmos-sphaerae/

#### Breathe — Diego Galafassi (2020)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Collective & networked · Medium: Mixed reality, VR headset
- Shown at: Sundance New Frontier 2020
- Idea: Your breath is not yours: becoming the air shows how every exhalation joins a planetary atmosphere.
- What it is: A social mixed-reality piece that makes each participant's breath visible and follows it as air that passes between people, forests and oceans around the planet.
- How it works: Breath sensing drives particle visualisations in a shared mixed-reality space (Magic Leap / VR headsets).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8-pVNx5rpgg
- Project page: https://voicesofvr.com/888-sundance-breathe-visualizes-how-breath-connects-us-to-each-other-in-social-ar-experience/

#### Digital Atmosphere — Studio Above&Below (2020)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Breath & interoception · Medium: AR, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Near Now Fellowship, Broadway Nottingham 2020
- Idea: Gives the invisible air a visible body and a voice, so a passer-by perceives the atmosphere they are breathing.
- What it is: A public sculpture with a built-in air-quality sensor whose AR layer, seen through a phone, changes form and colour as pollution in the surrounding air changes.
- How it works: An 'Atmo' air-quality sensor streams readings to a mobile AR app that deforms a virtual sculpture anchored to the physical one.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/463156008
- Images: https://www.studioaboveandbelow.com/media/2026/08/Studio-AboveBelow-DigitalAtmosphere.webp
- Project page: https://www.studioaboveandbelow.com/work/digital-atmosphere

#### Fly with Aerocene Pacha — Tomás Saraceno (2020)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Performance & participatory
- Shown at: CONNECT, BTS 2020
- Idea: A human body is lifted by the atmosphere alone, joining the air as a traveller rather than a passenger of engines.
- What it is: A solar-powered balloon flight over the Salinas Grandes in Argentina that carried a person aloft using only sun and air, made with local Indigenous communities.
- How it works: Tethered solar balloon heated by sunlight, flown in a record-setting human flight on 25 January 2020.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AG_UXEXg_Mk
- Images: https://aerocene.org/wp-content/uploads/2020/01/19ARG_BTS_AerocenePacha_03637-1.jpg
- Project page: https://aerocene.org/pacha/

#### Wunderkammer — Olafur Eliasson, Acute Art (2020)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: AR
- Idea: Weather is brought into the living room at hand scale, so a cloud raining on the carpet makes the atmosphere domestic.
- What it is: A collection of AR objects placed in one's home — a rain cloud, the sun, a rainbow, a puffin, a floating stone and more — released during the 2020 lockdowns.
- How it works: Smartphone AR app by Acute Art placing animated objects with light and rain simulation in the user's space.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4-UT2MYSjMo
- Images: https://res.cloudinary.com/olafureliasson-net/image/private/q_auto:eco,c_fit,h_640,w_640/img/wunderkammer_23497.jpg https://res.cloudinary.com/olafureliasson-net/image/private/q_auto:eco,c_fit,h_640,w_640/img/wunderkammer_23499.jpg
- Project page: https://olafureliasson.net/artwork/wunderkammer-2020/

#### Atmospheric Memory — Rafael Lozano-Hemmer (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Hearing & vibration, Multisensory · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Manchester International Festival 2019
- Idea: The atmosphere is treated as a recording medium that holds every breath and word.
- What it is: An exhibition of works that capture visitors' voices and breath in the air — as ripples in water, vapour clouds, words in mist and weather — inspired by Charles Babbage's idea that the air is a library of every word ever spoken.
- How it works: Microphones, breath sensors, vapour printers, ultrasonic misters and water tanks translate speech and breath into visible air and water patterns.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=RdduY-CUBHM
- Project page: https://www.lozano-hemmer.com/atmospheric_memory.php

#### The Tides Within Us — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: The State of Us, The Lowry, Salford 2019–20; Human Nature, York Mediale, York Art Gallery 2020–21; Works of Nature, ACMI, Melbourne 2023–24; Breathe | Mauri Ora, Te Papa, Wellington 2025–26
- Idea: A human-centred work about breath: the body is shown as open to its surroundings, with no clear line where air ends and blood begins.
- What it is: A series of installations and six large prints that follow oxygen from the lungs through the branching vessels of the body, showing the body's interior as a tidal, tree-like ecosystem.
- How it works: Medical imaging of the lungs and vascular system rendered with CG artist Erik Ferguson, shown as projections and Giclée prints.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eO28znCLu-Q
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/TWU-Lung-1.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/tides-within-us/

#### Pollution Pods — Michael Pinsky (2017)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Smell & taste, Multisensory · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Trondheim 2017; Somerset House London 2018
- Idea: Air quality becomes an experience of the lungs, smell and eyes rather than a number.
- What it is: Five linked geodesic domes that recreate the air of London, Beijing, New Delhi, São Paulo and Tautra in Norway, which visitors walk through and breathe.
- How it works: Mixtures of safe chemicals, scent and humidity calibrated to each city's pollution profile, released inside each dome.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=I7nMME-3aC8
- Project page: https://www.michaelpinsky.com/project/pollution-pods/

#### Rainbow — Olafur Eliasson, Acute Art (2017)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Collective & networked · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: A rainbow exists only between a body, water droplets and light, so the viewer co-produces the weather they see.
- What it is: A multi-user VR work in which visitors stand inside a curtain of falling rain and see a rainbow form as they move; other visitors appear as figures of light.
- How it works: Networked room-scale VR with physically based simulation of light refracting through rain droplets; produced by Acute Art.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Z74cSovLh-s
- Project page: https://olafureliasson.net

#### Aerocene — Tomás Saraceno (2015)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Time & scale · Medium: Performance & participatory, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: COP21 Paris, Grand Palais 2015
- Idea: To float on air and sun alone is to live with the atmosphere as a medium, following winds instead of routes.
- What it is: An open community and art project flying sculptures lifted only by the sun's heat and the air, with no fossil fuels, helium or batteries.
- How it works: Solar balloon sculptures of lightweight film heated by sunlight; community launches with open-source 'Aerocene Explorer' kits.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZIyljW9ejxU
- Images: https://aerocene.org/wp-content/uploads/2020/03/TS_09MAS_museo-prato_00121edit.jpg
- Project page: https://aerocene.org/

#### Vicious Circular Breathing — Rafael Lozano-Hemmer (2013)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Borusan Contemporary Istanbul 2013; Musée d'art contemporain de Montréal 2018
- Idea: Air becomes a shared, recycled body: to enter is to take in the breath of strangers.
- What it is: A sealed glass room of motorised bellows and brown paper bags where visitors may enter and breathe air already breathed by everyone before them.
- How it works: Closed-circuit system of bellows, tubes and paper bags that inflate and deflate like lungs; entry requires signing a risk waiver.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b5BG4F8Nd7g
- Images: https://www.lozano-hemmer.com/image_sets/vicious_circular_breathing/monterrey_2019/vcb_monterrey_2019_my_505A7355_t.jpg
- Project page: https://www.lozano-hemmer.com/vicious_circular_breathing.php

#### in orbit — Tomás Saraceno (2013)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Collective & networked · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: K21 Ständehaus Düsseldorf 2013
- Idea: Walking on air like a cloud-dweller, and sensing others through vibration, borrows a spider's way of knowing space.
- What it is: A walkable net suspended 25 metres above the piazza of K21 Ständehaus, with giant air-filled spheres, where visitors climb and feel each other's movements through the mesh.
- How it works: Three layers of steel-wire mesh spanning 2,500 m², with PVC spheres; the net transmits the movements of up to ten visitors.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ROqL-8h_7DM
- Images: https://studiotomassaraceno.org/files/ts_11k21_51331-1400x933-e1614949390714.jpg
- Project page: https://studiotomassaraceno.org/in-orbit/

#### Last Breath — Rafael Lozano-Hemmer (2012)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Idea: A single breath is kept alive as air in motion, outliving the body that exhaled it.
- What it is: A device that stores a breath of Cuban singer Omara Portuondo in a brown paper bag and circulates it forever, inflating and deflating as if breathing.
- How it works: Sealed bellows and tubing circulate the stored air through a paper bag; a display counts the breaths.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=C9s-MUdXmm4
- Images: https://www.lozano-hemmer.com/image_sets/last_breath/naples_2024/last_breath_naples_2024_rlh_001_t.jpg
- Project page: https://www.lozano-hemmer.com/last_breath.php

#### Flower — thatgamecompany (2009)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Shown at: The Art of Video Games, Smithsonian American Art Museum 2012
- Idea: Becoming wind: the player has no body, only a direction and a gust.
- What it is: A game in which you are the wind, gathering a stream of petals across fields and bringing colour and life back to a grey landscape.
- How it works: Tilting the PlayStation 3 SIXAXIS controller steers the wind and pressing any button makes it blow harder.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=s1oZnf3475c
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/966330/header.jpg
- Project page: http://thatgamecompany.com/flower/

#### Cloud — Jenova Chen (2005)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Landmark counterpoint: the player is a dreaming boy, not a cloud, but pulling, merging and raining clouds makes weather a material shaped by the body.
- What it is: A student game from USC in which a boy in hospital dreams of flying, gathering clouds into larger ones and making it rain over the land.
- How it works: PC game made by a USC Interactive Media student team; the mouse draws clouds together and triggers rain.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zM0NwnQV0Nk
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/8/89/Cloudbox.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cloud_(video_game)

#### The Weather Project — Olafur Eliasson (2003)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Collective & networked · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Tate Modern Unilever Series 2003
- Idea: Counterpoint: weather is staged indoors so people experience it together, as a shared atmosphere rather than a backdrop.
- What it is: An artificial sun of mono-frequency lamps and a haze-filled Turbine Hall at Tate Modern, with a mirrored ceiling where visitors lay down and saw themselves as small figures under the sky.
- How it works: Semicircle of mono-frequency sodium lamps doubled by a mirrored ceiling, with humidifiers releasing sugar-water mist.
- Images: https://res.cloudinary.com/olafureliasson-net/image/private/q_auto:eco,c_fit,h_640,w_640/img/the-weather-project_21881.jpg https://res.cloudinary.com/olafureliasson-net/image/private/q_auto:eco,c_fit,h_640,w_640/img/the-weather-project_8200.jpg
- Project page: https://olafureliasson.net/artwork/the-weather-project-2003/

#### Osmose — Char Davies (1995)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Musée d'art contemporain de Montréal 1995
- Idea: Breath and balance replace hands and eyes as the way to move, so the body behaves like a diver or a cloud rather than a tool user.
- What it is: A VR work in which the immersant floats through a grid, a forest, a pond, clouds and an abyss, rising by breathing in and sinking by breathing out.
- How it works: Head-mounted display with a vest that measures chest expansion for vertical motion and body tilt for direction; real-time 3D sound; audience watches the immersant's shadow and view.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/240806.240808 (ACM SIGGRAPH Computer Graphics 1996)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=54O4VP3tCoY
- Images: https://www.immersence.com/images/osmose/Osm_Tree_Pond_600.jpg https://www.immersence.com/images/osmose/Osm_Subt_Earth_600_v2.jpg
- Project page: https://www.immersence.com/osmose/

#### Fog Sculpture — Fujiko Nakaya (1970)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Touch & haptics, Multisensory · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Expo '70 Osaka; Exploratorium Fog Bridge 2013
- Idea: Standing inside a cloud, people lose their outlines and feel weather as a body around them.
- What it is: A series of sculptures of artificial fog, begun at the Pepsi Pavilion at Expo '70, which envelop visitors, erase sight lines and move with wind and temperature.
- How it works: High-pressure pumps push pure water through micro-nozzles to make droplets small enough to hang in the air, choreographed with local wind data.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=f0ynptF97n4
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fujiko_Nakaya

### Rock, soil & deep time

Stones, mountains, minerals and geological time.

#### Collective Body — Sarah Silverblatt-Buser (2025)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Collective & networked · Medium: VR headset, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: Venice Immersive 2025
- Idea: Your way of moving decides which element you become: embodiment as earth, air, water or fire rather than as a human figure.
- What it is: A multi-user VR dance experience set in a New Mexican storm: an algorithm analyses how each participant moves and assigns them one of sixteen avatars, each a natural element (earth, air, water or fire) with its own musical theme, before they meet and move with the others.
- How it works: Full-body tracked social VR with movement classification into a 4×4 grid of elements and energy phases (solid, liquid, gas, plasma) driving avatars and music.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=c1uOAEL7Do8
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2025/Schede_film/970x647/Ve_Immersive/collective_body.jpg?itok=Ff8l25Pv
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2025/venice-immersive/collective-body

#### The Ephesus Experience Museum — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2023)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Multisensory · Medium: Multisensory installation, Dome, CAVE & projection
- Shown at: Ephesus Experience Museum, Selçuk (permanent, 2023–)
- Idea: A human-history work; listed for completeness, its nearest link to this gallery is the felt compression of deep time in stone and place.
- What it is: A projection-based museum near the ancient city of Ephesus in Türkiye, in which visitors walk through the city's history across millennia.
- How it works: Large-scale projection, spatial audio, sculpture and animation, developed with DEM Museums, architects, historians and archaeologists.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=waUPEuJ6f5Q
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2025/10/Copy-of-SCiampone-SC_08949-min.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/the-ephesus-experience-museum/

#### FRAMERATE: Pulse of the Earth — ScanLAB Projects (2022)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: Venice Immersive 2022
- Idea: Seeing at the Earth's pace: geological and seasonal change compressed until land appears to breathe.
- What it is: A multi-screen installation of LiDAR time-lapses of changing landscapes: a cliff eroding, a pumpkin growing, sand shifting and forests turning with the seasons.
- How it works: Repeated terrestrial LiDAR scans of the same sites over months, rendered as volumetric point-cloud time-lapses.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8bcrX3ah-PY
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2022/Schede_film/970x647/Venice_Immersive/shaw_trossel_.jpg?itok=MsvUsDiC
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2022/venice-immersive/framerate-pulse-earth

#### Stone — Shinseungback Kimyonghun (2017)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Hearing & vibration, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Goethe-Institut Shanghai commission 2017
- Idea: Becoming a stone is standing still while the sea arrives, again and again, on your surface.
- What it is: Water sensors on a stone on the shore of the volcanic island Ulleungdo recorded the waves; in the gallery a structure of 64 solenoids replays the waves breaking on that stone, and visitors step inside to 'become the stone'.
- How it works: 64 water sensors and 64 solenoids on wooden panels, Arduino, custom software, speakers and projection.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/211470143
- Images: https://djhznh41oxwef.cloudfront.net/works/stone/ssbkyh_stone_01.png
- Project page: http://ssbkyh.com/works/stone/

#### Deep Time Walk — Stephan Harding (2016)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Time & scale, Body schema & movement · Medium: Spatial audio, Performance & participatory
- Idea: Walking deep time with your own legs turns geological scale into bodily effort and distance.
- What it is: An audio walk in which each metre walked equals a million years, so a 4.6 km walk takes the listener through Earth's whole history, with humans arriving in the last few centimetres.
- How it works: Smartphone app with a dramatised audio script by the ecologist, synchronised to GPS distance walked.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5kY24DUm0VM
- Images: https://www.deeptimewalk.org/wp-content/uploads/2017/12/feature-product-app.jpg
- Project page: https://www.deeptimewalk.org/

#### Mountain — David OReilly (2014)
- Type: Game · Senses: Time & scale · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Being a mountain means being almost passive: the experience is the time scale, not control.
- What it is: An ambient game in which you are a mountain floating in space; seasons pass, objects crash into you and stay, and you can only watch and play notes.
- How it works: After the player answers drawing prompts to seed the mountain, it runs on its own with simulated weather and seasons; the keyboard only plays notes.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GMyOTRrM9jg
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/313340/header.jpg
- Project page: http://mountain-game.com

#### From Dust — Eric Chahi (2011)
- Type: Game · Senses: Touch & haptics, Time & scale · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: The player is a geological force, learning how rivers carve sand and lava hardens into rock.
- What it is: A god game in which the player, as 'the Breath', scoops and pours sand, water and lava to reshape an island and protect a tribe from floods and eruptions.
- How it works: Real-time simulation of sediment, water flow, erosion and lava on a heightfield terrain.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mkdAssf3kJA
- Images: https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/3/3b/From_Dust_cover.png
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/From_Dust

#### Tele Echo Tube — Hiroki (Hill) Kobayashi (2010)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Touch & haptics · Medium: Multisensory installation, Spatial audio
- Shown at: MUAC, Mexico City 2010; ACM Multimedia 2013; Matsudo International Science Art Festival 2018
- Idea: Draws on the yamabiko, the mountain spirit that answers: the visitor is heard and answered by an actual mountain rather than a recording.
- What it is: A speaking-tube installation linked in real time to a remote mountain forest in Japan: the visitor calls into the tube, the voice is played in the valley, and the mountain's real echo returns with vibration.
- How it works: Networked microphone and speaker placed in a forest valley, a tube interface that likely vibrates (slit drum) as the echo returns, and live audio streaming.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2502081.2502125 (ACM Multimedia 2013)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KDlQG92_zhQ
- Project page: https://doi.org/10.1145/2502081.2502125

#### Ephémère — Char Davies (1998)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Body schema & movement, Time & scale · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: National Gallery of Canada 1998
- Idea: Descending by breath through soil and into the body makes landscape and body one continuous, changing substance.
- What it is: A breath-controlled VR landscape in three layers — land, earth below it, and the interior of the body — where seeds, rivers and organs come and go with the seasons.
- How it works: Same breath-and-balance vest as Osmose with a head-mounted display; elements transform over time as the immersant lingers or passes.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XCWaMll0leI
- Images: https://www.immersence.com/images/ephemere/Eph_Autumn_Flux_I_600.jpg https://www.immersence.com/images/ephemere/016_Ephemere_Installation_View_2.jpg
- Project page: https://www.immersence.com/ephemere/

#### Dialogue with the Knowbotic South — Knowbotic Research (1994)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Hearing & vibration, Heat & infrared · Medium: Multisensory installation, Dome, CAVE & projection
- Idea: Antarctica is known only through instruments; the work lets a visitor feel a remote environment as data made bodily.
- What it is: In a dark room, visitors navigate a cloud of 'knowbots', agents built from live Antarctic research data, while wind and sound driven by Antarctic conditions fill the space.
- How it works: Networked feeds from Antarctic stations and databases drive computer-generated agents, projection, sound and a wind machine; navigation by tracked pointer (details likely varied by venue).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dJ3ZbD5uGkE

### Planets & cosmos

Earth as a whole, other planets and cosmic scale.

#### YOU:MATTER — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2025)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Time & scale, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: National Science and Media Museum, Bradford 2025–26
- Idea: Mostly about human belonging to cosmic and planetary cycles; the breath-into-sequoia room is the direct nonhuman encounter.
- What it is: A seven-room exhibition for Bradford 2025 UK City of Culture that traces the visitor's body back to the Big Bang: visitors breathe into a digital sequoia, watch Earth breathe CO2 from satellite data, follow a water drop from cloud to sweat, and see sunlight in their own blood vessels.
- How it works: Breath sensors and real-time projection, NASA satellite CO2 data, head-in cloud sculptures with narration, hand-illuminating projection and face capture merged into a morphing tree of life.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=WZUr6PChM00
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2025/04/NSMM_YouMatter_Photography_250325_DSC01088.jpeg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2025/04/NSMM_YouMatter_Photography_250325_DSC01449-1.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/youmatter/

#### Genesis — Jörg Courtial (2021)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Time & scale, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset
- Shown at: Venice VR Expanded 2021
- Idea: Taking the planet's time scale: humanity appears only in the final second of Earth's day.
- What it is: A VR journey through 4.7 billion years of Earth condensed onto a 24-hour clock: the young glowing planet, dark oceans, prehistoric jungles with giant insects, dinosaurs, mass extinctions and finally human ancestors.
- How it works: Real-time rendered VR (Vive / Quest) with an on-screen Earth clock and paleontologically informed CG environments.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rx70fLFpTp8
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2021/Schede_film/970x647/Venice_VR_Expanded/courtial_genesis.jpg?itok=h5M0IGwZ
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2021/lineup/venice-vr-expanded/genesis

#### Distortions in Spacetime — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2018)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation, Dome, CAVE & projection
- Shown at: British Science Festival, Hull 2018; Manchester Science Festival, Science and Industry Museum 2018; Shifting Proximities, Nxt Museum, Amsterdam 2021–22; Into the Black Hole, Valkhof Museum, Nijmegen 2023–24; Works of Nature, ACMI, Melbourne 2023–24; The Presence of Absence, Frankfurter Kunstverein 2024; Noor Riyadh 2024
- Idea: The body is shown as star matter: stepping toward the event horizon links a human gesture to cosmic scale and deep time.
- What it is: An interactive audiovisual installation in which visitors' movements are mirrored by particle systems as they fall toward a black hole and witness a supernova, tracing the elements in their bodies back to dying stars.
- How it works: Body tracking drives real-time particle simulations of gravitational collapse, with music by Arthur Jeffes (Penguin Cafe) and scientific advice from astrophysicist Samaya Nissanke.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/254467760
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/DIST_02.jpg https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2023/11/DIST_04.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/distortions-in-spacetime/

#### Gaia — Luke Jerram (2018)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Idea: Counterpoint: a screen-free overview effect, seeing the whole planet hanging at human scale.
- What it is: A seven-metre illuminated globe printed with NASA imagery of Earth, hung in cathedrals, museums and festivals with a surround soundscape.
- How it works: Internally lit inflatable sculpture printed with NASA Blue Marble imagery at about 1.8 million to one, with music by Dan Jones.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=sjvw-8VwAbw
- Images: https://my-gaia.com/www.my-gaia.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/03/0231-1-1.jpg
- Project page: https://my-gaia.com/

#### Spheres — Eliza McNitt, Atlas V (2018)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Time & scale, Hearing & vibration · Medium: VR headset
- Shown at: Sundance New Frontier 2018; Venice VR 2018 Grand Jury Prize
- Idea: Cosmic scale is reached through the hands and ears: you hold a star system and hear gravitational waves.
- What it is: An interactive VR series in three chapters where the viewer holds planets, listens to the sounds of space and falls into a black hole, narrated by Millie Bobby Brown, Jessica Chastain and Patti Smith.
- How it works: Real-time VR with hand controllers and spatial audio based on astrophysical data, including LIGO gravitational-wave sounds.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Wv6P36Z4HDA
- Images: https://atlasv.io/wp-content/uploads/2026/01/Spheres_Key-Art-scaled.jpg https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/seo_thumbnail/public/cinema/2018/Schede_film/970x647/Venice-VR/spheres.jpg?itok=EusW57yV
- Project page: https://atlasv.io/spheres/

#### Sonoseismic Earth — Saša Spačal (2015)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Touch & haptics · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Festival Mfru Kiblix 2015, Maribor; Aksioma, Ljubljana 2017; Device_art 6.018, Zagreb 2018
- Idea: A planetary perspective delivered as vibration: human proximity drives the seismic shifts, so the visitor feels the planet respond to them.
- What it is: A responsive kinetic globe that cracks and darkens as visitors approach, filling the room with the infrasound of earthquakes felt in the body rather than heard.
- How it works: Proximity sensors drive seismographic animation on a kinetic globe and infrasonic speakers.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/247492932
- Images: https://www.agapea.si/wp-content/uploads/2015/12/11_aksioma_spacal-hirsenfelder_sonoseismic-earth_img_9571_32258997414_o.jpg
- Project page: https://www.agapea.si/en/projects/earth-a-sonoseismic-instrument

#### Earth–Moon–Earth (Moonlight Sonata Reflected from the Surface of the Moon) — Katie Paterson (2007)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Time & scale · Medium: Spatial audio, Multisensory installation
- Idea: The Moon becomes a co-author: its craters and shadows erase parts of the music.
- What it is: Beethoven's Moonlight Sonata translated into Morse code, bounced off the Moon by radio and played back on a self-playing piano with the notes the Moon's surface lost.
- How it works: Earth–Moon–Earth radio transmission by amateur radio operators; received Morse code re-translated into a score for a player piano.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=zwUSkLf1OVg
- Images: https://i0.wp.com/katiepaterson.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/02/Katie_Paterson_EME_4_33.jpg?resize=1920%2C2947&ssl=1
- Project page: https://katiepaterson.org/artwork/earth-moon-earth/

## Becoming Robot

Machinic worlds: robot and drone bodies, AI and machine perception, objects and things, and cyborg senses.

### Robot & drone bodies

Embodying a robot, a drone or a vehicle from the inside.

#### Inattentive Robot — Botao 'Amber' Hu (2026)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement, Time & scale · Medium: Screen & web
- Idea: Taking the robot's position shows that one body can attend to only one claim at a time, so attention becomes a question of priority and care.
- What it is: A provocation paper and set of six illustrated scenarios about a household robot with one body and many users, which cannot attend to everyone at once — pancakes versus homework, a car repair versus grandpa falling.
- How it works: Scenario-based attention-conflict tests, drawn as illustrations, used as a design method to expose how a shared robot decides whose time matters first.
- Paper: https://amber.botao.hu/project/inattentive-robot (CHI 2027 (under review))
- Images: https://images.spr.so/cdn-cgi/imagedelivery/j42No7y-dcokJuNgXeA0ig/5b36fbb7-90cd-4c14-9e4b-21e92b43e2f4/image_-3/public https://images.spr.so/cdn-cgi/imagedelivery/j42No7y-dcokJuNgXeA0ig/24d8ee0e-6050-4aad-954d-eb9970a6db60/image/public https://images.spr.so/cdn-cgi/imagedelivery/j42No7y-dcokJuNgXeA0ig/76fdc61f-abf2-410b-9cad-7de73a811b6c/image/public
- Project page: https://amber.botao.hu/project/inattentive-robot

#### BirdViewAR — Yoshifumi Kitamura (2023)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: AR, Screen & web
- Idea: Embodying a drone can mean seeing it from behind, as a game character, rather than through its own eyes.
- What it is: A drone-piloting interface that gives the remote pilot an augmented third-person view of the drone within its surroundings, like following your own avatar.
- How it works: The drone's camera captures its surroundings and the interface reconstructs a third-person viewpoint with AR overlays of the drone's position and nearby obstacles.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3544548.3580681 (CHI 2023)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DtTpvL-JXKY

#### My Name is O90 — Siyeon Kim (2023)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Body schema & movement, Time & scale · Medium: VR headset
- Shown at: Venice Immersive 2023
- Idea: A robot's memory, loyalty and low battery become the lens: the viewer walks in the shoes of an aging device.
- What it is: An XR animation that follows the long night journey of O90, an abandoned AI robot dog searching the city for charging cables while remembering a lost friend.
- How it works: A VR animation produced by Studio Metapo and the Korean Academy of Film Arts, presented at Venice Immersive.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=90v9PMtR5oc
- Images: https://static.labiennale.org/files/styles/full_screen_slide/public/cinema/2023/Schede_film/970x647/Ve_Immersive/kim-siyeon.jpg?itok=e_cYnFf2
- Project page: https://www.labiennale.org/en/cinema/2023/venice-immersive/my-name-o90

#### Avatar Robot Café DAWN — Ory Laboratory (2018)
- Type: Product · Senses: Body schema & movement, Collective & networked · Medium: Multisensory installation, Performance & participatory
- Idea: A robot body lets someone who cannot leave their bed go to work and be met in public.
- What it is: A café in Tokyo staffed by OriHime robots piloted from home by people with severe disabilities such as ALS, who take orders, talk and serve through the robots.
- How it works: Pilots control 120 cm OriHime-D robots and tabletop OriHime units remotely over the internet, including by eye-gaze input, using camera, microphone and speaker on the robot.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=vj1z6HEAkYY
- Images: https://dawn2021.orylab.com/assets/images/common/ogp2.jpg
- Project page: https://dawn2021.orylab.com/en/

#### Data-driven Body–Machine Interface for Drones — EPFL Laboratory of Intelligent Systems (2018)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: The most natural way to be a drone is with your whole trunk, like a bird banking.
- What it is: By recording how people spontaneously move while imitating a drone in VR, researchers found that steering with the torso beats a joystick for flying.
- How it works: Motion capture and EMG recorded body movements during simulated flight in a head-mounted display; the most informative signals (torso) were used to control simulated and real drones.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1073/pnas.1718648115 (PNAS 2018)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=LLam4TNVi_g

#### FlyJacket — EPFL Laboratory of Intelligent Systems (2018)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision, Touch & haptics · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, VR headset
- Idea: Flying a drone like a bird: the body's posture becomes the aircraft's attitude.
- What it is: A soft upper-body exoskeleton that lets a person fly a fixed-wing drone with arms spread and torso bending, as if they were the aircraft.
- How it works: An IMU-tracked soft jacket with arm supports maps torso pitch and roll to the drone, streams its camera to a head-mounted display and gives haptic feedback on the body.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/lra.2018.2810955 (IEEE Robotics and Automation Letters 2018)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=L0FTPYkLKHI

#### Fusion — MHD Yamen Saraiji, Kouta Minamizawa (2018)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement, Collective & networked · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, VR headset
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH 2018 Emerging Technologies
- Idea: Two minds, one body: the robot is a second self grafted onto someone else.
- What it is: A backpack robot with a head and two arms that a remote operator inhabits through VR, sharing one body with the person who wears it.
- How it works: A remote operator in a head-mounted display controls the robot's stereo head and anthropomorphic arms; modes range from guiding the wearer's hands to moving them by force.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3214907.3214912 (SIGGRAPH 2018 Emerging Technologies)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Nrc7gH6dydw

#### Geomancer — Lawrence Lek (2017)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: Screen & web
- Shown at: Jerwood/FVU Awards 2017
- Idea: Told from inside a machine mind: what does a planet-watching intelligence want once it has a body?
- What it is: A CGI film set in 2065 in which an adolescent satellite AI, created to monitor the weather, descends to Singapore on the nation's centenary hoping to become an artist.
- How it works: Game-engine rendered film with a synthetic AI narrator.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NNZH_HJaqbI
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/NNZH_HJaqbI/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lawrence_Lek

#### Lone Echo — Ready At Dawn (2017)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Idea: Your tracked hands become an android's hands; the machine body is felt through grabbing and pushing off.
- What it is: A VR game in which you are Jack, an android assistant on a mining station in the rings of Saturn, moving hand over hand through zero gravity.
- How it works: Oculus Rift with Touch controllers drives full-body inverse kinematics for the android, with physics-based zero-g locomotion.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2pmV2mwAV9k

#### Non-human Looking Robot Arms Induce Illusion of Embodiment — Laura Aymerich-Franch (2017)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: A body does not have to look human to become yours; moving it and seeing from it is enough.
- What it is: Participants felt embodied in a humanoid robot even when its arms ended in mechanical grippers rather than human-like hands.
- How it works: First-person views from the HRP-2 robot's cameras were shown in a head-mounted display while participants saw its non-human arms; embodiment was compared across conditions by questionnaire.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/s12369-017-0397-8 (International Journal of Social Robotics 2017)

#### The Second Me: Humanoid Robot Embodiment and Bi-location — Laura Aymerich-Franch (2016)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Being a robot does not erase the human body: the self can split between the machine and the flesh.
- What it is: People embodied in an HRP-2 humanoid robot saw their own real body through the robot's eyes, and felt as if they were in two places at once.
- How it works: Participants wore a head-mounted display showing the HRP-2's head camera view while controlling the robot; questionnaires measured embodiment and bi-location when their real body appeared in view.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.concog.2016.09.017 (Consciousness and Cognition 2016)

#### Avatar Anthropomorphism and Body Ownership in VR — Jean-Luc Lugrin, Marc Erich Latoschik (2015)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Non-human, machine-like bodies can be owned about as readily as human ones: likeness matters less than expected.
- What it is: A VR study comparing how strongly people own virtual bodies that range from abstract and robot-like figures to realistic humans.
- How it works: Participants embodied full-body tracked avatars of varying human-likeness in a head-mounted display with virtual mirror exposure and threat events, and rated body ownership.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/vr.2015.7223379 (IEEE VR 2015)

#### Inferno — Louis-Philippe Demers, Bill Vorn (2015)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement, Collective & networked · Medium: Performance & participatory, Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Being moved by a machine: participants give their limbs to an exoskeleton and become part of a robotic performance.
- What it is: A participatory performance in which audience members wear robotic exoskeletons that move their arms and torsos to a loud electronic score, turning a crowd into a robot choir.
- How it works: Upper-body exoskeletons with motorised arm joints are sequenced with light and sound; some let wearers push back against the programmed motion.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JaUAVo8PBJ4

#### Propel: Body on Robot Arm — Stelarc (2015)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Performance & participatory
- Idea: The body gives up its own motion and becomes the payload of a machine's choreography.
- What it is: Strapped to an industrial robot arm, Stelarc's body is swung through space for 30 minutes along a choreography programmed for the robot.
- How it works: A six-axis industrial robot arm with a 3 m task envelope runs an offline-programmed looping sequence; motor sounds are amplified as the soundtrack.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2bRpTn0KKd8
- Images: https://stelarc.org/media/img/projects-overview/propel2.jpg
- Project page: https://stelarc.org/_activity-20354.php

#### SOMA — Frictional Games (2015)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Game
- Idea: Becoming a machine as an identity crisis: what remains of a person in a scanned copy?
- What it is: A first-person horror game set in an undersea facility, where the player slowly realises their mind has been copied into a machine body.
- How it works: First-person narrative game (PC, PS4) built on Frictional's HPL engine.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=syhcF0Mx0j0
- Images: https://cdn.akamai.steamstatic.com/steam/apps/282140/header.jpg
- Project page: https://store.steampowered.com/app/282140/

#### Big Robot Mk.1 — Hiroo Iwata (2014)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation, Wearable & sensory device
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH 2016 Emerging Technologies (Mk.1A)
- Idea: Becoming a giant robot is mostly a change of scale: streets, people and trees are re-measured from a body three times taller.
- What it is: A five-metre-tall walking robot vehicle with the rider seated at its head, so the rider moves through the world at the height and pace of a giant.
- How it works: Two-legged walking frame with the seat at head height; later version Mk.1A was shown at SIGGRAPH 2016 Emerging Technologies (control details not verified).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5VJXQBGEGoU
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/5VJXQBGEGoU/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5VJXQBGEGoU

#### The Talos Principle — Croteam (2014)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: Game
- Idea: Inhabiting a robot body turns puzzles into questions about consciousness.
- What it is: A first-person puzzle game in which you play an android wandering a simulated world, questioning whether it is a person.
- How it works: First-person puzzle game with terminal-based dialogue on philosophy of mind (PC, consoles).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iAVh4_wnOIw
- Images: https://cdn.akamai.steamstatic.com/steam/apps/257510/header.jpg
- Project page: https://store.steampowered.com/app/257510/

#### Flying Head — Keita Higuchi, Jun Rekimoto (2013)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: The drone becomes a flying head: your body is the joystick, and your eyes move to where it is.
- What it is: A drone that copies the position and orientation of the pilot's head, so walking, crouching and turning become flying, seen through the drone's camera.
- How it works: Motion capture tracks the pilot's head and maps it to the quadcopter's pose; the drone's camera feed is shown in a head-mounted display.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2468356.2468721 (CHI 2013 Extended Abstracts)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9HLyZqhkNKg

#### Humanlike Robot Hands Controlled by Brain Activity — Maryam Alimardani, Hiroshi Ishiguro (2013)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, VR headset
- Idea: Ownership of a machine body can come from thought alone, with no muscle moving.
- What it is: Participants moved a pair of android hands only by imagining the movement, and came to feel the robot hands as their own.
- How it works: An EEG motor-imagery brain-computer interface drove the hands of a Geminoid android seen from a first-person view (likely through a head-mounted display); ownership was measured by questionnaire and skin conductance.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/srep02396 (Scientific Reports 2013)

#### Body Ownership Transfer to a Teleoperated Android — Shuichi Nishio, Hiroshi Ishiguro (2012)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Screen & web
- Idea: Simply moving in sync with a robot you see is enough for your body sense to transfer into it.
- What it is: Operators who teleoperated a Geminoid android while watching it reacted physically when its hand was threatened, showing they had come to feel the robot body as their own.
- How it works: Operators' motions were mirrored by the android while they watched it on a monitor; skin conductance was recorded when its hand was pinched with a needle, in synchronous versus delayed conditions.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-34103-8_40 (ICSR 2012 (LNCS))

#### Meet Your Creator — Marshmallow Laser Feast, Memo Akten (2012)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: Performance & participatory
- Shown at: Saatchi & Saatchi New Directors' Showcase, Cannes Lions 2012
- Idea: Drones, then known mainly as weapons, are recast as graceful performing bodies; the audience watches machine bodies rather than inhabiting them.
- What it is: A live 'robo-ballet' for the Saatchi & Saatchi New Directors' Showcase at Cannes Lions, in which a fleet of quadrotor drones carrying LEDs and motorised mirrors danced with moving-head spotlights to music by Oneohtrix Point Never.
- How it works: Custom quadrotors with LEDs and motorised mirrors, choreographed in a virtual animation system whose data drove the physical drones and automated spotlights in sync with the score.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/61227959
- Images: https://media.superradiance.net/cdn-cgi/image/width=2000,height=2000,fit=scale-down,format=auto,onerror=redirect/memotv/projects/2012/meet-your-creator/gallery-cannes-lions/01_meet_your_creator_02.jpg
- Project page: https://www.memo.tv/works/meet-your-creator/

#### TELESAR V — Susumu Tachi, Kouta Minamizawa (2012)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Touch & haptics, Altered vision, Hearing & vibration · Medium: VR headset, Wearable & sensory device
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH 2012 Emerging Technologies
- Idea: Telexistence is becoming a robot for real: your movements are its movements and its fingertips are your fingertips.
- What it is: A telexistence robot that an operator inhabits through a head-mounted display and haptic gloves, seeing, hearing and feeling textures and temperature through the robot's body.
- How it works: Head, arm and finger motion are captured and mapped to a 53-DoF anthropomorphic robot; stereo cameras, binaural microphones and fingertip force, vibration and thermal sensors stream back to the operator.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2343456.2343479 (SIGGRAPH 2012 Emerging Technologies)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZMF0p15GPYg

#### Machinarium — Amanita Design (2009)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Game
- Idea: A robot body with telescopic limbs becomes the main puzzle tool.
- What it is: A hand-drawn point-and-click adventure in which you play Josef, a small robot who stretches and shrinks his body to solve puzzles in a city of robots.
- How it works: Wordless 2D point-and-click game with hand-drawn art (PC, mobile, consoles).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uwZBdWRSBRs
- Images: https://cdn.akamai.steamstatic.com/steam/apps/40700/header.jpg
- Project page: https://store.steampowered.com/app/40700/

#### Geminoid HI-1 — Hiroshi Ishiguro (2007)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: Screen & web, Multisensory installation
- Idea: Operating your own robot double, you start to feel the android's body as yours and its presence as your presence.
- What it is: A lifelike android copy of Hiroshi Ishiguro that he, and others, teleoperate from a remote booth, speaking and moving through its face and body.
- How it works: The operator's face and head movements are tracked by camera and motion capture and replayed on the android's pneumatic actuators; voice is streamed through its speaker while monitors show its view.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.5772/4876 (Humanoid Robots: New Developments (2007))
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=fhS9KkOXDXE
- Project page: https://www.geminoid.jp

#### Muscle Machine — Stelarc (2003)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Performance & participatory, Multisensory installation
- Idea: A human leg lift becomes three machine legs swinging forward: the body is scaled up into a hybrid walker.
- What it is: A five-metre, six-legged walking robot powered by pneumatic rubber muscles, which Stelarc drives from inside by lifting his own legs and turning his torso.
- How it works: Encoders at the hip joints of a lower-body exoskeleton read the operator's leg and torso movement and drive fluidic muscle actuators on the robot's legs.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lkLZNXG55_8
- Project page: https://stelarc.org/_activity-20231.php

#### The Eighth Day — Eduardo Kac (2001)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Collective & networked · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: Arizona State University Art Museum 2001
- Idea: You look out through a robot whose body is steered by single-celled organisms, a shared body of human, machine and microbe.
