- What it is: Under a Plexiglas dome live glowing GFP plants, amoebae, fish and mice, plus a 'biobot' robot whose movement is driven by an amoeba colony; online visitors control the biobot's camera eye and see the dome from inside.
- How it works: Biobot's motors respond to amoeba-colony activity sensed inside it; its camera streams to the web, where viewers set the gaze.
- Images: https://www.ekac.org/8thday-general-nopublic.jpg
- Project page: https://www.ekac.org/8thday.html

#### Movatar — Stelarc (2000)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Performance & participatory, Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Instead of a human animating an avatar, the avatar possesses the human: the virtual body performs through the real one.
- What it is: An inverse motion-capture system in which a virtual avatar moves Stelarc's physical body through muscle stimulation, while his foot switches can influence the avatar's behaviour.
- How it works: An avatar's movements are sent to a muscle stimulation system on Stelarc's arms via a motion prosthesis worn on the upper body; foot switches let him alter the avatar's state.
- Images: https://stelarc.org/media/img/projects-overview/movatar2.jpg
- Project page: https://stelarc.org/_activity-20225.php

#### Exoskeleton — Stelarc (1998)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Performance & participatory
- Idea: Human arm gestures become the legs of a machine; the body is the driver inside a larger insect-like body.
- What it is: Stelarc stands on a six-legged pneumatic walking machine, steering it with arm gestures while an extended robotic arm opens and closes.
- How it works: Sensors on an upper-body exoskeleton translate arm gestures into ripple or tripod gaits of a pneumatic hexapod built with Tom Diekmann, Stefan Doepner and Gwendolin Taube (f18).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=R2MntBUwUxY
- Images: https://stelarc.org/media/img/projects-overview/exo2.jpg
- Project page: https://stelarc.org/_activity-20227.php

#### Rara Avis — Eduardo Kac (1996)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Hearing & vibration · Medium: VR headset, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Nexus Contemporary Art Center, Atlanta 1996
- Idea: Becoming a robot bird among living birds: the visitor is both an outsider and a strange member of the flock.
- What it is: Visitors put on a VR headset and see through the eyes of a telerobotic macaw perched in an aviary among thirty real birds, while internet users could also look and speak through it.
- How it works: Stereo cameras in the robot macaw's eyes were slaved to the visitor's head movements in a VR headset; the stream and a microphone channel were shared online.
- Images: https://www.ekac.org/rara.avis.jpg
- Project page: https://www.ekac.org/raraavis.html

#### The Telegarden — Ken Goldberg (1995)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Collective & networked, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: Ars Electronica Center, Linz (1996–2004)
- Idea: Thousands of people share one robot arm as their hand in the soil, and learn plant time through it.
- What it is: An online garden in which internet users planted, watered and watched seedlings by commanding an industrial robot arm, forming a community of remote gardeners.
- How it works: A web interface sent commands to an Adept robot arm over a circular garden bed; a camera on the arm returned images of the plants.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BCEC1tfc5Jc
- Images: https://goldberg.berkeley.edu/garden/telegarden-8x6-72dpi.jpg
- Project page: https://goldberg.berkeley.edu/garden/Ars/

#### Epizoo — Marcel·lí Antúnez Roca (1994)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Performance & participatory, Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: The artist becomes a machine operated by others, and the audience feels what it is to control a body.
- What it is: A performance in which the audience, using a computer interface, moves pneumatic devices strapped to the artist's nose, ears, mouth, chest and buttocks.
- How it works: A body-worn exoskeleton of pneumatic actuators is driven by a mouse-controlled interface, with animations of the body projected behind.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5utDR8VWxms

#### Ornitorrinco — Eduardo Kac (1989)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Idea: Telepresence as an artwork: to inhabit a small machine body in another city.
- What it is: A small telerobot that remote participants drove through gallery spaces using telephone keypads, seeing the world through its camera eye.
- How it works: Developed with Ed Bennett, the robot was controlled over phone lines and later the internet, sending back slow-scan or video images from its camera.
- Images: https://www.ekac.org/ornitorrinco.color.jpg
- Project page: https://www.ekac.org/ornitorrinco.html

### AI & machine perception

Seeing and hearing as neural networks, sensors and algorithms do.

#### City of Sparkles — Botao 'Amber' Hu, Reality Design Lab (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: VR headset
- Shown at: SIGGRAPH 2019 Immersive Pavilion; New Media Film Festival 2019; DTLA Film Festival 2019; IEEE VR 2025 XR Gallery; ISEA 2025 Seoul
- Idea: Embodying an AI means perceiving a city as sampled, clustered traces of people rather than as streets and buildings.
- What it is: A VR flight through New York rebuilt from four years of geotagged tweets, seen from the perspective of an AI that lives in a sea of human memory fragments; later rebuilt for Apple Vision Pro with bare-hand flying.
- How it works: Unity point-cloud city built from photogrammetry and geotagged Twitter data with sentiment-mapped colour and motion; first HTC Vive, later Apple Vision Pro hand tracking, with adaptive music.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3757369.3767604 (SIGGRAPH Asia 2025 Art Papers)
- Video: https://vimeo.com/777136892
- Images: https://reality.design/media/_resources/City%20Of%20Sparkles/project-city-of-sparkles-cover-01.jpg https://reality.design/media/_resources/City%20Of%20Sparkles/project-city-of-sparkles-figure-01.jpg https://arxiv.org/html/2511.12533v3/images/cityofsparkles.jpg
- Project page: https://reality.design/project/city-of-sparkles

#### ImageNet Roulette — Trevor Paglen, Kate Crawford (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Screen & web, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: Training Humans, Fondazione Prada, Milan 2019
- Idea: Seeing yourself as a machine classifies you exposes whose categories machine perception inherits.
- What it is: A web app and installation that labelled visitors' selfies with the 'person' categories of the ImageNet dataset, often with absurd or offensive results.
- How it works: A classifier trained on ImageNet's person subcategories labelled uploaded or camera-captured faces; part of the Training Humans exhibition.
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/5d6567d1afafe900010b2c70/1567267717980-E7VFIRCT4YB01IA8XAWT/apple_treachery.jpg
- Project page: https://excavating.ai/

#### Machine Hallucination — Refik Anadol (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Time & scale · Medium: Dome, CAVE & projection, Multisensory installation
- Shown at: ARTECHOUSE New York 2019
- Idea: Visitors step into a machine's memory of a city: not what it sees now, but how it recombines what it has seen.
- What it is: A room-scale projection that takes visitors through a neural network's latent space built from over 100 million photographs of New York City.
- How it works: Generative adversarial networks trained on public images of New York are rendered as a 360° projection with spatial sound.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=x1EVhNM-uf4
- Images: https://refikanadol.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/05/MH-Photo-02-2099x1400.jpg
- Project page: https://refikanadol.com/works/machine-hallucination/

#### Observation — No Code (2019)
- Type: Game · Senses: Altered vision, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Game
- Idea: You become the machine: perception is a grid of security cameras and a floating spherical drone.
- What it is: A sci-fi thriller game in which you play SAM, the artificial intelligence of a damaged space station, seeing only through its cameras and acting through its systems.
- How it works: Third-person camera-switching gameplay where the player controls station cameras, doors and a mobile sphere (PC, PS4).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FW3zuScqSHU
- Images: https://cdn.akamai.steamstatic.com/steam/apps/906100/header.jpg
- Project page: https://store.steampowered.com/app/906100/

#### SOMEONE — Lauren Lee McCarthy (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Multisensory installation, Performance & participatory
- Idea: Visitors become the machine intelligence in someone else's home and feel its power and intimacy.
- What it is: Gallery visitors take the role of a home assistant, watching real people's homes through laptops and responding when the residents call out 'Someone'.
- How it works: Live feeds from cameras and smart devices in volunteers' homes were routed to gallery laptops that could control lights, music and messages.
- Images: https://freight.cargo.site/w/1200/i/f6bc6a5b71efdbe13e0835b0d9f455b6fbee199b2576156c84eab0fdda89014a/_SP_6164-web.jpg
- Project page: https://lauren-mccarthy.com/SOMEONE

#### Perception Engines — Tom White (2018)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Idea: Art made for machine eyes: the audience sees the gap between human and machine perception.
- What it is: Abstract ink prints that look like loose scribbles to people but are recognised by neural networks as an electric fan, a cello or a tick.
- How it works: Drawings are optimised against an ensemble of ImageNet classifiers until they are strongly labelled, then screen-printed.
- Images: https://images.squarespace-cdn.com/content/v1/5c3518f55cfd7963746dd576/1609721536034-7SU963UJO2QP104FXYBO/cello_16_095_240_thumbnail.jpg
- Project page: https://drib.net/perception-engines

#### Autonomous Trap 001 — James Bridle (2017)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Performance & participatory, Multisensory installation
- Idea: Thinking like a machine's vision lets a human trap it with a simple sign; perception defines the machine's world.
- What it is: A circle of road markings drawn in salt around a self-driving car: a dashed line outside and a solid line inside, so the car's vision lets it enter but never leave.
- How it works: Salt road markings on Mount Parnassus, Greece, exploiting lane-detection rules, with a self-built autonomous car using computer vision.
- Images: https://jamesbridle.com/media/pages/works/autonomous-trap-001/329e6817a6-1680515123/1-autonomous-trap-001-003.jpg
- Project page: https://jamesbridle.com/works/autonomous-trap-001

#### Dragonfly Eyes (蜻蜓之眼) — Xu Bing (2017)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Altered vision, Collective & networked · Medium: Screen & web
- Shown at: Locarno Film Festival 2017; TIFF 2017
- Idea: A related screen work: the compound eye of a surveillance society, where people exist only as machines see them.
- What it is: A feature film edited entirely from about 10,000 hours of public surveillance footage from China, into which a fictional love story is written; the audience watches only through the fixed eyes of cameras.
- How it works: Footage collected from surveillance feeds streamed online, edited with voice actors into a narrative.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-ccfz77ifeU
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/-ccfz77ifeU/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://www.xubing.com

#### Hallucination Machine — Keisuke Suzuki, Anil Seth (2017)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, 360° / immersive film
- Idea: Seeing through a neural network's learned features feels like a psychedelic trip: machine perception made embodied.
- What it is: A VR platform that plays 360° video of a university campus processed by Deep Dream, letting people walk through the world as a neural network hallucinates it.
- How it works: Panoramic video was processed frame by frame with Deep Dream and shown in a head-mounted display; participants rated altered experience against psilocybin reports.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-017-16316-2 (Scientific Reports 2017)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Clk4rAj6YuY

#### LAUREN — Lauren Lee McCarthy (2017)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Altered vision, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Performance & participatory, Multisensory installation
- Idea: The artist becomes the AI: a human performs machine attention, and the residents feel what being attended to means.
- What it is: Lauren Lee McCarthy installed cameras, microphones and smart devices in people's homes and became their smart-home assistant, watching and controlling the house herself.
- How it works: Networked cameras, microphones, smart lights, locks and appliances were remotely monitored and operated by the artist for days at a time.
- Images: https://freight.cargo.site/w/1200/i/3834e708eefe30e8707e2fbfca24eff5bbf63d7f006f3c645c1cf44f0017ab5d/LAUREN2.jpg
- Project page: https://lauren-mccarthy.com/LAUREN

#### Learning to See — Memo Akten (2017)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Idea: A machine sees only what it already knows: perception as projection of past experience.
- What it is: A neural network watches a live camera feed of everyday objects on a table, cables, keys and cloth, and can only see them as the ocean, fire, flowers or galaxies it was trained on.
- How it works: Conditional generative networks (pix2pix-style) trained on single-subject image sets reinterpret a live webcam view in real time.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=DE3402nH1wA
- Images: https://media.superradiance.net/cdn-cgi/image/width=1200,fit=scale-down,format=auto,onerror=redirect/memotv/projects/2017/learning-to-see/images/learning-to-see.jpg
- Project page: https://www.memo.tv/works/learning-to-see/

#### Sight Machine — Trevor Paglen (2017)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Performance & participatory, Screen & web
- Shown at: Pier 70, San Francisco 2017; Holland Festival 2018
- Idea: The audience watches a concert twice: once with human eyes and once through the categories of machines.
- What it is: Kronos Quartet performs while machine-vision and AI algorithms analyse the musicians live, projecting what the algorithms see above the stage.
- How it works: Cameras feed face detection, object recognition and other computer-vision models whose outputs are rendered as live projections.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5mg3MXETfj4

#### In the Robot Skies — Liam Young (2016)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Screen & web
- Idea: The camera is a drone with its own flight logic: the story is seen from the machines' point of view.
- What it is: A short film shot entirely by autonomous drones, telling a love story between two teenagers on London housing estates who communicate through drones.
- How it works: Drones with pre-programmed and autonomous flight paths captured all footage; written with Tim Maughan.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cXfYyk0G5Hs
- Project page: https://liamyoung.org/projects/in-the-robot-skies

#### Where the City Can't See — Liam Young (2016)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Screen & web
- Shown at: Abandon Normal Devices (AND) Festival; The Invisible City, St Helens 2016
- Idea: The city appears as a self-driving car perceives it: a point cloud with blind spots where people can hide.
- What it is: The first fiction film shot entirely with laser scanners, following young factory workers through a smart city in a driverless taxi, seen as the car's sensors see.
- How it works: LiDAR scanners of the kind used by autonomous vehicles captured all scenes as point clouds; written by Tim Maughan.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/188626212
- Images: https://www.andfestival.org.uk/app/uploads/2018/01/Where-the-city-cant-see.jpg
- Project page: https://www.andfestival.org.uk/city-cant-see/

#### Inceptionism (DeepDream) — Alexander Mordvintsev (2015)
- Type: Book / theory · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Screen & web
- Idea: The first widely seen picture of a machine's way of seeing: perception pushed until it hallucinates.
- What it is: A Google Research post showing what image-recognition networks have learned by letting them amplify the patterns they detect, turning clouds into dogs and eyes.
- How it works: Gradient ascent on the activations of a trained convolutional network (GoogLeNet) modifies an input image to strengthen what the network detects.
- Images: https://storage.googleapis.com/gweb-research2023-media/images/7d982f4ad738241cdcdae235af13aca6-n.width-800.format-jpeg.jpg
- Project page: https://research.google/blog/inceptionism-going-deeper-into-neural-networks/

#### NeuralTalk and Walk — Kyle McDonald (2015)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Performance & participatory, Screen & web
- Idea: A walk narrated by a machine reveals what it notices and what it cannot see.
- What it is: Kyle McDonald walked through Amsterdam with a laptop whose neural network described, in live captions, everything its webcam saw.
- How it works: Andrej Karpathy's NeuralTalk2 image-captioning model ran on a laptop with a webcam, streaming captions to the screen.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/146492001
- Project page: https://kylemcdonald.net/

#### The Drone Aviary — Superflux (2015)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Idea: Looking through drones' eyes makes their quiet sorting of people and places visible.
- What it is: An installation of speculative drones and a film that shows a city through their eyes, overlaid with the data they collect and the judgements they make.
- How it works: Custom drone props and a film using drone footage with machine-vision graphic overlays.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dXgHG9GfWwc
- Images: https://superflux.in/wp-content/uploads/2016/11/adrone.jpg
- Project page: https://superflux.in/index.php/work/drones/

#### Cloud Face — Shinseungback Kimyonghun (2012)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: Ars Electronica Center 2014–2021
- Idea: Seeing like a machine: its errors resemble human pareidolia, yet the machine believes what it sees.
- What it is: A collection of photographs of clouds in which a face-detection algorithm found human faces; visitors compare what the machine sees with the faces they imagine.
- How it works: Camera and a face-detection algorithm with custom software; pigment prints.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QUEVeL4Y9-M
- Images: https://djhznh41oxwef.cloudfront.net/works/cloud_face/resized/ssbkyh_detail1.png
- Project page: http://ssbkyh.com/works/cloud_face/

#### Robot Readable World — Timo Arnall (2012)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Screen & web
- Idea: The world redrawn for robots: a catalogue of how machines read space.
- What it is: A short film assembled from computer-vision footage, showing streets, faces and objects as tracking boxes, feature points and depth maps.
- How it works: Found footage from computer-vision research and demos, edited into a single film.
- Images: https://www.elasticspace.com/images/robot-readable-world/robot-readable-world-poster.jpg
- Project page: https://www.elasticspace.com/2012/02/robot-readable-world

#### Eye/Machine I–III — Harun Farocki (2001)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Idea: Operational images are not made to be looked at by people; watching them is watching machine perception at work.
- What it is: A three-part video installation built from images made by machines for machines: smart-bomb cameras, industrial robots and computer-vision systems.
- How it works: Two-channel video essays compiled from military, industrial and research footage of machine vision (2001–2003).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=r4sDXhHqndk
- Project page: https://www.harunfarocki.de/installations/2000s/2001/eye-machine.html

#### Floating Eye — Hiroo Iwata (2000)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Body schema & movement · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, Multisensory installation
- Idea: Vision is handed to a floating machine: you steer your body from a bird's- or drone's-eye position outside it.
- What it is: A camera hangs from a small blimp floating above the participant, who wears a dome-shaped display and walks while seeing only the blimp's wide-angle view, looking down on their own body.
- How it works: Helium blimp tethered to the participant carries a wide-angle camera whose video is shown in a head-worn hemispherical screen.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=In-M1eVAnYc

### Objects & things

Becoming a chair, a stone, a tool: object-oriented experience.

#### Performing Textiles — Kawita Vatanajyankur (2020)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics, Time & scale · Medium: Performance & participatory, Screen & web
- Idea: The body as machine: repetitive industrial motion is felt in human muscles, making invisible garment labour visible.
- What it is: A video series in which the artist performs the steps of textile production with her body — spinning, weaving, dyeing — acting as the loom, spindle or shuttle herself.
- How it works: Endurance performances filmed in single takes, often with assistants operating threads and frames around her body.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CMo2hKE-PYk
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/CMo2hKE-PYk/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CMo2hKE-PYk

#### All That Perishes at the Edge of Land — Hira Nabi (2019)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Hearing & vibration · Medium: Screen & web
- Shown at: Cambridge Film Festival 2019; Prince Claus Fund 25 Years 25 Hours
- Idea: A related screen work, included because the ship itself narrates: a machine body on its deathbed addresses the humans who unmake it.
- What it is: A film shot at the Gadani ship-breaking yards in Pakistan in which Ocean Master, a decommissioned container ship, speaks with the workers who are taking it apart about home, dreams and the violence of dismantling.
- How it works: Documentary footage of the yards combined with a scripted, voiced dialogue attributed to the ship.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ikpa-f2cnUg
- Project page: https://www.newday.com/film/all-perishes-edge-land

#### The Quest — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2019)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Smell & taste, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Idea: A brand commission with no nonhuman perspective; listed for completeness as a machine-choreographed object that stands in for taste.
- What it is: A kinetic light sculpture commissioned by Moët Hennessy for Hennessy Paradis Impérial, translating the blending of eaux-de-vie into choreographed light.
- How it works: Motorised kinetic light sculpture with custom control software (details not published).
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AcYYP_vwuIc
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2025/10/Hennessy__0001_Layer-42.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/the-quest/

#### Donut County — Ben Esposito (2018)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Becoming absence: the player is a hole, defined only by what falls into it.
- What it is: A physics puzzle game in which you are a hole in the ground that grows as it swallows objects, people and eventually whole buildings.
- How it works: A moving hole that cuts through the physics floor and grows with each swallowed object.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jei2tYpNvcw
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/702670/header.jpg
- Project page: http://donutcounty.com

#### Morse Things — Ron Wakkary (2017)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Collective & networked, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Idea: Things with lives of their own: designing objects whose world is not centred on us.
- What it is: A set of ceramic bowls and cups that live in people's homes and talk to each other in Morse code over the home network, mostly ignoring their human owners.
- How it works: Ceramic vessels with embedded single-board computers exchange Morse messages over WiFi and post to a Twitter account; deployed in homes.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3064663.3064734 (DIS 2017)

#### Thing Ethnography — Elisa Giaccardi (2016)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Altered vision, Collective & networked · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Things become co-ethnographers: an object's viewpoint reveals practices people do not notice.
- What it is: Design researchers attached cameras and sensors to household things such as kettles, cups and fridges to see everyday life from the things' point of view.
- How it works: Autographer cameras and sensors mounted on objects captured images and use data, analysed with participants.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2901790.2901905 (DIS 2016)

#### Affordance++ — Pedro Lopes (2015)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Taking the object's side: its behaviour is expressed through your body.
- What it is: Objects communicate how they want to be used by moving the user's muscles: a spray can makes you shake it, a door handle tells you it is hot.
- How it works: Electrical muscle stimulation on the forearm, triggered by object recognition and tracking, actuates wrist and finger movements.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2702123.2702128 (CHI 2015)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Gz4dphzBb6I

#### Body-ownership for Actively Operated Non-corporeal Objects — Ke Ma, Bernhard Hommel (2015)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Screen & web
- Idea: Control, not shape, makes a thing feel like yours: an object can be embodied if it answers to your movement.
- What it is: People came to feel a virtual balloon or rectangle as part of their body when it moved in sync with their own hand.
- How it works: A data glove drove virtual objects on screen (likely a projection-based virtual-hand setup), comparing synchronous and asynchronous movement with ownership questionnaires.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.concog.2015.06.003 (Consciousness and Cognition 2015)

#### Cape Mongo — Francois Knoetze (2015)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Performance & participatory, Wearable & sensory device, Screen & web
- Shown at: LagosPhoto; KLEX 2015
- Idea: The performer's body becomes discarded matter itself, retracing the life of consumer objects from their own point of view.
- What it is: A series of five short films in which the artist wears sculptural suits made entirely of Cape Town's discarded waste (plastic, paper, glass, metal, VHS tape) and walks as these 'trash creatures' back through the places of their material past.
- How it works: Five wearable sculptures built from sorted waste streams are performed in public space and filmed as short videos, one per material.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=04Hpd_cfUSw
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=04Hpd_cfUSw

#### I Am Bread — Bossa Studios (2015)
- Type: Game · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Game, Screen & web
- Idea: Object embodiment through awkward control: each corner is a limb, and the kitchen becomes terrain.
- What it is: A physics game in which you are a slice of bread trying to become toast, moving by lifting and planting each of its four corners.
- How it works: Each corner mapped to a separate button or trigger with grip, over a physics-simulated slice that can get dirty or damp.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MwDhUt5ewKk
- Images: https://shared.akamai.steamstatic.com/store_item_assets/steam/apps/327890/header.jpg
- Project page: http://www.iambreadgame.com

#### Tools — Kawita Vatanajyankur (2015)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Performance & participatory, Screen & web
- Idea: Becoming an object exposes how women's bodies are used as instruments of domestic labour.
- What it is: A series of brightly coloured videos in which the artist uses her own body as household tools — hanging as a scale loaded with fruit, sweeping as a broom, scooping as a dustpan — until the strain shows.
- How it works: Single-take studio performances filmed against saturated colour backdrops.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Rqew7gYP4C0
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/Rqew7gYP4C0/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Rqew7gYP4C0

#### The Uncomfortable — Katerina Kamprani (2011)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: Screen & web
- Idea: Breaking the fit between object and body makes us feel the object's own form and demands.
- What it is: A series of everyday objects redesigned to be deliberately inconvenient: a fork made of chain, a watering can that pours on your feet, a chair you cannot sit in.
- How it works: Mostly 3D-rendered images of redesigned objects, with a few physical prototypes.
- Images: https://www.theuncomfortable.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/05/chain_fork_01_p-655x437.jpg https://www.theuncomfortable.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/Hoop-Chair-04-655x655.jpg
- Project page: https://www.theuncomfortable.com/

#### Projecting Sensations to External Objects — K. Carrie Armel, V. S. Ramachandran (2003)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Idea: The body can extend into a plain object: ownership of a table, not just a rubber hand.
- What it is: When a table was stroked in sync with the participant's hidden hand, people felt the touch on the table and reacted physiologically when it was 'hurt'.
- How it works: A rubber-hand-style paradigm in which the tabletop and the hidden hand were stroked in synchrony, after which the table was threatened while skin conductance was recorded.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1098/rspb.2003.2364 (Proceedings of the Royal Society B 2003)

#### Seeking Comfort in an Uncomfortable Chair — Bruno Munari (1944)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Screen & web, Performance & participatory
- Idea: The body bends to the object: a playful study of how a chair shapes the person who uses it.
- What it is: A sequence of fourteen photographs in Domus in which Munari twists into ever odder positions in a modern armchair, searching for a comfortable way to sit.
- How it works: Staged photographic series published in Domus no. 202 (1944).
- Images: https://www.domusweb.it/content/dam/domusweb/en/from-the-archive/2012/03/31/searching-for-comfort-in-an-uncomfortable-chair/domus-munariarchivio.png.foto.rbig.png
- Project page: https://www.domusweb.it/en/from-the-archive/2012/03/31/searching-for-comfort-in-an-uncomfortable-chair.html

### Cyborg & new senses

Devices that add senses humans lack: magnetic north, infrared, seismic or data senses.

#### Cyborg Botany — Harpreet Sareen (2019)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Touch & haptics, Electroreception · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Idea: Not a robot next to a plant but circuitry inside it: the plant's own body becomes the interface.
- What it is: Plants are augmented with conductive material grown inside their tissue so that the living plant can act as a sensor, display and actuator.
- How it works: Conductive polymers are introduced into the plant's vascular tissue to form in-vivo wires, combined with the plant's natural signals and movements.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3290607.3311778 (CHI 2019 Extended Abstracts)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3fsE_c__zV4
- Images: https://dam-prod.media.mit.edu/thumb/2019/05/08/titlegid-nosub.gif.1400x1400.gif
- Project page: https://www.media.mit.edu/projects/cyborg-botany/overview/

#### Elowan — Harpreet Sareen (2018)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Altered vision, Electroreception · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Idea: The plant gets a machine body: its bioelectric response to light becomes locomotion.
- What it is: A potted plant on a robotic base that uses its own electrical signals to drive itself toward light.
- How it works: Electrodes on the leaves read the plant's bioelectrical responses to light; an amplifier and microcontroller turn them into motor commands for a wheeled base.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rptKlKZc7cs
- Images: https://dam-prod.media.mit.edu/thumb/2018/11/01/BannerImage_v01.jpg.1400x1400.jpg
- Project page: https://www.media.mit.edu/projects/elowan-a-plant-robot-hybrid/overview/

#### Sensory Augmentation with an Auditory Compass — Frank Schumann, J. Kevin O'Regan (2017)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Magnetic sense, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, Spatial audio
- Idea: An artificial sense does not stay separate; it gets absorbed into the body's own sense of orientation.
- What it is: People who wore an auditory compass signal for a period integrated it with their vestibular sense of rotation, showing a new sense merging with an old one.
- How it works: Headphones played a sound signal encoding the direction of north during a training period; perceived self-rotation was tested before and after (likely with passive rotation in darkness).
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/srep42197 (Scientific Reports 2017)

#### Weather Fins — Manel De Aguas (2017)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Breath & interoception · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, Performance & participatory
- Idea: The weather becomes a felt, continuous sense, closer to how many animals track the air.
- What it is: Fins worn on the sides of Manel De Aguas's head sense atmospheric pressure, humidity and temperature and let him hear the weather as sound.
- How it works: Environmental sensors in the fins drive sound transmitted by bone conduction; he also uses the data to compose music.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hSo_cZhyRA4

#### Corpus Nil — Marco Donnarumma (2016)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Performance & participatory, Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Body and algorithm shape each other; the performer is a hybrid organism with a machine.
- What it is: A performance in which an AI system listens to the sounds and electrical signals of Donnarumma's muscles and turns them into light and sound, as the body slowly contorts.
- How it works: Wearable mechanomyography and electromyography sensors feed a machine learning system that controls sound synthesis and lights in real time.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CAIh8FxLMtk
- Images: https://marcodonnarumma.com/live/wp-content/uploads/2017/03/Marco-Donnarumma_Corpus-Nil_1_web_by-onuk.jpg
- Project page: https://marcodonnarumma.com/works/corpus-nil/

#### Long-term Sensory Augmentation with a Magnetic Belt — Peter König (2016)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Magnetic sense, Touch & haptics · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Learning a new sense is learning new sensorimotor contingencies, and it reshapes both experience and brain activity.
- What it is: A seven-week study of people wearing the feelSpace belt, combining behaviour, subjective reports and brain imaging to track how a new sense is learned.
- How it works: Participants wore the vibrotactile compass belt daily for seven weeks; brain imaging, behavioural tasks and questionnaires were compared with controls.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0166647 (PLOS ONE 2016)

#### North Sense — Cyborg Nest (2016)
- Type: Product · Senses: Magnetic sense, Touch & haptics · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Sold as a sense rather than a gadget: the aim is a direction sense that becomes part of perception.
- What it is: A small silicone device attached to the chest with piercings that vibrates whenever the wearer faces magnetic north.
- How it works: A magnetometer and vibration motor in a silicone housing, fixed by two titanium bar piercings.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0xPMpKxd1R8

#### Architecture of Radio — Richard Vijgen (2015)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Electroreception · Medium: AR
- Idea: Seeing the radio spectrum around you turns infrastructure into a perceivable environment.
- What it is: A site-specific AR app that shows the cell towers, WiFi routers and satellites whose signals surround the viewer, as a 360° data landscape.
- How it works: The phone's location and orientation sensors place data from open databases of cell towers, WiFi networks and satellites around the user.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XUKc0Hrxn6s
- Images: https://www.architectureofradio.com/img/thumb/1.jpg
- Project page: https://www.architectureofradio.com/

#### Hortum machina, B — Interactive Architecture Lab (2015)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Electroreception, Time & scale · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Shown at: The Bartlett BPro Show 2015
- Idea: A garden becomes a cyborg vehicle: plants choose where their machine body goes.
- What it is: A geodesic sphere planted as a garden that can roll through the city, steered by the electrical signals of the plants it carries.
- How it works: Electrophysiological sensors on the plants feed a controller that shifts the sphere's weight to roll it; part of the reEarth project at The Bartlett BPro.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8Qk3BeolyiA

#### VEST (Versatile Extra-Sensory Transducer) — David Eagleman (2015)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Touch & haptics, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: The skin can carry a new channel of the world: data becomes a felt sense after training.
- What it is: A vibrating vest that turns sound into patterns on the torso so deaf people can learn to understand speech through the skin; Eagleman proposed feeding it any data stream as a new sense.
- How it works: An array of vibrotactile motors on a vest encodes audio frequencies spatially and temporally; the paper estimates how much information the skin can carry.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1007/s00221-015-4346-1 (Experimental Brain Research 2015)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kbKzF8gKxT4

#### Aposematic Jacket — Shinseungback Kimyonghun (2014)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Body schema & movement · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Borrowing an animal defence strategy: many eyes on the skin, like the eyespots of moths and butterflies.
- What it is: A jacket covered in camera lenses that, like the warning colours of animals, signals 'I can record you'; under threat the wearer presses a button and it records a 360° image and sends it online.
- How it works: Lenses, cameras, Raspberry Pi, Wi-Fi module and battery sewn into fabric.
- Video: https://vimeo.com/104411181
- Images: https://djhznh41oxwef.cloudfront.net/works/aposematic_jacket/resized/ssbkyh_aposematic_jacket_01.png
- Project page: http://ssbkyh.com/works/aposematic_jacket/

#### Phantom Terrains — Frank Swain (2014)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Electroreception · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, Spatial audio
- Idea: A medical device becomes an organ for the invisible electromagnetic city.
- What it is: Frank Swain's hearing aids were modified to stream the surrounding WiFi landscape as sound, letting him hear networks, their strength and their names as he walks.
- How it works: An iPhone app with sound artist Daniel Jones sonifies WiFi router data (signal, channel, encryption, names) and streams it to Bluetooth hearing aids.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=v-vpR3FmxWU

#### Seismic Sense — Moon Ribas (2013)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Touch & haptics, Time & scale · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: A body tuned to the planet: she senses the Earth moving all the time, far beyond the reach of human perception.
- What it is: Implanted vibration sensors that let Moon Ribas feel earthquakes happening anywhere on Earth, their strength felt as vibration intensity.
- How it works: Online seismograph data is sent to vibrating implants, first in her arm and later in her feet.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MdDfAdSeRNQ
- Images: https://images.hoobaweb.com/8930/imgf-1200-630/moon-ribas.png
- Project page: https://www.moonribas.com/

#### SpiderSense — Victor Mateevitsi (2013)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: A whole-body proximity sense, like a spider feeling vibration, extends the skin into space.
- What it is: A suit with sensor modules that press on the skin when objects approach, letting blindfolded wearers sense their surroundings and even throw at targets.
- How it works: Ultrasonic distance sensors distributed around the body drive servo-actuated pressure pads, closer objects pressing harder.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/2459236.2459246 (Augmented Human 2013)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=D2BZ5wCTwGU

#### Waiting for Earthquakes — Moon Ribas (2013)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement, Time & scale · Medium: Performance & participatory
- Idea: The planet is the choreographer: a performance timed and scored by the Earth's movements.
- What it is: A dance performance in which Moon Ribas stands still until her seismic sense detects an earthquake, then translates its intensity into movement.
- How it works: Real-time earthquake data from her seismic sense implants cues improvised movement on stage.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1Un4MFR-vNI

#### Immaterials: Light Painting WiFi — Timo Arnall (2011)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Electroreception · Medium: Performance & participatory, Screen & web
- Idea: Making a machine sense visible in the city lets people see radio the way devices do.
- What it is: A four-metre rod of lights that measures WiFi signal strength was walked through Oslo and photographed in long exposures, revealing the shape of invisible networks.
- How it works: A rod of 80 LEDs displays measured WiFi signal strength as bar height while moved through streets during long-exposure photography; with Jørn Knutsen and Einar Sneve Martinussen.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1111/j.1740-9713.2013.00683.x (Significance 2013)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cxdjfOkPu-E
- Project page: https://www.elasticspace.com/2013/09/the-immaterials-project

#### PossessedHand — Emi Tamaki, Jun Rekimoto (2011)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Being moved by a computer from inside your own muscles: the hand becomes a machine output.
- What it is: A forearm band that moves the wearer's fingers with electrical muscle stimulation, so a computer can play the hand like an instrument.
- How it works: Twenty-eight electrode pads on the forearm stimulate muscles to flex 16 finger joints.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/1978942.1979018 (CHI 2011)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9XBoZyfB8hY

#### Eyeborg — Rob Spence (2009)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, Screen & web
- Idea: The eye literally becomes a camera: seeing and recording collapse into one organ.
- What it is: Filmmaker Rob Spence replaced his lost eye with a prosthetic eye containing a wireless camera, recording and transmitting what it sees.
- How it works: A miniature low-resolution camera, battery and wireless transmitter fit inside an ocular prosthesis, streaming video to a receiver.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_8fFj4-CKY8

#### North Paw — Eric Boyd (2008)
- Type: Product · Senses: Magnetic sense, Touch & haptics · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: A magnetic sense anyone can solder: umwelt expansion as a hobby kit.
- What it is: An open-source anklet with a ring of vibration motors that buzzes on the side facing north, sold as a DIY kit.
- How it works: A compass module drives eight vibration motors around the ankle; inspired by the feelSpace belt.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=D4shfNufqSg

#### electric stimulus to face — Daito Manabe (2008)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Performance & participatory, Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: A face becomes a display for a machine signal, performing expressions no one intended.
- What it is: Electrodes on the faces of Daito Manabe and friends were driven by music, making their facial muscles twitch in time with the beat.
- How it works: Audio signals are converted to low-current electrical muscle stimulation delivered through electrode pads on the face.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJ4_-2_oWuM

#### Ear on Arm — Stelarc (2006)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Body schema & movement · Medium: Performance & participatory
- Idea: An extra organ that listens for others: the body grows a part that serves the network rather than its owner.
- What it is: A human-cell-scaffolded ear surgically constructed on Stelarc's left forearm, intended to be wired as an internet-connected listening organ.
- How it works: A porous biopolymer scaffold was implanted under the skin and grown into with tissue; a miniature microphone was once inserted and later removed after infection.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=MhpV4sLgVEE
- Images: https://stelarc.org/media/img/projects-overview/earonarm2.jpg
- Project page: https://stelarc.org/_activity-20242.php

#### Haptic Radar — Alvaro Cassinelli (2006)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Skin that reaches out into space: like whiskers or antennae for humans.
- What it is: A headband of rangefinders and vibration motors that lets the wearer feel approaching objects around the head, an 'extended skin' that works without sight.
- How it works: Infrared rangefinders on modular units map distance to vibration intensity on the scalp.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/iswc.2006.286344 (ISWC 2006)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ow_RISC2S0A
- Images: http://ishikawa-vision.org/perception/HapticRadar/DSC_8196.jpg
- Project page: https://www.ishikawa-vision.org/perception/HapticRadar/index-e.html

#### feelSpace Belt: Learning the Sixth Sense — Peter König (2005)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Magnetic sense, Touch & haptics · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: A magnetic sense can be learned: with time the belt stops being a signal and becomes part of how space feels.
- What it is: Participants wore a vibrating belt that always indicated magnetic north for six weeks, and some reported that their perception of space changed.
- How it works: A belt of 13 vibrotactile motors with an electronic compass continuously vibrates the element pointing north; navigation tests and interviews assessed learning.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1088/1741-2560/2/4/r02 (Journal of Neural Engineering 2005)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0po8YOA-17U
- Images: https://feelspace.de/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/naviBelt_Features-e1647970100122.jpg

#### Cyborg Antenna (Eyeborg) — Neil Harbisson (2004)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, Performance & participatory
- Idea: A permanent new sense changes identity: Harbisson describes himself as a cyborg and his antenna as an organ.
- What it is: An antenna osseointegrated into Neil Harbisson's skull turns light frequencies into vibrations he hears through bone, letting him perceive colour, including infrared and ultraviolet.
- How it works: A camera sensor at the tip of the antenna maps hue to sound frequency, transmitted by bone conduction through the skull implant; an internet link lets others send him colours.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ygRNoieAnzI
- Project page: https://www.cyborgarts.com/

#### Electrical Walks — Christina Kubisch (2004)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Electroreception · Medium: Spatial audio, Performance & participatory
- Idea: Walking with an electromagnetic ear reveals a hidden layer of the city that other senses miss.
- What it is: Walkers wear special headphones that turn the electromagnetic fields of the city, from shop alarms to ATMs and lighting, into sound, following a map of hot spots.
- How it works: Wireless headphones with induction coils pick up and amplify electromagnetic fields directly as audio.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=gUYhwXteYP8
- Images: https://christinakubisch.de/wp-content/uploads/2022/01/MG_2068-2-1024x682.jpeg
- Project page: https://www.christinakubisch.de/electrical-walks

#### Project Cyborg 2.0 — Kevin Warwick (2002)
- Type: Research prototype · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: The nervous system wired straight to machines: a body that can extend across the internet and feel distance ultrasonically.
- What it is: Kevin Warwick had an electrode array implanted in his arm's median nerve, controlling a robot hand over the internet and receiving ultrasonic distance signals as a new sense.
- How it works: A 100-electrode Utah array in the median nerve recorded and stimulated nerve signals, linked to a robot hand, an ultrasonic sensor and later to his wife's implant.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=GLq7edATaFo
- Images: https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/Kevin_Warwick_2011.jpg/1280px-Kevin_Warwick_2011.jpg

#### Ping Body — Stelarc (1996)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement, Collective & networked · Medium: Performance & participatory, Wearable & sensory device
- Shown at: Dutch Electronic Art Festival (DEAF96), Rotterdam
- Idea: A body can be actuated by the network itself, becoming a sensor and puppet of global data traffic.
- What it is: Stelarc's muscles were moved by the flow of internet data: ping times to servers around the world drove a muscle stimulation system that made his limbs twitch and gesture.
- How it works: Ping round-trip times to dozens of global servers were mapped to voltages on a muscle stimulation system attached to his arms and legs; his body movements in turn controlled the Third Hand.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=45MOlsFGSHI

#### Stomach Sculpture — Stelarc (1993)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Breath & interoception, Body schema & movement · Medium: Performance & participatory
- Shown at: Fifth Australian Sculpture Triennale 1993
- Idea: The body becomes a site that hosts a machine, its hollow interior an exhibition space.
- What it is: A capsule-sized sculpture of gold, silver and steel was swallowed and opened inside Stelarc's stomach, filmed with a medical endoscope.
- How it works: A servo-driven worm-screw mechanism tethered to an external control box opens and closes the sculpture; an endoscope records the view inside.
- Images: https://stelarc.org/media/img/projects-overview/stomach-sculpture1.jpg
- Project page: https://stelarc.org/_activity-20349.php

#### The vOICe — Peter Meijer (1992)
- Type: Product · Senses: Hearing & vibration, Altered vision · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Vision is re-routed through hearing; the long-term training of users shows how the brain adopts a new sensory channel.
- What it is: A sensory-substitution system that turns camera images into soundscapes: each image is scanned left to right, with height heard as pitch and brightness as loudness, so users learn to 'see' with their ears.
- How it works: Software image-to-sound mapping running on PCs, phones and camera glasses, freely distributed since the 1990s.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1109/10.121642 (IEEE Transactions on Biomedical Engineering 1992)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=I0lmSYP7OcM
- Project page: https://www.seeingwithsound.com

#### Third Hand — Stelarc (1980)
- Type: Performance · Senses: Body schema & movement, Touch & haptics · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, Performance & participatory
- Idea: A third hand is not a tool you hold but a limb you learn to move with muscles that never moved a hand.
- What it is: A mechanical hand attached to Stelarc's right arm, driven by electrical signals from his abdominal and leg muscles, performed with for nearly two decades.
- How it works: EMG electrodes on abdominal and leg muscles switch a latex-covered aluminium hand to pinch, grasp and rotate its wrist; built in Yokohama from a Waseda University prototype.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Or1CVc6A9YA
- Images: https://stelarc.org/media/img/projects-overview/3rdhand2.jpg
- Project page: https://stelarc.org/_activity-20265.php

#### Tactile Vision Substitution System (TVSS) — Paul Bach-y-Rita (1969)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Touch & haptics, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Seeing is done by the brain, not the eye: a camera and the skin of the back can form a new route to vision.
- What it is: Blind participants sat in a chair whose back pressed a grid of vibrating pins against their skin, driven by a camera they moved, and learned to recognise objects and faces through touch.
- How it works: A television camera's image was converted into a 20 × 20 array of vibrotactile stimulators on the back of a dental chair; active camera control was key to learning.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/221963a0 (Nature 1969)

#### Electric Dress — Atsuko Tanaka (1956)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Body schema & movement, Heat & infrared · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, Performance & participatory
- Shown at: 2nd Gutai Art Exhibition, Tokyo 1956; documenta 12, Kassel 2007
- Idea: A precursor of cyborg wearables: the body becomes an electrical apparatus, with the risk and heat that implies.
- What it is: A dress made of about 200 painted light bulbs and tubes that flashed in sequence; Tanaka wore it at the Gutai exhibition, becoming a hot, blinking electrical body.
- How it works: Coloured incandescent bulbs and neon tubes wired to a sequencing controller, worn on the body.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KE_H6xgwqWw
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/KE_H6xgwqWw/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=tJpldOY-w50

## Foundations

Theory, surveys, critiques and methods behind nonhuman experience in XR: umwelt, embodiment science, the empathy-machine debate.

### Theory & philosophy

Umwelt, what it is like to be a bat, becoming-animal, arts of noticing.

#### Envisioning Posthuman/More-than-Human Futures for XR through HCI — Jae-eun Shin (2026)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Multisensory · Medium: VR headset, Mixed reality
- Idea: XR can be read as speculative worlding, distributed decentering and entangled becoming, rather than as a tool for human immersion.
- What it is: A CHI paper that critically reorients XR research away from human-centred presence and toward posthuman and more-than-human futures.
- How it works: Critical review of HCI and XR literature through posthumanist theory, proposing a taxonomy linking space, body and interaction.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3772318.3790771 (CHI 2026)
- Project page: https://doi.org/10.1145/3772318.3790771

#### Experiencing More-than-Humans: Augmented Noticing through Extended Reality — Botao 'Amber' Hu, Danlin Huang, Jae-eun Shin (2026)
- Type: Book / theory · Senses: Multisensory · Medium: Mixed reality, Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: No headset delivers another being's interiority; what XR can do is make the attempt and its failure perceptible, training attention to one's own attention.
- What it is: A practice-led monograph in preparation, built on an annotated portfolio of XR works that attempt to become bat, mole, octopus, fungi, creek, chair, AI and robot.
- How it works: Research through design, soma design and the annotated-portfolio method, with a 'noticing score' exercise closing each case chapter.
- Images: https://opengraph.githubassets.com/791ab569841ef1212cd12ab29d208927da6dcddc431cfa9b242f4d9b9c95e9c3/experiencing-mth/experiencing-mth.github.io
- Project page: https://experiencing-mth.github.io
- Code: https://github.com/experiencing-mth

#### Plausible Embodiment — Shuto Takashita (2026)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Plausibility, not realism, is the goal when designing a body humans do not have.
- What it is: A design framework for embodying non-human avatars that aims for bodies that feel plausible to control rather than realistic copies of animals.
- How it works: Conceptual framework drawn from the author's tentacle and supernumerary-limb studies.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3772363.3799215 (CHI 2026 Extended Abstracts)

#### Non-Anthropomorphic Hands — Jennifer Molnar (2022)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Truly non-human bodies need control schemes invented for them.
- What it is: A position paper on hands that differ from ours in topology and motion, and why one-to-one finger mapping limits them.
- How it works: Review of real and virtual non-anthropomorphic hands and their control strategies.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3491101.3519871 (CHI 2022 Extended Abstracts)

#### Morphological Interfaces: On Body Transforming Technologies — Sang-won Leigh (2017)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: The self is plastic; interfaces can change the body, not only the screen.
- What it is: A paper on technologies that change the shape and capacities of the body, from extra robotic fingers to reconfigured limbs.
- How it works: Position paper drawing on tool-use neuroscience and the authors' body-extending robots.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3027063.3052758 (CHI 2017 Extended Abstracts)
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=V6CMtXQj_Ts

#### Ways of Machine Seeing — Geoff Cox (2017)
- Type: Book / theory · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: Screen & web
- Idea: Machine seeing is also a way of seeing, shaped by power; any 'becoming machine' inherits it.
- What it is: An essay that updates John Berger's Ways of Seeing for an age in which images are increasingly made and read by machines.
- How it works: Critical essay in the journal APRJA, drawing on computer vision and Berger's television series.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.7146/aprja.v6i1.116007 (APRJA 2017)

#### Homuncular Flexibility: Inhabiting Nonhuman Avatars — Stanford Virtual Human Interaction Lab (2015)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: Nonhuman avatars are not a trick of rendering but a test of neural plasticity.
- What it is: An essay setting out homuncular flexibility, the idea that the brain's body map can adapt to bodies with extra or differently moving appendages.
- How it works: Theoretical synthesis of neuroscience, VR experiments and Lanier's early avatar work.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1002/9781118900772.etrds0165 (Emerging Trends in the Social and Behavioral Sciences 2015)

#### What Is It Like to Be a Bat? — Thomas Nagel (1974)
- Type: Book / theory · Senses: Echolocation · Medium: Screen & web
- Idea: The question almost every becoming-bat work sets out to answer, or to fail at honestly.
- What it is: A philosophy essay arguing that we cannot know what it is like to be a bat, because its experience is tied to a sonar sense we lack.
- How it works: Philosophical argument on subjective experience and physicalism.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.2307/2183914 (The Philosophical Review 1974)

#### A Stroll Through the Worlds of Animals and Men — Jakob von Uexküll (1934)
- Type: Book / theory · Senses: Multisensory · Medium: Screen & web
- Idea: Every animal lives in its own perceptual world; becoming another animal means entering its Umwelt.
- What it is: An illustrated book describing the Umwelt, the world as perceived and acted on by each animal, from the tick to the dog.
- How it works: Biological and philosophical account with illustrations comparing human and animal perception of the same scenes.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1515/semi.1992.89.4.319 (Semiotica 1992 (English translation; German original 1934))
- Images: https://minnesota-us.imgix.net/covers/9780816659005.jpg?w=298
- Project page: https://www.upress.umn.edu/9780816659005/a-foray-into-the-worlds-of-animals-and-humans/

### Embodiment science

Body ownership, avatars, homuncular flexibility and presence research.

#### Can Non-Humanlike Avatars Induce the Proteus Effect? — Xinmiao Lan (2023)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: The Proteus effect holds beyond human form, but depends on identifying with the body.
- What it is: An experiment testing whether the attractiveness of a non-humanlike avatar changes social participation, through identification and embodiment.
- How it works: Between-subjects VR study with attractive and unattractive non-humanlike avatars and mediation analysis.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.chbah.2023.100020 (Computers in Human Behavior: Artificial Humans 2023)

#### The Machine to Be Another — BeAnotherLab (2012)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: VR headset, Performance & participatory
- Idea: The human-to-human body swap: a baseline method, and a counterpoint, for works that try to swap into non-human bodies.
- What it is: Two participants wearing headsets see through each other's eyes and move in sync, experiencing the illusion of swapping bodies.
- How it works: Head-mounted displays show a live first-person camera view from the other participant, with a performer mirroring movements and synchronised touch.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eIQWcmhChBI
- Images: https://beanotherlab.org/wp-content/uploads/2019/04/bal_tmtba01.jpg
- Project page: https://beanotherlab.org/home/work/tmtba/

#### The Sense of Embodiment in Virtual Reality — Konstantina Kilteni (2012)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset
- Idea: The shared vocabulary for asking whether a bat, tail or tentacle has become part of you.
- What it is: A paper defining the sense of embodiment towards a virtual body as the combination of self-location, agency and body ownership.
- How it works: Conceptual review of body ownership, agency and self-location research.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1162/pres_a_00124 (Presence: Teleoperators and Virtual Environments 2012)

#### Rubber Hands 'Feel' Touch that Eyes See — Matthew Botvinick (1998)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Touch & haptics, Body schema & movement · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Idea: What counts as one's body is decided by matching senses, which is why a virtual animal body can be owned.
- What it is: The rubber hand illusion: when a visible rubber hand and a hidden real hand are stroked together, people feel the touch in the rubber hand.
- How it works: Synchronous brushing of a rubber hand in view and the participant's hidden hand; proprioceptive drift measured.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1038/35784 (Nature 1998)

#### Upside-Down Goggles — Carsten Höller (1994)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Body schema & movement · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Shown at: Decision, Hayward Gallery 2015
- Idea: Perception is learned and can be relearned: a simple optical swap shows how quickly a body adapts to a new way of seeing, the premise behind any sensory becoming.
- What it is: Visitors put on prism goggles that flip the visual world upside down and try to walk, reach and climb stairs until the body starts to adapt.
- How it works: Prism goggles inverting the image, after George Stratton's 1890s experiments; worn for minutes or, in trials, for days.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ct3c9PzS6yE

#### Inter Discommunication Machine — Kazuhiko Hachiya (1993)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Hearing & vibration, Body schema & movement · Medium: Wearable & sensory device, Performance & participatory
- Idea: An early swap of perspectives: the self is steered from outside, the ancestor of body-swap and perceptual-crossing works.
- What it is: Two people wear winged headsets with cameras and microphones; each sees and hears only what the other's headset captures, so they must move through space in each other's perception.
- How it works: Two head-mounted displays linked to each other's head-mounted camera and microphone via wireless video transmission, housed in feathered wing costumes.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=JOzVzcmK0VU
- Project page: https://www.petworks.co.jp/~hachiya/

#### Handsight — Agnes Hegedüs (1992)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Touch & haptics · Medium: Multisensory installation
- Idea: The eye moves into the hand: vision becomes something you reach with, like the eyes on an octopus's arms or a snail's stalks.
- What it is: A visitor holds an eyeball-shaped tracker and moves it inside a transparent glass sphere; a projection shows a virtual world 'inside' the sphere from the point of view of the hand-held eye.
- How it works: Polhemus-tracked eyeball interface registered to a physical glass sphere, driving real-time graphics on a projection screen.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kTfgGeMfRZI

#### Videoplace — Myron Krueger (1975)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Body schema & movement, Altered vision · Medium: Multisensory installation, Screen & web
- Shown at: Milwaukee Art Museum 1975; Prix Ars Electronica Golden Nica 1990
- Idea: The body becomes a graphic shape that other beings can climb, touch and respond to, the groundwork for embodied, full-body interaction.
- What it is: Participants see their live silhouettes on a large screen, where they play with graphic creatures such as the small CRITTER that climbs on their outline, and with other people's silhouettes from a remote room.
- How it works: Real-time video silhouette extraction on custom hardware, composited with computer graphics and projected; no goggles or gloves.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=dqZyZrN3Pl0

#### Máscaras Sensoriais (Sensorial Masks) — Lygia Clark (1967)
- Type: Artwork · Senses: Altered vision, Smell & taste, Hearing & vibration · Medium: Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: A precursor: art as a wearable that changes the senses themselves, not an image to look at.
- What it is: Cloth hoods fitted with mirrors, lenses, scented pouches and sound-making objects that participants wear over the head, altering sight, smell and hearing and turning attention inward.
- How it works: Hand-sewn fabric hoods with mirrors, eyepieces, aromatic herbs and small sound objects.
- Video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0kZ-WIORsZg
- Images: https://i.ytimg.com/vi/0kZ-WIORsZg/hqdefault.jpg
- Project page: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0kZ-WIORsZg

### Surveys & reviews

Surveys and systematic reviews of nonhuman or more-than-human XR.

#### How to be Non-Human: Animal Embodiment in VR Games — Siqi Yu (2026)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: Game, VR headset
- Idea: About 77% of animal-avatar games keep human interaction logic; the animal is mostly a skin.
- What it is: A reflexive thematic analysis of 48 first-person VR games with animal avatars, sorted into biomimicry, limited simulation, hybrid human-animal and human-behaviour themes.
- How it works: Researchers played all games on Meta Quest 3 with verbal logging, then coded movement, control and feedback patterns.
- Paper: https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08130 (DiGRA 2026)
- Images: https://arxiv.org/html/2606.08130v1/img/theme_fig/Fig_VRPigeons.jpg https://arxiv.org/html/2606.08130v1/img/theme_fig/Fig_Tentacular.jpg

#### How Avatars Influence User Behavior: A Review of the Proteus Effect — Anna Samira Praetorius (2020)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Body schema & movement · Medium: VR headset, Game
- Idea: Whatever body you inhabit, human or animal, tends to reshape how you act.
- What it is: A review of studies on the Proteus effect, classifying them by self-similarity, wishful identification and embodied presence.
- How it works: Literature review of avatar studies since Yee and Bailenson (2007).
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3402942.3403019 (Foundations of Digital Games (FDG) 2020)

### Methods & toolkits

Design methods, frameworks and toolkits for nonhuman perspective design.

#### Experiencing the More-than-Human Through Human Augmentation — Botao 'Amber' Hu, Danlin Huang (2025)
- Type: Paper · Senses: Multisensory · Medium: Mixed reality, Wearable & sensory device
- Idea: Turn augmentation sideways: use the same devices not to make humans more capable but to modulate the human sensorium toward other Umwelten.
- What it is: A design paper that repurposes human-augmentation technology to give temporary, first-person approximations of nonhuman senses, with seven design principles and five cases: EchoVision, FeltSight, FungiSync, TentacUs and City of Sparkles.
- How it works: Research through design grounded in eco-phenomenology and eco-somatics, synthesising principles across the authors' mixed-reality, haptic and EMS works.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.21606/drs.2026.814 (DRS 2026)
- Images: https://arxiv.org/html/2511.12533v3/images/MtHtHA.png
- Project page: https://amber.botao.hu/project/hth

#### The Beauty of Blood Flow — Marshmallow Laser Feast (2024)
- Type: Immersive film · Senses: Breath & interoception, Body schema & movement · Medium: Screen & web
- Idea: A method film: scientific imaging is the raw material for feeling the body's interior as a landscape.
- What it is: A short documentary on how MLF and Fraunhofer MEVIS turned medical scans of lungs, heart and blood flow into the imagery of Evolver.
- How it works: Interviews and process footage of 4D-flow MRI and CT data processing by the Fraunhofer MEVIS team (Matthias Günther, Bianka Hofmann and others).
- Video: https://vimeo.com/911276237
- Images: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/app/uploads/2024/02/The-Beauty-of-Blood-Flow_05.jpg
- Project page: https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com/project/the-beauty-of-blood-flow-documentary-short/

#### HoloKit — Botao 'Amber' Hu, Reality Design Lab (2017)
- Type: Product · Senses: Altered vision · Medium: AR, Mixed reality
- Shown at: Augmented World Expo 2017; Future of Storytelling 2017; SIGGRAPH 2017; Maker Faire New York 2017; Red Dot Design Award 2023; iF Design Award 2024; UbiComp/ISWC 2024 Best Demo Award
- Idea: Cheap, open, see-through MR keeps the participant in the real place and in view of others — the condition for public becoming-other experiences.
- What it is: An open-source headset that turns a smartphone into an optical see-through mixed-reality display; the shared hardware behind EchoVision, FungiSync and GravField.
- How it works: Cardboard (HoloKit 1, 2017) and then injection-moulded (HoloKit X, 2022) optics for iPhone, with a Unity SDK on ARKit and Multipeer Connectivity for co-located multiplayer.
- Paper: https://doi.org/10.1145/3675094.3677549 (UbiComp/ISWC 2024 Adjunct)
- Video: https://vimeo.com/801220687
- Images: https://reality.design/media/_resources/HoloKit%20X/project-holokit-x-cover-01.jpg https://reality.design/media/_resources/HoloKit%20X/project-holokit-x-gallery-01.jpg
- Project page: https://holokit.io
- Code: https://github.com/realitydeslab/holokit-app

## Creators

- **Marshmallow Laser Feast** (26) — Experiential artist collective. London collective founded in 2011 by Memo Akten, Robin McNicholas and Barnaby Steel, now led by McNicholas, Steel and Ersin Han Ersin. It makes research-led multisensory installations, VR and video works about breath, trees, animals and the systems that connect them, often built from LiDAR scans, medical imaging and scientific datasets. https://marshmallowlaserfeast.com
- **Botao 'Amber' Hu** (11) — Designer-researcher; Director of Reality Design Lab; DPhil candidate, University of Oxford. Works across somaesthetic design, mixed reality and more-than-human attunement, and invented the open-source HoloKit headset. Lead author of the monograph Experiencing More-than-Humans and of the portfolio works EchoVision, FungiSync, TentacUs, GravField and City of Sparkles. https://amber.botao.hu
- **Jakob Kudsk Steensen** (9) — Artist working with real-time simulation and ecology. Danish artist who builds game-engine simulations of forests, swamps and extinct habitats from fieldwork with biologists and sound artists. https://www.jakobkudsksteensen.com
- **Jiabao Li** (9) — Artist, designer and technologist; Associate Professor, Northeastern University. Works on more-than-human ecologies, feminist biotech and multispecies intelligence through installations, XR, AI, bio-art and performance; previously Assistant Professor at UT Austin and a designer at Apple. https://www.jiabaoli.org
- **Stelarc** (8) — Performance artist. Cyprus-born Australian artist who since the 1970s has extended his body with robotic hands, exoskeletons, internet-driven muscle stimulation and a cell-grown ear on his arm. https://stelarc.org
- **Špela Petrič** (8) — Bioartist working on plant-human relations. Slovenian artist with a PhD in biomedicine whose performances and lab works stage kinship between human bodies and plants. https://www.spelapetric.org
- **Eduardo Kac** (7) — Bioart and telepresence artist. Brazilian-American artist who coined the term transgenic art; known for GFP Bunny and the plantimal Edunia. https://www.ekac.org
- **Reality Design Lab** (7) — Independent design research lab for mixed reality, directed by Botao 'Amber' Hu. Makes mixed-reality artworks, open-source toolkits (HoloKit, HoloField, HoloMask, MultipeerConnectivity for Unity) and teaching programmes under the motto 'design new realities'. https://reality.design
- **Tomás Saraceno** (7) — Artist and architect. Argentine artist working with spiders, air, solar balloons and cloud-like structures; founder of Aerocene. https://studiotomassaraceno.org
- **Danlin Huang** (6) — Artist, designer-researcher and AR developer. Trained in industrial design and media art at the China Academy of Art; research assistant at Reality Design Lab. Uses XR, biosensors and AI to enrich bodily experience; lead designer of FeltSight and co-author of the monograph Experiencing More-than-Humans. https://danlinhuang.com
- **Saša Spačal** (6) — Artist working with fungi, soil and biofeedback. Slovenian artist whose studio Agapea builds living installations linking human bodies with mycelium, microbes and soil. https://www.agapea.si
- **Kouta Minamizawa** (5) — Professor, Keio University Graduate School of Media Design (KMD). Haptics researcher who leads the Embodied Media Project at Keio KMD, working on telexistence, haptic transmission and shared bodies. https://embodiedmedia.org
- **Might and Delight** (5) — Independent game studio, Stockholm. Swedish studio behind the Shelter series, in which players live as animal parents: a badger, a lynx, an elephant. https://www.mightanddelight.com/

- **Stanford Virtual Human Interaction Lab** (5) — Research lab. Lab directed by Jeremy Bailenson studying the psychology of virtual reality and embodiment. https://vhil.stanford.edu
- **Tosca Terán** (5) — Bio-sonification and bioart artist (Nanotopia). Interdisciplinary artist who, as Nanotopia, turns the electrical activity of living mycelium into music and immersive worlds. https://www.toscateran.com

- **Anastassia Andreasen** (4) — VR and sound researcher, Aalborg University Copenhagen. Anastassia Andreasen worked in Stefania Serafin's Multisensory Experience Lab on bat embodiment and echolocation in VR.
